# Onboarding: a step-by-step setup after creating a business

## Original request

After creating a business, a step-by-step setup (an interview) that walks the new business through its settings so it starts configured without hunting through Configuración: visually attractive and simple. Called **Onboarding**. Requested by Zeta (2026-10-05).

To be defined in a grill once the settings exist: the inventory of settings (Configuración, #60; pricing switches, #85; others), which ones the onboarding asks, in what order, which defaults a new business gets, skip/resume, and the design.

Waits for #60 and #85.

## PjM

New user-visible step-by-step Onboarding flow after creating a business; new behaviour that needs definition, so it is a feature.

## Interview: Product Analyst→PM

### Round 1: questions

### Round 1: the job to be done

The first-run checklist already covers part of this (navigation.md). Inicio shows four steps: price a variety, record a stock entry, publish the catalog, add a second user. It goes away for good once the two required steps are met. Settings live in Configuración. A new business opens with default expense categories and a default catalog header and footer. Nothing today walks the seller through setup. This round fixes only the job. Later rounds cover behaviour.

❓ **Q1** - **Situation**: In which situation does the seller reach for the Onboarding? The new-business invitation has just been accepted and they land on Inicio with an empty business. Is it only that moment, or also a later one, such as someone who skipped it and wants to come back?
➡️ Recommendation: the one moment right after creating the business, with a way to resume it if they leave halfway. Someone joining an existing business through a join invitation never sees it, because that business is already configured.

❓ **Q2** - **Progress wanted**: What progress does the seller want from it?
➡️ Recommendation: "start the first sale already configured, without hunting through Configuración". Concretely: the business name is right, the varieties and today's prices exist, opening stock is loaded, and the catalog and Telegram bot are connected or consciously left for later. The seller should not have to learn where each setting lives.

❓ **Q3** - **Judged as good**: How will the seller judge that it went well?
➡️ Recommendation, as a job story and success criteria:
- Job story: "When I have just opened my business in the app and it is empty, I want to be walked through the few settings I need, one at a time, with sensible defaults, so I can record my first sale the same day without hunting through Configuración."
- Criteria:
  1. From the phone, the seller reaches a business ready for the first sale in under 5 minutes.
  2. Nothing is mandatory beyond what a sale needs. Any other step can be skipped and found later in Configuración.
  3. Leaving halfway loses nothing already saved, and resuming picks up where they left off.

❓ **Q4** - **Today without it**: What does the seller do today without it?
➡️ Recommendation: they land on an empty Inicio, follow the first-run checklist, and open Variedades, Configuración and the catalog screen one by one. Before the app, they edited the spreadsheet's Ajustes sheet (prices, categories, catalog). The Onboarding replaces that hunting. It does not replace the checklist's job of telling them what is still missing.

❓ **Q5** - **Who it is for**: Is the Onboarding only for a friend who opens a brand-new business, or also for a business loaded through the spreadsheet import? Imported businesses already arrive with prices and stock and never see the checklist.
➡️ Recommendation: only brand-new businesses. An imported business is already configured, so it never sees the Onboarding, as with the checklist.

### Round 1: answers

### Round 1: the job to be done

**Q1**: Accepted. The Onboarding is for one moment: the first time a person lands in a business they have just opened with a new-business invitation. If they leave halfway (close the app, lose signal, or jump to record a sale), it resumes where they left off the next time they open the app, until they finish it or skip the rest. Someone joining through a join invitation never sees it, because they are joining a business their partner has already set up.
Assumption: there is no "run it again" entry in Configuración or Más. Once it is finished or skipped, every setting stays in its one place (Configuración, or the variety's page), and nothing in the navigation points back to the Onboarding. A way back would be a second door to the same settings and adds nothing.

**Q2**: Accepted, with one change. The progress wanted is: **"be ready to record my first sale, and have my business set up the way I want, without hunting for where each thing lives."**
- **Ready to sell** means the two things a sale and stock need: at least one variety with today's price, and its opening stock.
- **Set up** means the business-wide settings a new seller would otherwise go looking for, each one either set or deliberately left for later:
  - the business name;
  - the catalog header and footer;
  - the Telegram bot;
  - a partner;
  - the expense categories.
- The seller does not have to learn where each setting lives. They do need to come away knowing where to find a step they skipped (Configuración, or Variedades). This is about trust, not about teaching them the layout.
- Which settings are actually asked, in what order, and how #85's pricing switches fit in are for the next rounds.

**Q3**: Accepted, reworded and with one criterion added. This is the job against which everything after is judged.

*Job story*: **When** I have just opened my business in the app and it is still empty, **I want** to be walked through what my business needs, one short step at a time, with sensible defaults already filled in, **so I can** record my first sale the same day and have my catalog, bot and partner set up or knowingly left for later, without hunting through Configuración, Variedades and the catalog screen.

*Success criteria*:
1. **Fast, from the phone**: a seller goes from accepting the invitation to a business ready for its first sale (a variety priced today, with opening stock) in under 5 minutes, from the phone alone.
2. **Only the sale is required**: nothing is mandatory beyond what a sale needs. Every other step can be skipped in one tap, and says where it can be done later.
3. **Nothing lost**: leaving halfway loses nothing that was already saved, and resuming picks up at the first step not done.
4. **One fact, one place** (this criterion is added): every step saves the same setting, under the same rules and refusals, that Configuración or the variety's page saves. The Onboarding holds no copy of its own and no setting of its own. A value set there is the value seen afterwards in its usual place, and the reverse.
5. **Simple and attractive, and invents nothing**: the screens match the design, judged by eye on phone and computer. No placeholder data, no fake figures, and no step for a setting that does not exist yet.

**Q4**: Accepted. Today the seller lands on an empty Inicio, reads the first-run checklist, and opens Variedades, the catalog screen and Configuración one by one to find each thing. Before the app, they did all of this in the spreadsheet's Ajustes sheet. The Onboarding replaces that hunting.
- The checklist and the standing notices ("Arrancá por las variedades", the out-of-date catalog notice) keep telling the seller what is still missing.
- How the Onboarding and the checklist relate on Inicio (whether they both show, or one replaces the other) is for a later round. Whatever is decided, the seller never sees two lists asking for the same thing at once.

**Q5**: Accepted. Only brand-new businesses get the Onboarding.
- An imported business arrives with prices, stock, catalog and categories, so it never sees the Onboarding. It keeps its own after-import notice.
- A business that joined someone else's books never sees it either.

Assumption: businesses that existed before the Onboarding ships never see it. They are already in use or being set up through the checklist, and offering it to them now would interrupt work already under way.

### Round 2: questions

### Round 2: what the Onboarding asks and how it sits in the app

The job is settled. These questions are about behaviour that serves it. Two facts from the product knowledge shape them:
- The business name is already asked when the new-business invitation is accepted (accounts.md).
- A new business already starts with default expense categories ("Insumos de cultivo", "Packaging", "Envíos y traslados"…, expenses.md) and a default catalog header and footer (catalog.md).

❓ **Q1** - **The Onboarding and the first-run checklist**: navigation.md says "the checklist and the after-import notice are the only first-run elements: no tour, no welcome screen". The Onboarding changes that. Both ask for a priced variety and a stock entry, and the job says the seller never sees two lists asking for the same thing at once. Do they coexist, or does one replace the other?
➡️ Recommendation: for a new business, the Onboarding replaces the checklist while it runs.
- If the seller leaves before the two required steps (a priced variety and opening stock) are met, Inicio shows the existing checklist, with its rules unchanged: it goes for good once both are met.
- Once those two are met, no checklist ever shows for that business.
- Businesses that existed before this ships keep the checklist as it is.
- An imported business sees neither.

❓ **Q2** - **Which steps, in what order, and which are required**: the name is already taken at the invitation, and categories and header/footer already have defaults. The inventory I propose is below. Each step saves exactly what Configuración or the variety's page saves (criterion 4).
1. First variety with today's price (required). 
2. Its opening stock (required).
3. Catalog header and footer, shown with their defaults to keep or edit.
4. Telegram bot: token, channel and Probarlo. It ends at the saved bot; publishing stays on the catalog screen.
5. A partner: one join link to copy or share.
6. Expense categories, shown with the defaults to keep or edit.

➡️ Recommendation: this order, with sale-readiness first and the rest in order of how often a seller wants it. Only 1 and 2 are required, and all of 3–6 can be skipped in one tap. Skipping 3 or 6 leaves the defaults in place. Pricing switches from #85 get no step until they exist and are defined (criterion 5). Is this inventory right, and is anything missing, extra, or in a different place?

❓ **Q3** - **More than one variety**: a seller usually opens with several varieties, each with its own price and stock. After the first one, what does the Onboarding do?
➡️ Recommendation: after the first variety and its stock, offer "Agregar otra variedad" (price and stock again) or "Seguir". There is no cap, and every variety saved counts immediately. The seller can stop at one, since a sale needs only one, and the rest are added later from Variedades.

❓ **Q4** - **Skipping, finishing and resuming**: the checklist is derived from data, never from a stored tick (navigation.md). A skipped optional step leaves no data, so resume cannot tell "skipped" from "not yet reached" unless the skip is remembered. What are the rules?
➡️ Recommendation:
- A skipped step is remembered, and resume lands on the first step that is neither done nor skipped.
- The seller can also tap "Terminar por ahora" at any step.
- The Onboarding is over for good when the seller finishes the last step, skips the rest, or both required steps are met and every other step is done or skipped.
- It never returns, and nothing in the app points back to it (round 1).
- Leaving before the two required steps ends it too, and the checklist from Q1 takes over.
- A step whose data already exists (say a bot saved earlier) shows as "Hecho" and is passed over on resume.

❓ **Q5** - **Where it lives and how it opens**: the chrome rule gives every screen one of three kinds: tab root, card or detail, form or sheet.
➡️ Recommendation:
- Accepting the new-business invitation takes the seller straight into the Onboarding instead of an empty Inicio.
- It is a full screen of the form-or-sheet kind: one step at a time, with no bar or sidebar and no "+ Nueva venta".
- It shows progress ("Paso 2 de 6") and has "Atrás", "Saltar" and "Terminar por ahora" ("Cerrar" is the form kind's close).
- "Terminar por ahora" lands on Inicio.
- The seller who left it can come back to it from a notice on Inicio, such as "Seguí con la configuración", until it is over.
- It is the same on phone and computer, as one centred column on the computer.

### Round 2: answers

### Round 2: what the Onboarding asks and how it sits in the app

**Q1**: Partly accepted, partly overruled. For a new business, the Onboarding takes the checklist's place entirely; the two never both apply to the same business.
- **Why overruled**: round 1 settled that leaving halfway never ends the Onboarding; it resumes. So there is never a moment where a new business hands over to the checklist. Handing over would put two first-run lists on the same business, against the job.
- **While the Onboarding is not over**, Inicio shows one notice in the checklist's place: "Seguí con la configuración". It opens the Onboarding at its resume step (Q4, Q5).
- **The checklist's side rules still apply**, tied to the same data:
  - while no variety has a price today, "+ Nueva venta" says in words what is missing;
  - while the two required steps are unmet and nothing qualifies for the catalog, "Mandar catálogo" is disabled with its reason.
- **Once the Onboarding is over**, neither it nor the checklist ever shows again. Ongoing gaps are told by the standing notices, as today: "Arrancá por las variedades" and the out-of-date catalog notice.
- **Businesses that existed before this ships** keep the checklist exactly as it is.
- **An imported business** sees neither the Onboarding nor the checklist.
- navigation.md's "no tour, no welcome screen" line is amended to name the Onboarding as the first-run element for new businesses. The request asks for it, so this is no contradiction to resolve.

**Q2**: Accepted: this inventory, in this order, with only steps 1 and 2 required.
1. **A variety with today's price** (required): its name, plus a price entry effective today (list price, and optionally a bulk price and its minimum). Same rules and refusals as Variedades.
2. **Its opening stock** (required): a stock entry with origin Stock inicial, grams, today's date, and an optional total cost. The step says plainly that with no cost the lot costs $0, which is the definition of opening stock.
3. **Catalog header and footer**: the defaults shown, ready to keep or edit.
4. **Telegram bot**: token, channel chat, own chat (with Buscar mi chat), and Probarlo. It ends at the saved bot. Publishing stays on the catalog screen, and the step says so in one line ("Cuando quieras, publicalo desde Variedades → Mandar catálogo").
5. **A partner**: one join link, to copy or share.
6. **Expense categories**: the defaults shown, ready to keep or edit.

Steps 3–6 can each be skipped in one tap. Skipping 3 or 6 keeps the defaults. Each skipped step says where it can be done later (Configuración and its group).

Things left out, each on purpose:
- **No business-name step**: the name is already taken when the invitation is accepted.
- **No per-variety catalog attributes** (emoji, % índica, aroma) **and no low-stock threshold**: they live on the variety's page, and adding them makes the 5-minute criterion harder for little gain.
- **No Apariencia step**: it is a device choice, not a business setting.
- **No step for #85's pricing switches** until they exist and are defined (criterion 5). When they do, adding a step is that issue's decision.

**Q3**: Accepted. After a variety and its opening stock, the seller sees "Agregar otra variedad" (another name, price and opening stock) or "Seguir".
- There is no cap.
- Each variety is saved the moment its step is saved.
- One variety is enough to continue.
- The progress count stays on step 1–2 while varieties are being added.

**Q4**: Overruled in part: leaving never ends the Onboarding (round 1). The rules are these.
- **Required steps**: done is worked out from the data. Step 1 is done when a variety has a price today; step 2 when a stock entry exists.
- **Optional steps**: each is "passed" once the seller saves it, keeps the defaults, or skips it. The pass is remembered for the business; this stored state belongs to the Onboarding alone, and the checklist's derive-only rule is unchanged. A step whose data already exists (a bot saved, a second user in the business) shows "Hecho" and counts as passed.
- **Resume**: it lands on the first step that is neither done nor passed.
- **Leaving** ("Cerrar", closing the app, a dropped connection) keeps everything saved and the resume point.
- **"Saltar el resto"**: shown on the optional steps only, once both required steps are done. It passes every remaining step at once.
- **It is over for good** when both required steps are done and every optional step is passed. Then it never returns, and nothing in the app points to it.
- **There is no early end before the required steps are met**: those are what the job requires. Until they are met, the resume notice stays on Inicio.

Assumption: the Onboarding's state belongs to the business, not to the user. A partner who joins midway sees the same Inicio notice and can carry on. This keeps one state per business and adds no roles.

**Q5**: Accepted, with "Terminar por ahora" removed.
- **Entry**: accepting a new-business invitation lands the seller in the Onboarding, not on an empty Inicio. That is the only time it opens by itself. Afterwards it is reached only from the "Seguí con la configuración" notice on Inicio, until it is over.
- **Kind**: a full screen of the form-or-sheet kind. No bar or sidebar, no "+ Nueva venta".
- **Each step shows**:
  - the progress ("Paso 2 de 6");
  - "Atrás";
  - "Saltar" on optional steps;
  - "Saltar el resto" as described in Q4;
  - "Cerrar", which lands on Inicio and keeps the resume point. "Cerrar" does the job "Terminar por ahora" was meant to do, so a second exit with the same effect would only add confusion.
- **Unsaved changes**: leaving a step with something typed and not saved asks before discarding, as every form does.
- **Computer**: the same single centred column, with no layout of its own.
- **Inside the steps**: Probarlo's reasons, the refusals and toasts are the owners' own, and so are "Sin conexión" and "Eso ya no está.".

### Round 3: questions

### Round 3: the ending, what counts as "passed", the look, and Inicio

Round 2 is applied as you ruled it. The Onboarding replaces the checklist for new businesses and never ends before the two required steps are met. The state belongs to the business. It is left through "Cerrar", and the 6-step inventory stands. These questions cover what is still open.

❓ **Q1** - **The ending**: round 1 says the seller should come away knowing where to find a step they skipped, and the job is to record the first sale the same day. When the last step is passed, or "Saltar el resto" is tapped, what does the seller see and where do they land?
➡️ Recommendation: one closing screen, "Listo", with a line per step.
- Each line reads "Hecho" or "Para después — en Configuración → <grupo>" (or "en Variedades").
- The primary action is "Cargar mi primera venta", which opens Nueva venta.
- The secondary action is "Ir a Inicio".
- Closing the screen by any means counts as finished, and it never shows again. No celebration or extra content.

❓ **Q2** - **What counts as "passed" for each optional step**: Q4 said a step is passed when saved, kept as default, or skipped, and resume lands on the first step not passed. Applying it step by step:
- **Header and footer**: "Guardar" (edited) or "Dejar así" (defaults kept) passes it.
- **Expense categories**: "Seguir" with the defaults, or any add, rename or removal, passes it.
- **Bot**: only a saved bot (Probarlo, the only way it is saved) is "Hecho"; "Saltar" passes it without a bot.
- **Partner**: creating a join link and copying or sharing it passes it; a second user already in the business shows "Hecho".

➡️ Recommendation: exactly these rules. Creating a link is not the same as the partner joining, so the step says "Enviale el link a tu socio" and does not wait for them. Leaving a step with a typed token that was never saved through Probarlo counts as unsaved and asks before discarding, like every form.

❓ **Q3** - **"Visually attractive and simple"**: the request asks for this, but the design system (`docs/product/design-system/`, with every screen in `scripts/design/screens.json`) is the product's one look, and criterion 5 says "the screens match the design, judged by eye". What does "attractive" allow?
➡️ Recommendation: built only from the design system's existing elements, with nothing new in it.
- Per step: one short title, one sentence on why it matters, the field or fields, one primary action and "Saltar".
- A progress indicator, shown in words as well as shape, never by color alone.
- No illustrations, animation or marketing copy, and no new colors or components.
- Texts stay in Rioplatense Spanish, and the 320–430 px and 130 % text rules apply.
- Each step and the closing screen get an entry in `screens.json` and are judged by eye on phone and computer.

❓ **Q4** - **Measuring success**: criterion 1 says under 5 minutes from the phone. Do we measure it, and how?
➡️ Recommendation: no usage tracking and nothing stored about how long a seller took or where they left. ADR-0005 keeps request data out of logs, and the only thing the browser stores is the theme (ADR-0026).
- Verified by one end-to-end front run from a phone viewport: accept an invitation, price a variety, load its stock, pass the optional steps, and land ready to sell.
- A second end-to-end run covers leaving halfway and resuming at the first step not done.

❓ **Q5** - **The notice on Inicio**: "Seguí con la configuración" takes the checklist's place while the Onboarding is not over. Where and with what?
➡️ Recommendation: at the top of Inicio, the checklist's place (navigation.md; results.md gives Inicio's order), on phone and `InicioCompu` alike.
- It shows how far along the seller is in words ("2 de 6 pasos") and says what is next ("Falta: stock inicial").
- It is not dismissible, since it is the only way back in.
- It disappears for good the moment the Onboarding is over.
- A partner who joined sees it too (Q4's state is the business's).

### Round 3: answers

### Round 3: the ending, what counts as "passed", the look, and Inicio

**Q1**: Accepted, with two additions.
- **The closing screen, "Listo"**, has one line per step:
  - "Hecho" for each step whose data exists or that was saved;
  - "Para después — en Configuración → <grupo>" (Integraciones, Usuarios, Mensaje del catálogo or Categorías de gastos), or "en Variedades", for each skipped step.
- **Two actions**: the primary "Cargar mi primera venta" opens Nueva venta; the secondary "Ir a Inicio".
- **The Onboarding is over** once this screen is reached. Closing it by any means never brings it back. No celebration or extra content.
- **Addition 1, Nueva venta's way back**: Nueva venta opened from "Listo" goes back to Inicio, whether it is closed with "Cerrar" or saved. It never goes back to "Listo" or to the Onboarding, because those are over.
- **Addition 2, the count**: the required steps' lines say what exists ("Hecho · 2 variedades"). This is a count of records the seller just made, not a figure or an amount.

**Q2**: Accepted, with two overrules so that "passed" always comes from one explicit tap, never from an edit made along the way.
- **Header and footer**: accepted. "Guardar" (edited) or "Dejar así" (defaults kept) passes it.
- **Expense categories**: overruled. Adding, renaming or removing a category saves at once, as it does in Configuración, but none of these passes the step. Only "Seguir" passes it, whether the defaults were kept or edited. A seller who adds one category usually adds another, and resume must not jump past a step the seller is still in.
- **Bot**: accepted.
  - Only a bot saved through a successful Probarlo is "Hecho". "Saltar" passes the step without a bot.
  - A failed Probarlo shows its own reason and saves nothing.
  - Leaving with typed values that were never saved, by "Cerrar", "Atrás" or "Saltar", asks before discarding.
- **Partner**: overruled on "copy or share". The app cannot tell a shared link from a cancelled share sheet: catalog.md treats a cancel as showing nothing. So the step is passed by "Seguir" once a link has been created, or by "Saltar". It reads "Enviale el link a tu socio" and never waits for the partner to join. A second user already in the business shows "Hecho".

**Q3**: Accepted, with one adjustment.
- **The look is the design system's**: tokens, components and both themes. There are no illustrations, no animation, no marketing copy and no new colours.
- **One adjustment**: if no existing element fits the progress indicator or the step layout, design draws it from tokens only, inside the design system, before it is built. It is not invented in code.
- **Each step**: one short title, one sentence on why it matters, its field or fields, one primary action and "Saltar" (optional steps).
- **Progress**: told in words as well as shape, never by colour alone.
- **Text and sizes**: Rioplatense Spanish with vos. Nothing clipped at 320–430 px or at 130 % text, controls at least 44 px tall, inputs at least 16 px.
- **Registry and checks**: every step and "Listo" go into `scripts/design/screens.json`, and `compare.sh` runs before and after, phone and computer, judged by eye.

**Q4**: Accepted. There is no usage tracking: nothing is stored or logged about time taken or where a seller left (ADR-0005, ADR-0026).
- **Criterion 1** is checked by an end-to-end front run from a phone viewport, which accepts an invitation, prices a variety, loads its opening stock, passes the optional steps and opens Nueva venta from "Listo", in under 5 minutes.
- **A second run** leaves halfway, resumes from Inicio's notice, and lands on the first step that is neither done nor passed.
- **Also, as fast tests**:
  - a resume matrix: done worked out from the data for the required steps, passed remembered for the optional ones, and "Hecho" for data that already exists;
  - criterion 4: a value saved in a step reads the same in Configuración or on the variety's page;
  - the Onboarding's state belongs to its business: its routes join the isolation sweep and the gated-route test, and its table carries `business_id`, so business removal covers it (ADR-0004, ADR-0015).

**Q5**: Accepted.
- **Where**: at the top of Inicio, in position 1 of results.md's order, on the phone and on `InicioCompu`.
- **It reads** "Seguí con la configuración", with "2 de 6 pasos" and what comes next (e.g. "Falta: stock inicial").
- **It cannot be dismissed**: it goes for good when the Onboarding is over. The seller who wants it gone sooner finishes the two required steps and taps "Saltar el resto".
- **Partners**: a partner who joined sees it too.
- **No overlap with "Arrancá por las variedades"**: it never shows together with that notice, which takes over only once the Onboarding is over, as with the checklist.

### Closing

### Closing summary

This is the last round, so I close with what was decided. Nothing left could change the PRD.

**Job (round 1)**
- When I have just opened my business and it is still empty, I want to be walked through what it needs, one short step at a time, with defaults filled in, so I can record my first sale the same day without hunting through Configuración, Variedades and the catalog screen.
- Success criteria:
  1. Under 5 minutes from the phone to a business ready to sell.
  2. Only the sale is required.
  3. Nothing is lost on leaving.
  4. One fact, one place: no setting of its own.
  5. Simple and attractive, inventing nothing.

**Who and when**
- Only a brand-new business, right after its new-business invitation is accepted.
- Never an imported business, a business joined by invitation, or one that existed before this ships.
- There is no "run it again".

**Relation to the checklist**
- The Onboarding replaces the first-run checklist for a new business, so the two never both apply.
- Existing businesses keep the checklist.
- navigation.md's "no tour, no welcome screen" line is amended to name the Onboarding.
- The side rules stay: "+ Nueva venta" says what is missing, and "Mandar catálogo" is disabled with its reason.

**Steps, in order**
1. A variety with today's price (required).
2. Its opening stock (required). The seller can add more varieties.
3. Catalog header and footer.
4. Telegram bot, ending at the saved bot.
5. A partner's join link.
6. Expense categories.

- Left out on purpose: the business name, per-variety attributes and thresholds, Apariencia, and #85's pricing switches until they exist.
- Steps 3–6 are skippable in one tap, and a skipped step says where it lives.

**Passing, resuming, finishing**
- Required steps are worked out from the data.
- Optional steps are passed by one explicit tap, and the pass is remembered for the business.
  - Header and footer: "Guardar" or "Dejar así".
  - Categories: "Seguir".
  - Bot: only a successful Probarlo counts as "Hecho"; "Saltar" passes it without one.
  - Partner: "Seguir" after a link is created.
  - Data that already exists shows "Hecho".
- Resume lands on the first step that is neither done nor passed.
- "Saltar el resto" is available once both required steps are done.
- It never ends before the required steps are met, and it is over for good after "Listo".
- The state belongs to the business, so a partner who joined sees it too.

**Screens and entry**
- Accepting the invitation lands in the Onboarding.
- Afterwards it is reached only from the Inicio notice "Seguí con la configuración" ("2 de 6 pasos", what is next). The notice is at the top of Inicio, cannot be dismissed, and goes for good when the Onboarding is over.
- It is a form-kind full screen: no bar, no "+ Nueva venta".
- Each step has progress in words, "Atrás", "Saltar" and "Cerrar".
- "Listo" has one line per step ("Hecho" or "Para después — en <where>"), with "Cargar mi primera venta" and "Ir a Inicio". Nueva venta opened from "Listo" goes back to Inicio.

**Design and checks**
- Design-system elements only, with a progress indicator drawn from tokens if none fits.
- Rioplatense Spanish, 320–430 px, 130 % text, controls of 44 px or more.
- Every screen goes into `screens.json` and is judged by eye.
- No usage tracking.
- Verified by an end-to-end phone run (under 5 minutes), a resume run, a resume matrix, the same-value check for criterion 4, and the isolation sweep for the Onboarding's state.

**Glossary**
- I added **Onboarding** to CONTEXT.md and narrowed the "Avoid" line on First-run checklist.

**Not decided, by design**
- The pricing-switch step, left to #85.
- The Onboarding's state per business is stated as behaviour only, with no technical design.

## Product Analyst

### Job to be done

**Job story.** When I have just opened my business and it is still empty, I want to be walked through what it needs, one short step at a time, with defaults already filled in, so I can record my first sale the same day without hunting through Configuración, Variedades and the catalog screen.

**Success criteria (the seller's own).**
1. **Fast, from the phone**: from accepting the invitation to a business ready for its first sale (a variety priced today, with opening stock) in under 5 minutes, from the phone alone.
2. **Only the sale is required**: nothing is mandatory beyond a priced variety and its opening stock. Every other step can be skipped in one tap and says where it can be done later.
3. **Nothing lost**: leaving halfway loses nothing already saved. Resuming picks up at the first step that is neither done nor passed.
4. **One fact, one place**: every step saves the same setting, under the same rules and refusals, that Configuración or the variety's page saves. The Onboarding holds no copy of a setting and no setting of its own. A value set in a step is the value seen afterwards in its usual place, and the reverse.
5. **Simple and attractive, inventing nothing**: the screens use the design system and are judged by eye on phone and computer. No placeholder data, no fake figures, no step for a setting that does not exist yet.

**Asked for, and left out on purpose.**
- A business-name step: the name is already taken when the invitation is accepted.
- Per-variety catalog attributes (emoji, % índica, aroma) and the low-stock threshold: they live on the variety's page and would strain the 5-minute criterion.
- An Apariencia step: it is a device choice, not a business setting.
- A step for #85's pricing switches: none until they exist and are defined. Adding one is that issue's decision.
- A "run it again" entry in Configuración or Más: it would be a second door to the same settings.
- "Terminar por ahora": "Cerrar" already does what it was meant to do.
- Usage tracking (time taken, where a seller left).
- Illustrations, animation, marketing copy, new colours or new components.

### Competitors

No competitor data was gathered in this interview, and I have none I can cite. I do not describe how other products serve this job. The criteria come only from the job above and the PM's decisions: one short step at a time, defaults filled in, skip in one tap, resume where left, a closing screen that says where skipped things live, and the product's own look.

### Behaviour definition

#### Who gets it, and when

1. **A brand-new business.** The Onboarding is offered only to a business just opened by a new-business invitation, from the moment that invitation is accepted.
2. **Never offered to:**
   - a business loaded through the spreadsheet import (it keeps its own after-import notice);
   - a business a user joined by a join invitation, because it is already set up;
   - a business that existed before the Onboarding shipped, which keeps the first-run checklist exactly as it is.
3. **No "run it again".** Once it is over, nothing in the app points back to it.
4. **One state per business.** A partner who joins midway sees the same notice and can carry on. No roles are added.
5. **Assumption (PM):** pre-existing businesses never see the Onboarding, and there is no "run it again" entry.

#### Relation to the first-run checklist

6. For a brand-new business the Onboarding fully replaces the checklist. The two never both apply to the same business, and the seller never sees two lists asking for the same thing.
7. While the Onboarding is not over, the checklist does not show for that business. "Arrancá por las variedades" does not show either; it takes over only once the Onboarding is over, as it does after the checklist.
8. The checklist's side rules stay, tied to the same data:
   - while no variety has a price today, "+ Nueva venta" says in words what is missing;
   - while nothing qualifies for the catalog and the two required steps are unmet, "Mandar catálogo" is disabled with its reason in words.
9. **navigation.md changes.** The line "The checklist and the after-import notice are the only first-run elements: no tour, no welcome screen" is amended to name the Onboarding as the first-run element for a brand-new business, next to the checklist and the after-import notice. The checklist's own rules are unchanged for the businesses that keep it.

#### Entry and the Inicio notice

10. **Entry.** Accepting a new-business invitation lands the seller in the Onboarding, not on an empty Inicio. Accepting a join invitation still lands on Inicio. This is the only time it opens by itself.
11. **The notice.** While the Onboarding is not over, Inicio shows "Seguí con la configuración" at the top, in position 1 of results.md's order. It does the same on phone and on `InicioCompu`.
    - It shows progress in words ("2 de 6 pasos") and what is next (e.g. "Falta: stock inicial").
    - Tapping it opens the Onboarding at the resume step.
    - It cannot be dismissed. It is the only way back in, and it goes for good when the Onboarding is over.
    - A partner sees it too.
12. A seller who wants it gone sooner finishes the two required steps and taps "Saltar el resto".

#### The screens

13. **Kind.** The Onboarding is a full screen of the form-or-sheet kind (navigation.md): no bar or sidebar, no "+ Nueva venta". It is one centred, phone-width column on every viewport, with no computer layout of its own.
14. **Every step shows:**
    - one short title and one sentence on why it matters;
    - its field or fields and one primary action;
    - progress in words as well as shape, never by colour alone, e.g. "Paso 2 de 6";
    - "Atrás";
    - "Saltar", on optional steps only;
    - "Saltar el resto", on optional steps only and only once both required steps are done;
    - "Cerrar", which lands on Inicio and keeps everything saved and the resume point.
15. **Unsaved changes.** Leaving a step with something typed and not saved, by "Cerrar", "Atrás" or "Saltar", asks before discarding, as every form does. An untouched step leaves at once.
16. **Inside the steps**, the owners' own behaviour applies unchanged: field rules, refusals inline keeping what was typed, toasts, Probarlo's reasons, "Sin conexión", the generic retry and "Eso ya no está.".

#### The six steps, in this order

| # | Step | Required | Saves what |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | A variety with today's price | Yes | A variety and a price entry effective today (list price, optionally a bulk price and its minimum), with Variedades' rules and refusals |
| 2 | Its opening stock | Yes | A stock entry with origin Stock inicial, grams, today's date and an optional total cost |
| 3 | Catalog header and footer | No | The business's header and footer, shown with their defaults |
| 4 | Telegram bot | No | Token, channel chat, own chat (with Buscar mi chat) and Probarlo, exactly as Integraciones |
| 5 | A partner | No | One join link per tap, to copy or share |
| 6 | Expense categories | No | Add, rename and remove, as Categorías de gastos |

17. **Step 2** says plainly that with no cost the lot costs $0, which is the definition of opening stock.
18. **More varieties.** After a variety and its opening stock, the seller sees "Agregar otra variedad" (another name, price and opening stock) or "Seguir".
    - There is no cap.
    - Each variety is saved the moment its step is saved.
    - One variety is enough to continue.
    - The progress stays on steps 1–2 while varieties are being added.
19. **Step 4** ends at the saved bot. It says in one line: "Cuando quieras, publicalo desde Variedades → Mandar catálogo". Publicar and the pin's status stay on the catalog screen.
20. **Step 5** reads "Enviale el link a tu socio" and never waits for the partner to join.
21. **Skipped steps** say where they can be done later: Configuración → Integraciones, Usuarios, Mensaje del catálogo or Categorías de gastos. Skipping step 3 or 6 keeps the defaults.

#### What "done" and "passed" mean

22. **Required steps are worked out from the data.** Step 1 is done when a variety has a price today. Step 2 is done when a stock entry exists.
23. **Optional steps are passed by one explicit tap.** The pass is remembered for the business. This stored state belongs to the Onboarding alone, and the checklist's derive-only rule is unchanged.

| Step | Passed by |
|---|---|
| 3. Header and footer | "Guardar" (edited) or "Dejar así" (defaults kept) |
| 4. Bot | A successful Probarlo (the only way it is saved) shows "Hecho". "Saltar" passes the step without a bot. A failed Probarlo shows its own reason and saves nothing |
| 5. Partner | "Seguir" once a link has been created, or "Saltar" |
| 6. Categories | "Seguir", whether the defaults were kept or edited |

24. In step 6, adding, renaming or removing a category saves at once, as in Configuración, but never passes the step. Only "Seguir" does.
25. Data that already exists shows "Hecho" and counts as passed: a bot saved earlier, a second user already in the business.
26. **Resume** lands on the first step that is neither done nor passed.
27. **Leaving** by "Cerrar", closing the app or losing the connection keeps everything saved and the resume point.
28. "Saltar el resto" passes every remaining optional step at once.
29. **It never ends before both required steps are met.** Until then the Inicio notice stays.

#### The closing screen, "Listo"

30. When both required steps are done and every optional step is passed (the last one tapped, or "Saltar el resto"), the seller reaches "Listo".
31. It has one line per step:
    - "Hecho" for a step whose data exists or that was saved;
    - "Para después — en Configuración → <grupo>" or "en Variedades" for a skipped one;
    - the required steps' lines say what exists, e.g. "Hecho · 2 variedades". This is a count of records the seller just made, not a figure or an amount.
32. It has two actions: the primary "Cargar mi primera venta", which opens Nueva venta, and the secondary "Ir a Inicio". There is no celebration and no extra content.
33. **The Onboarding is over once "Listo" is reached.** Closing it by any means never brings it back. The Inicio notice goes for good.
34. **Nueva venta opened from "Listo"** goes back to Inicio, whether it is closed with "Cerrar" or saved. It never goes back to "Listo" or to the Onboarding.

#### Empty states and errors

35. **An empty business is the starting point.** Step 1 shows its empty fields. No step shows a figure, placeholder or sample data.
36. **Refusals and failures** belong to the owner of each setting (Variedades, stock, catalog, Integraciones, Usuarios, Categorías de gastos). They show inline, keep what was typed and carry no data. A dropped network shows "Sin conexión", and a server error shows the generic retry.
37. **A record gone** (a partner removed what the step shows) follows accounts.md's "Eso ya no está." rule.
38. **A partner acting at the same time.** Data a partner already created shows "Hecho" on the next open. No live updates are added.

#### Design

39. Only design-system elements: tokens, components and both themes. If no existing element fits the progress indicator or the step layout, design draws it from tokens only, inside the design system, before it is built. It is not invented in code.
40. Texts in Rioplatense Spanish with vos. Nothing clipped at 320–430 px or at 130 % text. Controls at least 44 px tall and inputs at least 16 px.
41. Every step and "Listo" go into `scripts/design/screens.json`. `compare.sh` runs before and after, phone and computer, judged by eye.

#### Privacy and isolation

42. The Onboarding's state carries `business_id` and is covered by business removal. Its routes join the isolation sweep and the gated-route test. Another business's state does not exist (404 or absent). Nothing about it goes to logs, the console or browser storage (ADR-0004, ADR-0005, ADR-0026). Nothing is stored or logged about time taken or where a seller left.

### Metrics and dashboard

There is no usage tracking and no telemetry for this feature (ADR-0005, ADR-0026). The job's criteria are shipped measured as follows.

- **Criterion 1, under 5 minutes.** One end-to-end front run from a phone viewport accepts an invitation, prices a variety, loads its opening stock, passes the optional steps and opens Nueva venta from "Listo", in under 5 minutes.
- **Criterion 3, nothing lost.**
  - A second end-to-end run leaves halfway, resumes from Inicio's notice and lands on the first step neither done nor passed.
  - A resume matrix, 100% passing, covers:
    - required steps worked out from the data;
    - optional steps remembered as passed;
    - "Hecho" for data that already exists;
    - no end before both required steps are met.
- **Criterion 4, one fact, one place.** A same-value test: a value saved in each step reads the same in Configuración or on the variety's page, and the reverse.
- **Criterion 2, only the sale is required.** A front test shows that every optional step can be skipped in one tap, that "Saltar el resto" shows only once both required steps are done, and that "Listo" names where each skipped step lives.
- **Who gets it.** Tests show that a brand-new business sees it; an imported business, a joined user's business and a pre-existing business never do; and that the checklist never shows alongside it.
- **Inicio and chrome.** Front tests, phone and computer: the notice sits at the top with its words and cannot be dismissed, "Arrancá por las variedades" and the checklist never show with it, and the Onboarding has no bar and no "+ Nueva venta". Nueva venta opened from "Listo" returns to Inicio.
- **Isolation.** The Onboarding's routes sit in the isolation sweep and the gated-route test, and its table carries `business_id`.
- **Design (criterion 5).** Every screen is in `screens.json`, `compare.sh` runs before and after, and a by-eye pass covers phone and computer in both themes.
- **Business metrics from data that already exists, with no new tracking.** The operator can read, from the records the product already keeps:
  - the share of brand-new businesses that have a priced variety and a stock entry;
  - the share whose first sale falls on the day they were opened.

  These are read from the existing records, never shown in the app, and there is no dashboard in the product.

### Flags

- `touches_ui`: yes. It adds a new full-screen flow, its closing screen and an Inicio notice, and it changes what Inicio shows for a new business.
- `touches_copy`: yes. It adds user-facing text: step titles and sentences, "Seguí con la configuración", "Listo", its lines and the button labels.

### For the next role

PM, please validate:

1. **The imported business.** The import runs after the new-business invitation is accepted, so a business that is later imported will already have landed in the Onboarding. I wrote "never offered to an imported business" as: once the import loads a business, the Onboarding is over for it and the notice never shows. Please confirm that reading.
2. **How "pre-existing" is told.** A business "that existed before it shipped" never sees the Onboarding. I wrote only the behaviour and no way of telling them apart, since that is a technical decision. Please confirm that the behaviour alone is enough for you.
3. **The notice's count.** "2 de 6 pasos" counts steps done or passed. Please confirm that while extra varieties are added the count stays on steps 1–2.
4. **The navigation.md amendment** in item 9, and the CONTEXT.md entry already added, are the only changes to existing definitions.
5. **Nothing was left open.** The pricing-switch step is deliberately left to #85.

## PM (v1)

### Verdict

`approved`: the PRD follows every decision from the three rounds and serves the settled job. The gaps left were small and are closed below.

### Adjustments

These are the answers to the Product Analyst's five questions, plus four small gaps I closed myself.

1. **Imported business (PA question 1): confirmed.** The import runs after the invitation is accepted, so the seller may already have landed in the Onboarding. Once the import loads a business, the Onboarding is over for it. The Inicio notice never shows again, "Listo" is never shown, and the after-import notice takes over.
   - The Onboarding's remembered passes are not records for the import's eligibility check (import.md's list stays as it is).
   - If the seller has already saved a variety, price, stock entry or another record that counts, the import is refused, as today.
2. **Pre-existing businesses (PA question 2): the behaviour alone is enough.** How they are told apart is the Tech Lead's call.
3. **The notice's count (PA question 3): confirmed.** "N de 6 pasos" counts steps done or passed. Adding more varieties never moves it past steps 1–2.
4. **Definitions that change (PA question 4).** The navigation.md amendment stands. Three more existing definitions follow from it, and I wrote them myself:
   - results.md: Inicio's order;
   - the glossary's **First-run checklist**: now only for businesses that do not get the Onboarding;
   - the glossary's **Onboarding**: now says it is over at "Listo" and over once an import loads.
5. **When it is over.** The Onboarding is over the moment its last optional step is passed (the last step tapped, or "Saltar el resto"). That same tap shows "Listo". Closing the app while on "Listo" leaves it over. A partner who opens it afterwards never sees it again.
6. **Business removal.** The Onboarding's remembered passes are business data like any other row. They are exported and deleted with the business, and the dry run counts them. They hold no secret.
7. **What the notice names next.** "Falta: …" names the resume step, the first step neither done nor passed, in the step's own short name.
8. **Business metrics.** No operator script or dashboard is built for them. They stay something the operator can read from existing records.

### Validated flags

- `touches_ui`: **true**. A new full-screen flow, its closing screen and an Inicio notice, and Inicio changes for a brand-new business.
- `touches_copy`: **true**. New step titles, sentences, buttons, "Seguí con la configuración", "Listo" and its lines.

### PRD

#### Job to be done

**When** I have just opened my business and it is still empty, **I want** to be walked through what it needs, one short step at a time, with defaults already filled in, **so I can** record my first sale the same day without hunting through Configuración, Variedades and the catalog screen.

Success criteria:
1. **Fast, from the phone**: from accepting the invitation to a business ready for its first sale (a variety priced today, with opening stock) in under 5 minutes, from the phone alone.
2. **Only the sale is required**: nothing is mandatory beyond a priced variety and its opening stock. Every other step can be skipped in one tap and says where it can be done later.
3. **Nothing lost**: leaving halfway loses nothing already saved. Resuming picks up at the first step that is neither done nor passed.
4. **One fact, one place**: every step saves the same setting, under the same rules and refusals, that Configuración or the variety's page saves. The Onboarding holds no copy of a setting and no setting of its own.
5. **Simple and attractive, inventing nothing**: built from design-system elements and judged by eye on phone and computer. No placeholder data, no fake figures, and no step for a setting that does not exist yet.

Left out on purpose:
- a business-name step;
- per-variety catalog attributes and the low-stock threshold;
- Apariencia;
- #85's pricing switches, until they exist;
- a "run it again" entry;
- "Terminar por ahora";
- usage tracking;
- illustrations, animation, marketing copy, new colours or new components.

#### Who gets it, and when

1. Only a business just opened by a new-business invitation, from the moment the invitation is accepted.
2. It is never offered to:
   - an imported business: once the import loads a business, the Onboarding is over for it, the notice never shows and the after-import notice takes over;
   - a business a user joined;
   - a business that existed before the Onboarding shipped, which keeps the first-run checklist exactly as it is.
3. There is no "run it again". Once it is over, nothing in the app points back to it.
4. Its state belongs to the business. A partner who joins midway sees the same notice and can carry on. No roles are added.

#### Relation to the first-run checklist

5. For a brand-new business the Onboarding fully replaces the checklist; the two never both apply.
6. While the Onboarding is not over, neither the checklist nor "Arrancá por las variedades" shows. "Arrancá por las variedades" takes over once the Onboarding is over.
7. The side rules stay, tied to the data:
   - while no variety has a price today, "+ Nueva venta" says in words what is missing;
   - while nothing qualifies for the catalog and the two required steps are unmet, "Mandar catálogo" is disabled with its reason in words.
8. navigation.md names the Onboarding as the first-run element for a brand-new business, next to the checklist (for the businesses that keep it) and the after-import notice.

#### Entry and the Inicio notice

9. Accepting a new-business invitation lands in the Onboarding. Accepting a join invitation still lands on Inicio. This is the only time the Onboarding opens by itself.
10. While the Onboarding is not over, Inicio shows "Seguí con la configuración" at the top (position 1), on phone and `InicioCompu`.
    - It shows progress in words ("2 de 6 pasos", counting steps done or passed) and the resume step ("Falta: stock inicial").
    - It opens the Onboarding at the resume step.
    - It cannot be dismissed, and goes for good when the Onboarding is over.
    - Partners see it too.
11. A seller who wants it gone sooner finishes the two required steps and taps "Saltar el resto".

#### The screens

12. A full screen of the form-or-sheet kind: no bar or sidebar, no "+ Nueva venta". One centred, phone-width column on every viewport.
13. Every step shows:
    - a short title and one sentence on why it matters;
    - its fields and one primary action;
    - progress in words as well as shape ("Paso 2 de 6"), never by colour alone;
    - "Atrás";
    - "Saltar", on optional steps only;
    - "Saltar el resto", on optional steps only, once both required steps are done;
    - "Cerrar", which lands on Inicio and keeps everything saved and the resume point.
14. Leaving a step by "Cerrar", "Atrás" or "Saltar" with something typed and not saved asks before discarding. An untouched step leaves at once.
15. Inside the steps, the owners' own behaviour applies unchanged: field rules, inline refusals, toasts, Probarlo's reasons, "Sin conexión", the generic retry and "Eso ya no está.".

#### The six steps, in order

| # | Step | Required | Saves |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | A variety with today's price | Yes | A variety and a price entry effective today (list price, optionally a bulk price and its minimum), under Variedades' rules |
| 2 | Its opening stock | Yes | A stock entry with origin Stock inicial, grams, today's date and an optional total cost |
| 3 | Catalog header and footer | No | The business's header and footer, shown with their defaults |
| 4 | Telegram bot | No | Token, channel chat, own chat (with Buscar mi chat) and Probarlo, exactly as Integraciones |
| 5 | A partner | No | One join link per tap, to copy or share |
| 6 | Expense categories | No | Add, rename and remove, as Categorías de gastos |

16. Step 2 says plainly that with no cost the lot costs $0.
17. After a variety and its opening stock, the seller sees "Agregar otra variedad" or "Seguir".
    - There is no cap, and each variety is saved the moment its step is saved.
    - One variety is enough to continue.
    - Progress stays on steps 1–2 while varieties are being added.
18. Step 4 ends at the saved bot and says: "Cuando quieras, publicalo desde Variedades → Mandar catálogo". Publicar and the pin's status stay on the catalog screen.
19. Step 5 reads "Enviale el link a tu socio" and never waits for the partner to join.
20. A skipped step says where it can be done later: Configuración → Integraciones, Usuarios, Mensaje del catálogo or Categorías de gastos. Skipping step 3 or 6 keeps the defaults.

#### Done and passed

21. Required steps are worked out from the data: step 1 is done when a variety has a price today, step 2 when a stock entry exists.
22. Optional steps are passed by one explicit tap, and the pass is remembered for the business. This stored state is the Onboarding's alone; the checklist stays derive-only.

| Step | Passed by |
|---|---|
| 3 | "Guardar" (edited) or "Dejar así" (defaults kept) |
| 4 | A successful Probarlo ("Hecho"), or "Saltar" (passed without a bot). A failed Probarlo shows its reason and saves nothing |
| 5 | "Seguir" once a link has been created, or "Saltar" |
| 6 | "Seguir" only. Adding, renaming or removing a category saves at once but never passes the step |

23. Data that already exists shows "Hecho" and counts as passed: a bot saved earlier, a second user already in the business.
24. Resume lands on the first step neither done nor passed.
25. Leaving ("Cerrar", closing the app, a dropped connection) keeps everything saved and the resume point.
26. "Saltar el resto" passes every remaining optional step at once.
27. The Onboarding never ends before both required steps are met.

#### "Listo"

28. The tap that passes the last optional step (or "Saltar el resto") ends the Onboarding and shows "Listo". From then on it is over for good: closing "Listo" by any means, or the app, never brings it back, and the Inicio notice goes.
29. "Listo" has one line per step:
    - "Hecho" for a step whose data exists or that was saved;
    - "Para después — en Configuración → <grupo>" or "en Variedades" for a skipped step;
    - the required steps' lines count the records made, e.g. "Hecho · 2 variedades", never a figure or an amount.
30. Its actions are the primary "Cargar mi primera venta", which opens Nueva venta, and the secondary "Ir a Inicio". No celebration and no extra content.
31. Nueva venta opened from "Listo" goes back to Inicio, whether closed or saved.

#### Empty states and errors

32. Step 1 starts with empty fields. No step shows a figure, a placeholder or sample data.
33. Refusals and failures belong to each setting's owner: inline, keeping what was typed, carrying no data. "Sin conexión" and the generic retry apply as everywhere.
34. A record that is gone follows "Eso ya no está.". Data a partner created shows "Hecho" on the next open, with no live updates.

#### Design

35. Design-system elements only: tokens, components and both themes. If nothing fits the progress indicator or the step layout, design draws it from tokens, inside the design system, before it is built.
36. Rioplatense Spanish with vos. Nothing clipped at 320–430 px or at 130 % text; controls at least 44 px tall and inputs at least 16 px.
37. Every step and "Listo" go into `scripts/design/screens.json`, and `compare.sh` runs before and after, phone and computer, judged by eye.

#### Privacy and isolation

38. The Onboarding's state carries `business_id`.
    - It is exported and deleted by business removal, and counted in the dry run.
    - Its routes join the isolation sweep and the gated-route test.
    - Another business's state does not exist (404 or absent).
    - Nothing about it goes to logs, the console or browser storage, and nothing is stored about time taken or where a seller left.

#### Metrics

There is no telemetry.
- **Under 5 minutes (criterion 1)**: an end-to-end phone run accepts an invitation, prices a variety, loads its opening stock, passes the optional steps and opens Nueva venta from "Listo", in under 5 minutes.
- **Nothing lost (criterion 3)**: a resume end-to-end run, plus a resume matrix, 100 % passing, covering:
  - required steps worked out from the data;
  - optional passes remembered;
  - "Hecho" for data that already exists;
  - no end before the required steps.
- **One fact, one place (criterion 4)**: a value saved in each step reads the same in its usual place, and the reverse.
- **Only the sale is required (criterion 2)**: each optional step can be skipped in one tap; "Saltar el resto" shows only after the required steps; "Listo" names where each skipped step lives.
- **Who gets it**:
  - a brand-new business gets it;
  - an imported business (even one that landed in it first), a joined user's business and a pre-existing business never do;
  - the checklist never shows with it.
- **Inicio and chrome**, phone and computer:
  - the notice sits at the top, cannot be dismissed and keeps its count on steps 1–2 while varieties are added;
  - neither "Arrancá por las variedades" nor the checklist shows with it;
  - the Onboarding has no bar and no "+ Nueva venta";
  - Nueva venta opened from "Listo" returns to Inicio.
- **Isolation**: the sweep, the gated-route test and business removal cover the Onboarding's state.
- **Design (criterion 5)**: `screens.json`, `compare.sh` before and after, and a by-eye pass in both themes.
- **Business metrics**, read by the operator from existing records only:
  - the share of new businesses with a priced variety and a stock entry;
  - the share whose first sale falls on their opening day.

### For the next role

Tech Lead:
1. **A remembered state for the business.** The Onboarding is the product's first stored "passed" state for a business. It needs:
   - `business_id`, isolation-sweep and gated-route coverage;
   - export and delete under ADR-0015's schema reflection, counted in the dry run.

   It must not count as a record for import eligibility. A successful import ends it.
2. **The new-business acceptance** must land in the Onboarding; the join acceptance still lands on Inicio. Telling a pre-existing business apart is your decision; the behaviour is fixed.
3. **No new rules in the steps.** Every step reuses its owner's use cases and refusal codes (varieties, stock, catalog, pinned catalog's Probarlo and Buscar mi chat, invitations, expense categories), with no copies (criterion 4).
4. **Inicio** answers, from the server, whether the Onboarding is over, its step count and the resume step, for the session's business only.
5. **Two lines left as they were.** accounts.md ("They land on Inicio of that business") and catalog.md ("Mandar catálogo" disabled only while the checklist shows) are superseded for new businesses by onboarding.md and navigation.md. Update them in the same change.
6. **Design comes first.** Design draws the progress indicator and the step layout from tokens before they are built; every screen's route goes into `scripts/design/screens.json`.

   - export and delete under ADR-0015's schema reflection, counted in the dry run.

   It must not count as a record for import eligibility. A successful import ends it.
2. **The new-business acceptance** must land in the Onboarding; the join acceptance still lands on Inicio. Telling a pre-existing business apart is your decision; the behaviour is fixed.
3. **No new rules in the steps.** Every step reuses its owner's use cases and refusal codes (varieties, stock, catalog, pinned catalog's Probarlo and Buscar mi chat, invitations, expense categories), with no copies (criterion 4).
4. **Inicio** answers, from the server, whether the Onboarding is over, its step count and the resume step, for the session's business only.
5. **Two lines left as they were.** accounts.md ("They land on Inicio of that business") and catalog.md ("Mandar catálogo" disabled only while the checklist shows) are superseded for new businesses by onboarding.md and navigation.md. Update them in the same change.
6. **Design comes first.** Design draws the progress indicator and the step layout from tokens before they are built; every screen's route goes into `scripts/design/screens.json`.

### Clarifications (round 1)

Both elements stay defined in navigation.md and get built in their own task. Until then, their rules apply to nobody.

The interview kept them only on the belief that they already existed, so there is nothing to carry over. While the Onboarding runs, the job is still served:

## PM

### Verdict

`approved`: the PRD follows every decision from the three rounds and serves the settled job. The gaps left were small and are closed below.

### Adjustments

These are the answers to the Product Analyst's five questions, plus four small gaps I closed myself.

1. **Imported business (PA question 1): confirmed.** The import runs after the invitation is accepted, so the seller may already have landed in the Onboarding. Once the import loads a business, the Onboarding is over for it. The Inicio notice never shows again, "Listo" is never shown, and the after-import notice takes over.
   - The Onboarding's remembered passes are not records for the import's eligibility check (import.md's list stays as it is).
   - If the seller has already saved a variety, price, stock entry or another record that counts, the import is refused, as today.
2. **Pre-existing businesses (PA question 2): the behaviour alone is enough.** How they are told apart is the Tech Lead's call.
3. **The notice's count (PA question 3): confirmed.** "N de 6 pasos" counts steps done or passed. Adding more varieties never moves it past steps 1–2.
4. **Definitions that change (PA question 4).** The navigation.md amendment stands. Three more existing definitions follow from it, and I wrote them myself:
   - results.md: Inicio's order;
   - the glossary's **First-run checklist**: now only for businesses that do not get the Onboarding;
   - the glossary's **Onboarding**: now says it is over at "Listo" and over once an import loads.
5. **When it is over.** The Onboarding is over the moment its last optional step is passed (the last step tapped, or "Saltar el resto"). That same tap shows "Listo". Closing the app while on "Listo" leaves it over. A partner who opens it afterwards never sees it again.
6. **Business removal.** The Onboarding's remembered passes are business data like any other row. They are exported and deleted with the business, and the dry run counts them. They hold no secret.
7. **What the notice names next.** "Falta: …" names the resume step, the first step neither done nor passed, in the step's own short name.
8. **Business metrics.** No operator script or dashboard is built for them. They stay something the operator can read from existing records.

### Validated flags

- `touches_ui`: **true**. A new full-screen flow, its closing screen and an Inicio notice, and Inicio changes for a brand-new business.
- `touches_copy`: **true**. New step titles, sentences, buttons, "Seguí con la configuración", "Listo" and its lines.

### PRD

#### Job to be done

**When** I have just opened my business and it is still empty, **I want** to be walked through what it needs, one short step at a time, with defaults already filled in, **so I can** record my first sale the same day without hunting through Configuración, Variedades and the catalog screen.

Success criteria:
1. **Fast, from the phone**: from accepting the invitation to a business ready for its first sale (a variety priced today, with opening stock) in under 5 minutes, from the phone alone.
2. **Only the sale is required**: nothing is mandatory beyond a priced variety and its opening stock. Every other step can be skipped in one tap and says where it can be done later.
3. **Nothing lost**: leaving halfway loses nothing already saved. Resuming picks up at the first step that is neither done nor passed.
4. **One fact, one place**: every step saves the same setting, under the same rules and refusals, that Configuración or the variety's page saves. The Onboarding holds no copy of a setting and no setting of its own.
5. **Simple and attractive, inventing nothing**: built from design-system elements and judged by eye on phone and computer. No placeholder data, no fake figures, and no step for a setting that does not exist yet.

Left out on purpose:
- a business-name step;
- per-variety catalog attributes and the low-stock threshold;
- Apariencia;
- #85's pricing switches, until they exist;
- a "run it again" entry;
- "Terminar por ahora";
- usage tracking;
- illustrations, animation, marketing copy, new colours or new components.

#### Who gets it, and when

1. Only a business just opened by a new-business invitation, from the moment the invitation is accepted.
2. It is never offered to:
   - an imported business: once the import loads a business, the Onboarding is over for it, the notice never shows and the after-import notice takes over;
   - a business a user joined;
   - a business that existed before the Onboarding shipped, which keeps the first-run checklist exactly as it is.
3. There is no "run it again". Once it is over, nothing in the app points back to it.
4. Its state belongs to the business. A partner who joins midway sees the same notice and can carry on. No roles are added.

#### Relation to the first-run checklist

5. For a brand-new business the Onboarding fully replaces the checklist; the two never both apply.
6. While the Onboarding is not over, neither the checklist nor "Arrancá por las variedades" shows. "Arrancá por las variedades" takes over once the Onboarding is over.
7. The side rules stay, tied to the data:
   - while no variety has a price today, "+ Nueva venta" says in words what is missing;
   - while nothing qualifies for the catalog and the two required steps are unmet, "Mandar catálogo" is disabled with its reason in words.
8. navigation.md names the Onboarding as the first-run element for a brand-new business, next to the checklist (for the businesses that keep it) and the after-import notice.

#### Entry and the Inicio notice

9. Accepting a new-business invitation lands in the Onboarding. Accepting a join invitation still lands on Inicio. This is the only time the Onboarding opens by itself.
10. While the Onboarding is not over, Inicio shows "Seguí con la configuración" at the top (position 1), on phone and `InicioCompu`.
    - It shows progress in words ("2 de 6 pasos", counting steps done or passed) and the resume step ("Falta: stock inicial").
    - It opens the Onboarding at the resume step.
    - It cannot be dismissed, and goes for good when the Onboarding is over.
    - Partners see it too.
11. A seller who wants it gone sooner finishes the two required steps and taps "Saltar el resto".

#### The screens

12. A full screen of the form-or-sheet kind: no bar or sidebar, no "+ Nueva venta". One centred, phone-width column on every viewport.
13. Every step shows:
    - a short title and one sentence on why it matters;
    - its fields and one primary action;
    - progress in words as well as shape ("Paso 2 de 6"), never by colour alone;
    - "Atrás";
    - "Saltar", on optional steps only;
    - "Saltar el resto", on optional steps only, once both required steps are done;
    - "Cerrar", which lands on Inicio and keeps everything saved and the resume point.
14. Leaving a step by "Cerrar", "Atrás" or "Saltar" with something typed and not saved asks before discarding. An untouched step leaves at once.
15. Inside the steps, the owners' own behaviour applies unchanged: field rules, inline refusals, toasts, Probarlo's reasons, "Sin conexión", the generic retry and "Eso ya no está.".

#### The six steps, in order

| # | Step | Required | Saves |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | A variety with today's price | Yes | A variety and a price entry effective today (list price, optionally a bulk price and its minimum), under Variedades' rules |
| 2 | Its opening stock | Yes | A stock entry with origin Stock inicial, grams, today's date and an optional total cost |
| 3 | Catalog header and footer | No | The business's header and footer, shown with their defaults |
| 4 | Telegram bot | No | Token, channel chat, own chat (with Buscar mi chat) and Probarlo, exactly as Integraciones |
| 5 | A partner | No | One join link per tap, to copy or share |
| 6 | Expense categories | No | Add, rename and remove, as Categorías de gastos |

16. Step 2 says plainly that with no cost the lot costs $0.
17. After a variety and its opening stock, the seller sees "Agregar otra variedad" or "Seguir".
    - There is no cap, and each variety is saved the moment its step is saved.
    - One variety is enough to continue.
    - Progress stays on steps 1–2 while varieties are being added.
18. Step 4 ends at the saved bot and says: "Cuando quieras, publicalo desde Variedades → Mandar catálogo". Publicar and the pin's status stay on the catalog screen.
19. Step 5 reads "Enviale el link a tu socio" and never waits for the partner to join.
20. A skipped step says where it can be done later: Configuración → Integraciones, Usuarios, Mensaje del catálogo or Categorías de gastos. Skipping step 3 or 6 keeps the defaults.

#### Done and passed

21. Required steps are worked out from the data: step 1 is done when a variety has a price today, step 2 when a stock entry exists.
22. Optional steps are passed by one explicit tap, and the pass is remembered for the business. This stored state is the Onboarding's alone; the checklist stays derive-only.

| Step | Passed by |
|---|---|
| 3 | "Guardar" (edited) or "Dejar así" (defaults kept) |
| 4 | A successful Probarlo ("Hecho"), or "Saltar" (passed without a bot). A failed Probarlo shows its reason and saves nothing |
| 5 | "Seguir" once a link has been created, or "Saltar" |
| 6 | "Seguir" only. Adding, renaming or removing a category saves at once but never passes the step |

23. Data that already exists shows "Hecho" and counts as passed: a bot saved earlier, a second user already in the business.
24. Resume lands on the first step neither done nor passed.
25. Leaving ("Cerrar", closing the app, a dropped connection) keeps everything saved and the resume point.
26. "Saltar el resto" passes every remaining optional step at once.
27. The Onboarding never ends before both required steps are met.

#### "Listo"

28. The tap that passes the last optional step (or "Saltar el resto") ends the Onboarding and shows "Listo". From then on it is over for good: closing "Listo" by any means, or the app, never brings it back, and the Inicio notice goes.
29. "Listo" has one line per step:
    - "Hecho" for a step whose data exists or that was saved;
    - "Para después — en Configuración → <grupo>" or "en Variedades" for a skipped step;
    - the required steps' lines count the records made, e.g. "Hecho · 2 variedades", never a figure or an amount.
30. Its actions are the primary "Cargar mi primera venta", which opens Nueva venta, and the secondary "Ir a Inicio". No celebration and no extra content.
31. Nueva venta opened from "Listo" goes back to Inicio, whether closed or saved.

#### Empty states and errors

32. Step 1 starts with empty fields. No step shows a figure, a placeholder or sample data.
33. Refusals and failures belong to each setting's owner: inline, keeping what was typed, carrying no data. "Sin conexión" and the generic retry apply as everywhere.
34. A record that is gone follows "Eso ya no está.". Data a partner created shows "Hecho" on the next open, with no live updates.

#### Design

35. Design-system elements only: tokens, components and both themes. If nothing fits the progress indicator or the step layout, design draws it from tokens, inside the design system, before it is built.
36. Rioplatense Spanish with vos. Nothing clipped at 320–430 px or at 130 % text; controls at least 44 px tall and inputs at least 16 px.
37. Every step and "Listo" go into `scripts/design/screens.json`, and `compare.sh` runs before and after, phone and computer, judged by eye.

#### Privacy and isolation

38. The Onboarding's state carries `business_id`.
    - It is exported and deleted by business removal, and counted in the dry run.
    - Its routes join the isolation sweep and the gated-route test.
    - Another business's state does not exist (404 or absent).
    - Nothing about it goes to logs, the console or browser storage, and nothing is stored about time taken or where a seller left.

#### Metrics

There is no telemetry.
- **Under 5 minutes (criterion 1)**: an end-to-end phone run accepts an invitation, prices a variety, loads its opening stock, passes the optional steps and opens Nueva venta from "Listo", in under 5 minutes.
- **Nothing lost (criterion 3)**: a resume end-to-end run, plus a resume matrix, 100 % passing, covering:
  - required steps worked out from the data;
  - optional passes remembered;
  - "Hecho" for data that already exists;
  - no end before the required steps.
- **One fact, one place (criterion 4)**: a value saved in each step reads the same in its usual place, and the reverse.
- **Only the sale is required (criterion 2)**: each optional step can be skipped in one tap; "Saltar el resto" shows only after the required steps; "Listo" names where each skipped step lives.
- **Who gets it**:
  - a brand-new business gets it;
  - an imported business (even one that landed in it first), a joined user's business and a pre-existing business never do;
  - the checklist never shows with it.
- **Inicio and chrome**, phone and computer:
  - the notice sits at the top, cannot be dismissed and keeps its count on steps 1–2 while varieties are added;
  - neither "Arrancá por las variedades" nor the checklist shows with it;
  - the Onboarding has no bar and no "+ Nueva venta";
  - Nueva venta opened from "Listo" returns to Inicio.
- **Isolation**: the sweep, the gated-route test and business removal cover the Onboarding's state.
- **Design (criterion 5)**: `screens.json`, `compare.sh` before and after, and a by-eye pass in both themes.
- **Business metrics**, read by the operator from existing records only:
  - the share of new businesses with a priced variety and a stock entry;
  - the share whose first sale falls on their opening day.

### Clarifications (round 3)

### Answer to the Tech Lead (round 3)

**Your default stands: the notice reads "Seguí con la configuración" and "6 de 6 pasos", with no "Falta" line.** Tapping it opens "Listo".

One correction to how it ends. Reaching "Listo" ends the Onboarding. No tap on "Listo" is needed. This matches the rule already written: "over once Listo is reached; closing it by any means never brings it back." So:
- Opening "Listo" from the notice ends the Onboarding right away.
- If the seller leaves "Listo" without tapping anything, Inicio no longer shows the notice.

**The general rule**, which also covers the usual path:
- Once both required steps are done and every optional step is passed, the next screen the Onboarding shows is "Listo", and showing it ends the Onboarding.
- In the usual path, that happens on the tap that passes the last optional step.
- In this rare state, it happens when the notice is tapped.
- Until then, nothing ends the Onboarding: a price added from the variety's page does not end it.

I added this to onboarding.md: the notice's wording when every step is done, how "Listo" ends the Onboarding, and a line for this state in the resume matrix.

## UX Designer

### Flow

**Design data note.** The prototype (`docs/product/design-system/pantallas`) has no screen for the Onboarding's steps, the "Paso N de 6" progress indicator, "Listo" or the Inicio notice "Seguí con la configuración". The PRD requires design to draw the progress indicator and step layout from tokens before build. I do not invent those screens here. Each step below names the existing screen that owns its content (VariedadNueva, PrecioNuevo, EntradaStock, Catalogo and the Configuración screens). Where I write "Onboarding step screen" or "Listo", that screen is not drawn yet. UX Lead should flag this.

### Job: set up a new business after creating it (main flow)

**Starts:** the person accepts a new-business invitation (Invitacion). Entry is only through this acceptance. A join invitation still lands on Inicio and is unchanged.

**Layout on every step:** full-screen Onboarding. It has no bar and no "+ Nueva venta", and it is one centred phone-width column on every viewport. Controls on every step:
- "Paso N de 6"
- "Atrás"
- "Cerrar"
- "Saltar" (optional steps only)
- "Saltar el resto" (optional steps, only once both required steps are done)

**Steps**
