1. **Step 1, variety with today's price (required).** Screen: Onboarding step screen with the variety and price content (references: VariedadNueva, PrecioNuevo).
   - The person types the name and today's price, then saves (about 3 touches plus typing).
   - Afterwards the screen offers "Agregar otra variedad" or "Seguir". Progress stays on steps 1–2 while varieties are added. Each extra variety costs about 3 touches.
2. **Step 2, opening stock (required).** Screen: Onboarding step screen with the stock content (reference: EntradaStock).
   - The person picks a variety, enters the quantity and saves (about 3 touches).
   - Afterwards the screen offers "Agregar otra variedad" or "Seguir".
3. **Step 3, catalog header and footer (optional).** References: Catalogo, Configuración → Mensaje.
   - Passed by "Guardar" or "Dejar así" (1 touch to leave as is, or typing plus 1 to save). "Saltar" is the alternative.
4. **Step 4, Telegram bot (optional).** References: Configuración → Integraciones.
   - "Buscar mi chat" finds the chat, then "Probarlo" runs the pinned-catalog test (2 touches).
   - Passed by a successful Probarlo, or by "Saltar" (1 touch).
5. **Step 5, a partner's join link (optional).** Reference: Configuración → Usuarios.
   - The person creates a link (1–2 touches), then "Seguir" (1 touch). "Saltar" is the alternative (1 touch).
6. **Step 6, expense categories (optional).** Reference: Configuración → Categorías.
   - Passed by "Seguir" only (1 touch). Leaving a step by any other route does not pass it.

**Ends:** the Onboarding ends at the tap that passes step 6, or at "Saltar el resto", and shows "Listo". It also ends if an import loads the business. It never ends before both required steps are met.

**"Listo" screen (not drawn yet):**
- One line per step.
  - "Hecho", or "Hecho · 2 variedades" for the required steps, which count records.
  - "Para después — en Configuración → <grupo>" or "en Variedades" for steps left for later.
- Actions: "Cargar mi primera venta" and "Ir a Inicio".
- Nueva venta opened from "Listo" returns to Inicio on close or save.

**Touch count, fastest path:**
- Required steps only: about 3 + 1 + 3 + 1 touches (one variety, one stock entry, "Seguir" after each), then "Saltar el resto" and "Cargar mi primera venta". That is about 10–11 touches plus typing.
- All six steps done: roughly 16–20 touches plus typing.
- Both are well inside the under-5-minutes budget from the phone.

**What can go wrong and how the person recovers**
- **Unsaved changes on "Cerrar", "Atrás" or "Saltar":** the screen asks before discarding. The person can keep editing or discard. An untouched step leaves at once.
- **Inline refusals** (empty name, invalid price, invalid quantity, and so on): they use the owner's rule and wording, and what was typed is kept. The person corrects it and saves again.
- **Probarlo or Buscar mi chat refuses:** the pinned-catalog and chat-search reasons are shown. The person retries, or taps "Saltar" and the step is passed.
- **Link creation refused:** the accounts invitation rule applies. The person retries or taps "Saltar".
- **"Sin conexión" or a generic failure:** the retry is offered and the typed data is kept.
- **"Eso ya no está.":** a record was removed, for example by a partner. The person goes back to the step and picks again.
- **A partner changes data mid-flow:** required steps are derived from the data, so the step state follows the data on the next load.
- **An import loads the business mid-flow:** the Onboarding ends and shows "Listo".

### Job: leave and resume the setup

**Starts:** any step.
1. "Cerrar" (1 touch, plus 1 to confirm discard if there are unsaved changes). The person lands on Inicio and the resume point is kept.
2. Inicio shows the notice "Seguí con la configuración" at the top, on phone (Inicio) and on InicioCompu. It shows "N de 6 pasos" and "Falta: …", and it cannot be dismissed.
3. Tapping it opens the Onboarding at the first step that is neither done nor passed (1 touch).

**Ends:** back on the resume step.

**Touch count:** 2 touches to leave and return, 3 if discarding changes.

**Recovery:**
- Optional passes are stored per business by one explicit tap. Required steps are derived from the data, so there is nothing stale to repair.
- If a record is gone, "Eso ya no está." applies.

**Unsupported by the prototype:** the notice is not drawn on Inicio or InicioCompu.

### Job: skip optional steps

**Starts:** an optional step (3–6).
- "Saltar" passes this step (1 touch, plus 1 to confirm discard if the step is touched).
- "Saltar el resto" appears only once both required steps are done. It passes every remaining optional step and goes to "Listo" (1 touch, plus 1 to confirm discard if touched).
- Step 6 is passed only by "Seguir", so it has no "Saltar" route to ending other than "Saltar el resto".

**Recovery:** "Atrás" returns to an earlier step. Skipped steps appear in "Listo" as "Para después — en Configuración → <grupo>".

### Job: first sale after setup

**Starts:** "Listo".
1. "Cargar mi primera venta" opens Nueva venta, which is unchanged (NuevaVenta and NuevaVentaCompu) (1 touch).
2. On close or save the person goes back to Inicio.

**Alternative:** "Ir a Inicio" (1 touch).

**While the Onboarding is not over:**
- The checklist and "Arrancá por las variedades" are not shown.
- "+ Nueva venta" still says what is missing when there is no price today.
- "Mandar catálogo" stays disabled with its reason while nothing qualifies and the required steps are unmet.

### Jobs whose flow is unchanged

- **Joining a business through a join invitation:** unchanged. It lands on Inicio (Invitacion), with no Onboarding.
- **Navigation, Inicio otherwise:** unchanged except for the added top notice "Seguí con la configuración" while the Onboarding is not over.
- **Sale, payments (Cobro), clients, stock, variety and price maintenance, catalog sending, expenses (Gastos, GastoNuevo), analysis, import, accounts and Configuración:** unchanged. The steps reuse these owners' rules, refusals and toasts without changing them. The only addition is that Nueva venta opened from "Listo" returns to Inicio.

## UX Lead

_The system denied 1 write attempt: this role writes nothing; reproduce by reading the code, the task documents and the existing tests, or by running the declared checks. A denial here is not a syntax problem._

### Walk

The prototype (`docs/product/design-system/pantallas`) has no screen for the Onboarding itself: no step layout, no "Paso N de 6" indicator, no "Listo" and no Inicio notice. I walked each step through the existing screen that owns its content and judged the Onboarding's own screens on the flow's text alone, marked **sin prototipo**.

**Job: set up a new business (main flow)**
- **Starts.** The flow names *Invitacion*, but in the prototype that is the join screen ("Te invitaron a La Huerta… Sumate a La Huerta"). The new-business acceptance is *NegocioNuevo* ("Armá tu negocio… Creá el negocio"). The landing in the Onboarding must hang off NegocioNuevo's submit. Observation.
- **Step layout and "Paso N de 6"**: sin prototipo. The controls the flow lists (Atrás, Cerrar, Saltar, Saltar el resto) match the PRD.
- **Step 1**: sin prototipo for the step screen. The references split this job across two screens:
  - *VariedadNueva* takes only the name ("Agregá la variedad").
  - *PrecioNuevo* has Rige desde fixed to "Hoy", Precio de lista, an optional Precio por cantidad and Mínimo en gramos.

  The flow's "name and today's price, then saves, ~3 touches" does not say whether this is one save or two. It also does not say what happens when the variety is saved and the price is refused. Observation.
- **"Agregar otra variedad"**: the flow offers it after step 1 and after step 2. The PRD's loop is name, price and opening stock per variety. Offering it after step 1 lets the seller create varieties without stock. Observation.
- **Step 2**: sin prototipo for the step screen. Reference *EntradaStock*:
  - Frame 1 has a Variedad selector and Origen chips.
  - Frame 2 ("Mango Bomb, Stock inicial preseleccionado") labels Costo total "Opcional para stock inicial".

  The flow says the person "picks a variety". It should say the step comes with the new variety and Stock inicial already chosen. It is also missing the PRD's line "with no cost, the lot costs $0". Observation.
- **Step 3**: reference *ConfiguracionMensaje* has Encabezado and Pie with defaults and "Guardá el mensaje". "Dejar así" and "Guardar" fit those defaults. No finding.
- **Step 4**: reference *ConfiguracionIntegraciones* has:
  - Token del bot;
  - Chat del canal;
  - Tu chat ("Abrí tu chat con el bot y escribile cualquier cosa para verlo acá");
  - "Enviarme una prueba", not "Probarlo".

  The flow's "2 touches" leaves out the token, the channel and the trip out to Telegram and back. Nothing is saved until the test succeeds, so the flow should say what survives a reload during that trip. Observation.
- **Step 5**: reference *ConfiguracionUsuarios* has "Invitá a un socio" and a link with "Copiar". The flow matches. No finding.
- **Step 6**: reference *ConfiguracionCategorias* has the default categories, Renombrar, Eliminar, "+ Categoría" and the refusal "No se puede borrar: todavía tiene gastos". The flow says step 6 has no "Saltar", but the PRD layout puts "Saltar" on every optional step. Not a dead end, because "Seguir" is one tap. Observation.
- **Ends.**
  - **Required step undone at step 6.** If a partner removes the only priced variety or stock entry while the seller is on steps 3–6, passing step 6 cannot end the Onboarding, and the flow does not say where that tap lands. Observation.
  - **Import mid-flow.** The flow says the Onboarding "ends and shows 'Listo'". That contradicts PM adjustment 1: once an import loads, "Listo" is never shown and the after-import notice takes over (the *InicioPrimeraVez* "Centro Floral" frame). The import also runs outside the Onboarding, so there is no "Listo" to show. Observation; it must be corrected to the PM's decision.
- **"Listo"**: sin prototipo. The lines and the two actions match the PRD. Nueva venta (*NuevaVenta*, *NuevaVentaCompu*) is unchanged.
- **Recovery.** The discard dialogs exist on every reference screen with the same pattern ("¿Descartás…? / Se pierde lo que cargaste…"), so "asks before discarding" is backed by the prototype.

**Job: leave and resume**
- "Cerrar" exists on every reference form.
- The Inicio notice "Seguí con la configuración" is sin prototipo on both Inicio and InicioCompu. In *InicioPrimeraVez*, position 1 holds the "Para arrancar" checklist, and the notice takes that slot. That matches results.md's order as amended.
- The resume route is coherent: tapping the notice leads to the first step neither done nor passed. No finding beyond the missing drawing.

**Job: skip optional steps**
- "Saltar" and "Saltar el resto" are sin prototipo. The discard confirmation is backed by the reference screens.
- The step 6 inconsistency is noted above.

**Job: first sale after setup**
- *NuevaVenta* exists and is unchanged.
- *InicioPrimeraVez* already shows "+ Nueva venta" saying what is missing ("Cargale un precio a una variedad para poder vender"), so that side rule is backed.
- Returning to Inicio after Nueva venta is judged on the text alone.

**Unchanged jobs**
- Joining a business through a join invitation goes from *Invitacion* to Inicio, as the prototype shows. No finding.

### Verdict

`approved`: every job can be completed as written, and the Onboarding's own screens are sin prototipo, which is a fact about the prototype and not a defect of the flow. UX Designer should still fix the observations, first of all the import case, which must follow the PM's decision that "Listo" is never shown after an import.

## UI Designer

### Screens

Convention for the whole section: every screen is composed from the prototype's own tokens and components ("Papel y azul", light and dark through `data-tema="oscuro"`, blue ↔ amber and never green ↔ red, no state told by color alone, controls ≥ 44 px, inputs ≥ 16 px, nothing clipped at 320–430 px or 130 % text). No new visual language is introduced. Where the product has no drawn screen or component, it is flagged as a **Gap for UI Lead**. Every new screen and state must be registered in `scripts/design/screens.json`, and `compare.sh` must run before and after.

### Onboarding step container (shared by steps 1–6)

- **Built from:** the full-screen form-or-sheet kind, with no bar, no sidebar and no "+ Nueva venta". The layout is the single centred phone-width column of the getting-in screens (`AuthPage`, ADR-0025). It stays one column on every viewport, so it has no computer layout of its own. The "Cerrar" chrome comes from the existing form sheets. Each step's body is the owner's existing screen content, which the sections below name.
- **Fixed parts on every step:**
  - "Paso N de 6" at the top;
  - "Atrás" (hidden on step 1) and "Cerrar";
  - the step's title and its one-line reason;
  - the owner's fields;
  - the primary action at the bottom;
  - "Saltar" on the optional steps 3–6;
  - "Saltar el resto" on the optional steps, only once steps 1 and 2 are done.
- **States:**
  - Filled and untouched: the leave action closes at once.
  - Dirty: pressing "Atrás" or "Cerrar" shows the existing discard confirmation (the same one the form sheets use). "Cerrar" lands on Inicio and keeps the resume point.
  - Already done: the step opens with "Hecho" and the existing data, and "Seguir" is the primary action.
  - Loading: the "Estados" loading with no figures.
  - "Sin conexión" and the generic retry: the "Estados" versions, with the typed data kept.
  - "Eso ya no está.": the "Estados" version, for when something was removed elsewhere.
- **PRD change:** the container is new. It reuses the owners' rules, refusals, toasts and wording unchanged.
- **Gap for UI Lead:** the prototype has no Onboarding step layout and no "Paso N de 6" indicator. They must be drawn from existing tokens (the type scale, the spacing and the blue accent). The indicator needs a text form ("Paso 3 de 6") and must not rely on color alone. The multi-line "Saltar" / "Saltar el resto" / "Atrás" arrangement at 320 px and at 130 % text must be drawn, with each control at least 44 px.

### Entry: NegocioNuevo

- **Built from:** NegocioNuevo ("Armá tu negocio… Creá el negocio") as is. Invitacion is not the entry; it only joins an existing business.
- **States:** its existing ones (empty, filled, submitting, error).
- **PRD change:** after "Creá el negocio" succeeds, the user lands on step 1 of the Onboarding instead of Inicio. Nothing else changes on the screen.

### Step 1: variedad con el precio de hoy (required)

- **Built from:** the fields of VariedadNueva (name) and PrecioNuevo (Precio de lista, optional Precio por cantidad, Mínimo en gramos) in one container. "Rige desde" is fixed to "Hoy", as in PrecioNuevo.
- **States:**
  - Empty: the name and the price are empty, and the primary action is disabled or refuses, as the owners do.
  - Filled.
  - Saving.
  - Field errors: the owners' refusals, shown inline with their wording.
  - Partial save: the variety is saved and the price is refused. The step stays on the screen, the saved name shows as saved, and the price field carries the refusal. Retrying saves only the price and does not create the variety again.
  - Done: it shows "Hecho" with the existing variety and its price.
  - Loading, "Sin conexión" and the generic retry: the shared Estados.
- **PRD change:** the name and the price come together on one step. This step is required, so it has no "Saltar".
- **Gap for UI Lead:** the prototype has no screen that combines name and price. The composition is flagged as new.

### Step 2: stock inicial (required)

- **Built from:** EntradaStock. The variety selector arrives with the variety from step 1 preselected, and Origen is preselected as "Stock inicial". "Costo total" keeps "Opcional para stock inicial", and a line under it states that without a cost the lot is saved at $0.
- **States:**
  - Empty: the quantity is empty.
  - Filled.
  - Saving.
  - Refusal: the owner's refusal, with its wording.
  - Done: "Hecho" with the existing stock.
  - After saving: the choice "Agregar otra variedad" or "Seguir".
  - Loading, "Sin conexión" and the generic retry: the shared Estados.
- **PRD change:**
  - The "Costo total" empty means a $0 lot, and the screen says so.
  - "Agregar otra variedad" loops back to step 1 (name and price), then step 2, with the count staying on steps 1–2.
  - This step is required, so it has no "Saltar".

### Step 3: encabezado y pie del catálogo (optional)

- **Built from:** ConfiguracionMensaje (Encabezado, Pie, the defaults).
- **States:**
  - Default: it shows the default text.
  - Edited.
  - Saving.
  - Error: the owner's wording.
  - Passed: "Hecho" after "Guardar" or "Dejar así".
  - Loading, "Sin conexión" and the generic retry: the shared Estados.
- **PRD change:**
  - The actions are "Guardar" and "Dejar así" (instead of "Guardá el mensaje"). Either one passes the step.
  - It also has "Saltar", and "Saltar el resto" once steps 1–2 are done.
  - "Atrás" and "Cerrar" apply as in the shared container.

### Step 4: bot de Telegram (optional)

- **Built from:** ConfiguracionIntegraciones (Token del bot, Chat del canal, Tu chat, "Enviarme una prueba", Buscar mi chat).
- **States:**
  - Empty.
  - Filled.
  - Testing.
  - Success: the step passes after a successful "Probarlo".
  - Failure: the owner's wording, with the typed data kept.
  - Already configured: "Hecho".
  - Loading, "Sin conexión" and the generic retry: the shared Estados.
- **PRD change:**
  - A successful "Probarlo" passes the step, and so does "Saltar".
  - The control is called "Probarlo" per the PRD, where the prototype's button says "Enviarme una prueba". UI Lead should confirm which label is used.
  - "Saltar el resto" shows once steps 1–2 are done.

### Step 5: link para un socio (optional)

- **Built from:** ConfiguracionUsuarios ("Invitá a un socio", the link with Copiar).
- **States:**
  - No link yet: the invite action is the primary one.
  - Link created: the link with "Copiar", and "Seguir" as the primary action.
  - Copied: the owner's toast.
  - Error: the owner's wording.
  - Already has a link: "Hecho".
  - Loading, "Sin conexión" and the generic retry: the shared Estados.
- **PRD change:** "Seguir" passes the step once a link exists, and "Saltar" passes it without one. "Saltar el resto" shows once steps 1–2 are done.

### Step 6: categorías de gastos (optional)

- **Built from:** ConfiguracionCategorias (the defaults, Renombrar, Eliminar, "+ Categoría", and the refusal "No se puede borrar: todavía tiene gastos. Podés renombrarla.").
- **States:**
  - Defaults.
  - Edited.
  - Adding.
  - Renaming.
  - Refusal: the existing wording.
  - Loading, "Sin conexión" and the generic retry: the shared Estados.
- **PRD change:**
  - "Seguir" is the only way to pass the step.
  - "Saltar" is also present, per the PRD. It leaves the step not passed, so "Listo" shows it as "Para después".
  - It is the last step, so there is no "Saltar el resto" here, because there is nothing left to skip.

### Listo

- **Built from:** the same centred column, with the lines of the step summary. The only components are the existing list rows and buttons.
- **Content:** one line per step: "Hecho", "Hecho · N variedades", or "Para después — en Configuración → <grupo>" / "en Variedades". The primary action is "Cargar mi primera venta" and the secondary is "Ir a Inicio". There is no celebration and nothing else.
- **States:**
  - Filled: all steps done or passed.
  - Some "Para después".
  - Loading and the generic retry: the shared Estados.
- **PRD change:** the screen is new. It is not shown after an import: the Onboarding ends there and the after-import notice takes over.
- **Gap for UI Lead:** the prototype has no Listo screen. It must be drawn from the existing row and button components, in light and dark themes and at 320 px and 130 % text.

### Inicio and InicioCompu

- **Built from:** InicioPrimeraVez and InicioPrimeraVezCompu for the first-run Inicio (with Inicio, InicioOscuro and InicioCompu for the normal case). The notice "Seguí con la configuración" takes the slot of the "Para arrancar" checklist card.
- **Notice content:** "N de 6 pasos" and "Falta: <short step name>". It sits at position 1 on phone and on InicioCompu. It cannot be dismissed, and tapping it opens the Onboarding at the resume step.
- **States:**
  - Some steps done.
  - Required steps missing.
  - Gone once the Onboarding is over.
  - Import: the after-import notice takes over, and this one never shows.
  - Loading and the shared Estados.
- **PRD change:**
  - While the Onboarding is not over, the checklist and "Arrancá por las variedades" do not show.
  - "+ Nueva venta" stays and says in words what is missing.
  - "Mandar catálogo" is disabled with its reason.
- **Gap for UI Lead:** the notice is not drawn on Inicio or InicioCompu. It must be drawn from the existing card, in the InicioPrimeraVez slot. The prototype has no variant with the notice and no disabled "Mandar catálogo".

### NuevaVenta and NuevaVentaCompu (opened from Listo)

- **Built from:** NuevaVenta and NuevaVentaCompu as they are.
- **PRD change:** none visually. Only the behaviour: when Nueva venta is opened from "Listo", closing it or finishing returns to Inicio.

### Estados

- **Reused as is:** "Sin conexión", the generic retry, loading with no figures, and "Eso ya no está.". They apply to every step and to "Listo", with the typed data kept. No new state is drawn.

## UI Lead

_The system denied 1 write attempt: this role writes nothing; reproduce by reading the code, the task documents and the existing tests, or by running the declared checks. A denial here is not a syntax problem._

### Comparisons

No Onboarding screen is built yet, and `screens.json` lists only the existing NegocioNuevo, InicioPrimeraVez and InicioPrimeraVezCompu. So there is no side-by-side comparison for the new screens. I judged the written design against the prototype (`docs/product/design-system/pantallas`) on the text.

**Step container (steps 1–6)**
- The single centred column of the getting-in screens (NegocioNuevo / AuthPage), with no bar and "Cerrar" from the form sheets, matches the PRD's form-or-sheet kind.
- The fixed parts and the Estados states are all present.
- *"Paso N de 6"*: defined only as text. The PRD asks for words **and** shape. The shape still has to be drawn from tokens and never told by colour alone. Observation.
- *Dirty state*: the discard confirmation covers "Atrás" and "Cerrar" but not "Saltar" or "Saltar el resto" (PRD 14). Observation.

**Entry: NegocioNuevo**
- Unchanged screen; only where its submit lands changes. Correct, and it fixes the flow's mistaken Invitacion. No finding.

**Step 1: variety with today's price**
- VariedadNueva's name field plus PrecioNuevo's fields, with "Rige desde" set to "Hoy", all in one container. This is built from existing fields.
- The partial-save state (variety saved, price refused) is well covered.
- *Loop*: say explicitly that "Agregar otra variedad" appears only after step 2, and that step 2's selector arrives preselected in the loop. Observation.

**Step 2: opening stock**
- EntradaStock's frame with the variety and Stock inicial preselected, "Opcional para stock inicial", and the $0 line. Faithful, and it covers PRD 16. No finding.

**Step 3: header and footer**
- ConfiguracionMensaje with "Guardar" and "Dejar así". Faithful. No finding.

**Step 4: Telegram bot**
- ConfiguracionIntegraciones' fields. On the label question, keep the owner's "Enviarme una prueba", since the step reuses its owner unchanged.
- *Success / Hecho*: missing PRD 18's line "Cuando quieras, publicalo desde Variedades → Mandar catálogo". Observation.

**Step 5: partner**
- ConfiguracionUsuarios' link row with "Copiar". Faithful.
- *Title*: the PRD copy "Enviale el link a tu socio" is not placed anywhere. Observation.

**Step 6: expense categories**
- ConfiguracionCategorias with its refusal. Faithful.
- *"Saltar"*: described as leaving the step not passed yet leading to "Listo". That contradicts "Listo" only appearing once every optional step is passed. The screen after that tap needs resolving: either "Saltar" passes the step with the defaults kept (PRD 20), or it is not drawn. Observation.

**Listo**
- The centred column with rows and two buttons, no celebration. All required content and states are there.
- *Lines*: name the InicioPrimeraVez checklist row as the component, with the state carried in its words. Observation.

**Inicio / InicioCompu**
- The notice takes the "Para arrancar" card's slot (#F2EEE6, radius 22) in InicioPrimeraVez and InicioPrimeraVezCompu. Recognisably the same design.
- *Notice*: it should be drawn as one tappable card, not a checklist. Observation.
- *Disabled "Mandar catálogo"*: the "gap" is mis-stated. That state already exists in Variedades.dc.html ("Cargá una variedad con precio y su stock"). Reuse it. Observation.

**NuevaVenta / NuevaVentaCompu and Estados**
- Reused as they are. No finding.

### Verdict

`approved`: every screen and state the PRD requires is present and can be built from the product's own components. The remaining findings are under-specified states and copy, all observations.

## PM: consolidation

### Verdict

`approved`: both the flow and the screens design were approved. Every finding still lets the job be completed, so all of them go to the pull request as observations.

### PRD

#### Job to be done

**When** I have just opened my business and it is still empty, **I want** to be walked through what it needs, one short step at a time, with defaults already filled in, **so I can** record my first sale the same day without hunting through Configuración, Variedades and the catalog screen.

Success criteria:
1. **Fast, from the phone**: from accepting the invitation to a business ready for its first sale (a variety priced today, with opening stock) in under 5 minutes, from the phone alone.
2. **Only the sale is required**: nothing is mandatory beyond a priced variety and its opening stock. Every other step can be skipped in one tap and says where it can be done later.
3. **Nothing lost**: leaving halfway loses nothing already saved. Resuming picks up at the first step that is neither done nor passed.
4. **One fact, one place**: every step saves the same setting, under the same rules and refusals, that Configuración or the variety's page saves. The Onboarding holds no copy of a setting and no setting of its own.
5. **Simple and attractive, inventing nothing**: built from design-system elements and judged by eye on phone and computer. No placeholder data, no fake figures, and no step for a setting that does not exist yet.

Left out on purpose:
- a business-name step;
- per-variety catalog attributes and the low-stock threshold;
- Apariencia;
- #85's pricing switches, until they exist;
- a "run it again" entry;
- "Terminar por ahora";
- usage tracking;
- illustrations, animation, marketing copy, new colours or new components.

#### Flow: set up a new business (main flow)

- **Starts**: the seller accepts a new-business invitation on **NegocioNuevo** ("Creá el negocio") and lands on step 1. The flow named Invitacion here; Invitacion is the join screen, and the screens design corrects it (observation). A join invitation still lands on Inicio.
- **Every step** is a full-screen Onboarding: one centred phone-width column, no bar, no "+ Nueva venta". It shows "Paso N de 6", "Atrás", "Cerrar", "Saltar" (optional steps) and "Saltar el resto" (optional steps, once both required steps are done).
- **The steps**:
  1. Variety with today's price (required): name and today's price, then save, about 3 touches plus typing. The flow offers "Agregar otra variedad" or "Seguir" afterwards. Offering it after step 1 is an observation; the PRD's loop is name, price and opening stock.
  2. Opening stock (required): quantity, then save, about 3 touches. Then "Agregar otra variedad" or "Seguir". Progress stays on steps 1–2 while varieties are added.
  3. Header and footer (optional): "Dejar así" (1 touch), typing then "Guardar", or "Saltar".
  4. Telegram bot (optional): Buscar mi chat, then the test send. A successful test passes the step, and so does "Saltar" (1 touch).
  5. A partner (optional): create a link (1–2 touches), then "Seguir" (1 touch), or "Saltar".
  6. Expense categories (optional): "Seguir" (1 touch). Whether this step has a "Saltar" is an open observation.
- **Ends**: the tap that passes step 6, or "Saltar el resto", shows "Listo". It never ends before both required steps are met. If an import loads the business, the Onboarding is over. Per PM adjustment 1, "Listo" is never shown in that case and the after-import notice takes over. The flow text said otherwise (observation).
- **Touch count**:
  - Required steps only: about 10–11 touches plus typing, including "Saltar el resto" and "Cargar mi primera venta".
  - All six steps: about 16–20 touches plus typing.
  - Both are well within the 5 minutes.
- **What can go wrong, and how the seller recovers**:
  - Unsaved changes on leaving: a confirmation asks before discarding. An untouched step leaves at once.
  - Owner refusals: shown inline, keeping what was typed.
  - Test send or Buscar mi chat fails: its own reason shows. Retry, or "Saltar".
  - Link creation is refused: retry, or "Saltar".
  - "Sin conexión" or the generic retry: what was typed is kept.
  - "Eso ya no está.": go back to the step and choose again.
  - A partner changes data: the required steps follow the data on the next load.

#### Flow: leave and resume

"Cerrar" goes to Inicio and keeps the resume point. That is 1 touch, plus 1 to confirm a discard. Inicio, on the phone and on InicioCompu, shows "Seguí con la configuración" with "N de 6 pasos" and "Falta: …". It cannot be dismissed. One tap opens the first step that is neither done nor passed. Leaving and coming back takes 2–3 touches.

#### Flow: skip optional steps

- "Saltar" passes the current step in 1 touch (plus 1 for a discard confirmation).
- "Saltar el resto" appears only once both required steps are done. It passes every remaining optional step and goes to "Listo".
- "Atrás" goes back to an earlier step.
- Skipped steps show in "Listo" as "Para después — en Configuración → <grupo>".

#### Flow: first sale after setup

- From "Listo", "Cargar mi primera venta" opens Nueva venta, which is unchanged. Closing it or saving returns to Inicio. "Ir a Inicio" is the alternative.
- While the Onboarding is not over:
  - neither the checklist nor "Arrancá por las variedades" shows;
  - "+ Nueva venta" says in words what is missing;
  - "Mandar catálogo" is disabled with its reason.

#### Who gets it, and when

1. Only a business just opened by a new-business invitation, from the moment the invitation is accepted.
2. It is never offered to:
   - an imported business: once the import loads a business, the Onboarding is over for it, the notice never shows, "Listo" is never shown and the after-import notice takes over;
   - a business a user joined;
   - a business that existed before the Onboarding shipped, which keeps the first-run checklist exactly as it is.
3. There is no "run it again".
4. Its state belongs to the business. A partner who joins midway sees the same notice and can carry on. No roles are added.

#### Relation to the first-run checklist

5. For a brand-new business the Onboarding fully replaces the checklist; the two never both apply.
6. While the Onboarding is not over, neither the checklist nor "Arrancá por las variedades" shows. "Arrancá por las variedades" takes over once it is over.
7. The side rules stay, tied to the data:
   - with no variety priced today, "+ Nueva venta" says in words what is missing;
   - while nothing qualifies for the catalog and the two required steps are unmet, "Mandar catálogo" is disabled with its reason in words.
8. navigation.md names the Onboarding as the first-run element for a brand-new business, next to the checklist and the after-import notice.

#### Entry and the Inicio notice

9. Accepting a new-business invitation lands in the Onboarding. A join invitation lands on Inicio. This is the only time the Onboarding opens by itself.
10. While it is not over, Inicio shows "Seguí con la configuración" in position 1, on the phone and on InicioCompu.
    - Progress in words ("2 de 6 pasos", counting steps done or passed).
    - The resume step ("Falta: stock inicial").
    - It opens the Onboarding at the resume step.
    - It cannot be dismissed, and partners see it too.
11. A seller who wants it gone sooner finishes the two required steps and taps "Saltar el resto".

#### The screens

12. A full screen of the form-or-sheet kind: no bar or sidebar, no "+ Nueva venta". One centred phone-width column on every viewport.
13. Every step shows:
    - a short title and one sentence on why it matters;
    - its fields and one primary action;
    - "Paso 2 de 6" in words as well as shape, never by colour alone;
    - "Atrás";
    - "Saltar", on optional steps;
    - "Saltar el resto", on optional steps once both required steps are done;
    - "Cerrar", which lands on Inicio and keeps the resume point.
14. Leaving a step by "Cerrar", "Atrás" or "Saltar" with something unsaved asks before discarding. An untouched step leaves at once.
15. Inside the steps, the owners' own behaviour applies unchanged.

#### The six steps, in order

| # | Step | Required | Saves |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | A variety with today's price | Yes | A variety and a price entry effective today, under Variedades' rules |
| 2 | Its opening stock | Yes | A stock entry: Stock inicial, grams, today, optional total cost |
| 3 | Catalog header and footer | No | Header and footer, shown with their defaults |
| 4 | Telegram bot | No | Token, channel chat, own chat (Buscar mi chat) and the test send, as Integraciones |
| 5 | A partner | No | One join link per tap, to copy or share |
| 6 | Expense categories | No | Add, rename and remove, as Categorías de gastos |

16. Step 2 says plainly that with no cost the lot costs $0.
17. After a variety and its opening stock, the seller sees "Agregar otra variedad" or "Seguir".
    - There is no cap, and each variety is saved the moment its step is saved.
    - One variety is enough to continue.
    - Progress stays on steps 1–2.
18. Step 4 ends at the saved bot and says "Cuando quieras, publicalo desde Variedades → Mandar catálogo".
19. Step 5 reads "Enviale el link a tu socio" and never waits for the partner to join.
20. A skipped step says where it can be done later. Skipping step 3 or step 6 keeps the defaults.

#### Done and passed

21. Required steps are worked out from the data.
22. Optional steps are passed by one explicit tap, and the pass is remembered for the business:

| Step | Passed by |
|---|---|
| 3 | "Guardar" or "Dejar así" |
| 4 | A successful test send ("Hecho"), or "Saltar". A failed test shows its reason and saves nothing |
| 5 | "Seguir" once a link exists, or "Saltar" |
| 6 | "Seguir" only. Category edits save at once but never pass the step |

23. Data that already exists shows "Hecho" and counts as passed.
24. Resume lands on the first step neither done nor passed.
25. Leaving keeps everything saved and the resume point.
26. "Saltar el resto" passes every remaining optional step.
27. The Onboarding never ends before both required steps are met.

#### "Listo"

28. The tap that passes the last optional step, or "Saltar el resto", ends the Onboarding and shows "Listo". It is then over for good.
29. One line per step: "Hecho", "Hecho · 2 variedades" (a count, never an amount), or "Para después — en Configuración → <grupo>" / "en Variedades".
30. Actions: the primary "Cargar mi primera venta" and the secondary "Ir a Inicio". No celebration.
31. Nueva venta opened from "Listo" goes back to Inicio, whether closed or saved.

#### Empty states and errors

32. Step 1 starts empty. No step shows a figure, a placeholder or sample data.
33. Refusals belong to each owner: inline, keeping what was typed. "Sin conexión" and the generic retry apply as everywhere.
34. A record that is gone follows "Eso ya no está.". Data a partner created shows "Hecho" on the next open.

#### Design (screens as validated)

35. Design-system elements only, in both themes. Anything missing (the progress indicator, the step layout, "Listo", the Inicio notice) is drawn from tokens before it is built.
36. Rioplatense Spanish with vos. Nothing clipped at 320–430 px or at 130 % text. Controls at least 44 px tall, inputs at least 16 px.
37. Every step and "Listo" go into `scripts/design/screens.json`. `compare.sh` runs before and after, phone and computer.

**Step container (steps 1–6).** The single centred column of the getting-in screens (AuthPage, ADR-0025), with "Cerrar" from the form sheets and the owner's screen content as the body.
- Fixed parts: "Paso N de 6" on top; "Atrás" (hidden on step 1) and "Cerrar"; title and reason; the owner's fields; the primary action at the bottom; "Saltar" on steps 3–6; "Saltar el resto" once steps 1–2 are done.
- States:
  - untouched: leaves at once;
  - dirty: the existing discard confirmation (it must cover "Saltar" too, observation);
  - already done: "Hecho" with the data, primary "Seguir";
  - the Estados versions of loading (no figures), "Sin conexión", the generic retry and "Eso ya no está.".
- Gap: the step layout and the indicator's shape are drawn from tokens.

**Entry: NegocioNuevo.** The screen is unchanged. On success it lands on step 1 instead of Inicio.

**Step 1.** VariedadNueva's name plus PrecioNuevo's Precio de lista, optional Precio por cantidad and Mínimo en gramos, with "Rige desde" fixed to "Hoy".
- States: empty, filled, saving, inline refusals, done, and the shared Estados.
- Partial save: the variety is saved and the price is refused. The step stays, the name shows as saved, and the price carries the refusal. A retry saves only the price.
- No "Saltar".
- Gap: the combined composition is new.

**Step 2.** EntradaStock, with the step-1 variety and "Stock inicial" preselected. "Costo total" reads "Opcional para stock inicial", with the $0 line under it.
- States: empty, filled, saving, refusal, done, then "Agregar otra variedad" / "Seguir", and the shared Estados.
- No "Saltar".

**Step 3.** ConfiguracionMensaje, with Encabezado and Pie holding their defaults. Actions: "Guardar" and "Dejar así", plus "Saltar" and "Saltar el resto".
- States: default, edited, saving, error, passed ("Hecho"), and the shared Estados.

**Step 4.** ConfiguracionIntegraciones: Token del bot, Chat del canal, Tu chat, Buscar mi chat and the test button.
- States: empty, filled, testing, success (passes), failure (owner's wording, typed data kept), already configured ("Hecho"), and the shared Estados.
- Open: the button label, "Probarlo" or "Enviarme una prueba" (observation).

**Step 5.** ConfiguracionUsuarios: "Invitá a un socio", and the link with "Copiar".
- States: no link (invite is primary), link created ("Seguir" is primary), copied (owner's toast), error, "Hecho", and the shared Estados.

**Step 6.** ConfiguracionCategorias: the defaults, Renombrar, Eliminar, "+ Categoría", and the refusal "No se puede borrar: todavía tiene gastos. Podés renombrarla."
- States: defaults, edited, adding, renaming, refusal, and the shared Estados.
- "Seguir" is the only pass. There is no "Saltar el resto".
- Open: whether this step has a "Saltar" at all (observation).

**Listo.** The centred column, with one existing list row per step (the InicioPrimeraVez checklist row is suggested) and two buttons.
- States: all done, some "Para después", loading and the generic retry.
- Never shown after an import.
- Gap: drawn in both themes, at 320 px and at 130 % text.

**Inicio / InicioCompu.** Based on InicioPrimeraVez and InicioPrimeraVezCompu. The notice takes the slot of the "Para arrancar" card, as one tappable card reading "N de 6 pasos" and "Falta: <step>".
- It cannot be dismissed and goes once the Onboarding is over.
- After an import, the after-import notice shows instead.
- The disabled "Mandar catálogo" reuses the existing state in Variedades.
- Gap: the notice itself is drawn.

**NuevaVenta / NuevaVentaCompu.** Unchanged. When opened from "Listo", they return to Inicio.

**Estados.** Reused as they are on every step and on "Listo".

#### Privacy and isolation

38. The Onboarding's state carries `business_id`.
    - Business removal exports and deletes it, and the dry run counts it.
    - It joins the isolation sweep and the gated-route test.
    - Another business's state answers 404 or is absent.
    - Nothing about it reaches logs, the console or browser storage.
    - Nothing is stored about time taken or where a seller left.

#### Metrics

There is no telemetry.
- **Under 5 minutes (criterion 1)**: an end-to-end phone run, from the invitation to Nueva venta opened from "Listo", in under 5 minutes.
- **Nothing lost (criterion 3)**: an end-to-end resume run, plus a resume matrix, 100 % passing: required steps from the data, passes remembered, "Hecho" for existing data, no end before the required steps.
- **One fact, one place (criterion 4)**: a value saved in each step reads the same in its usual place, and the reverse.
- **Only the sale is required (criterion 2)**: every optional step skips in one tap; "Saltar el resto" shows only after the required steps; "Listo" names where each skipped step lives.
- **Who gets it**:
  - a brand-new business gets it;
  - imported (even one that landed in it first), joined and pre-existing businesses never do;
  - the checklist never shows with it.
- **Inicio and chrome**:
  - the notice sits on top, cannot be dismissed, and keeps its count on steps 1–2 while varieties are added;
  - neither "Arrancá por las variedades" nor the checklist shows with it;
  - no bar and no "+ Nueva venta" in the Onboarding;
  - Nueva venta opened from "Listo" returns to Inicio.
- **Isolation**: the sweep, the gated-route test and business removal cover the state.
- **Design (criterion 5)**: every screen in `screens.json`, `compare.sh` before and after, and a by-eye pass in both themes.
- **Business metrics**, read by the operator from existing records only: the share of new businesses with a priced variety and a stock entry, and the share whose first sale falls on their opening day.

### Questions for the human

None. Neither lead marked a finding as blocking, and every job can be completed as designed.

### Observations on the design

- **Main flow, start**: the flow names Invitacion. The new-business entry is NegocioNuevo, which the screens design already uses.
- **Step 1**: the flow says neither whether name and price are one save or two, nor what happens when the price is refused. The screens design adds a partial-save state.
- **Step 1, "Agregar otra variedad"**: offering it after step 1 allows varieties with no stock. Show it only after step 2, with step 2 preselected inside the loop.
- **Step 2**: the flow leaves out the preselected variety and Stock inicial, and the $0 line. The screens design includes them.
- **Step 4**: the touch count leaves out typing the token and the channel, and the trip to Telegram. The flow also does not say what survives a reload during that trip.
- **Step 4, label**: "Probarlo" (PRD) or "Enviarme una prueba" (the owner's screen, and the UI Lead's preference).
- **Step 4, after success**: the line "Cuando quieras, publicalo desde Variedades → Mandar catálogo" is not placed.
- **Step 5**: "Enviale el link a tu socio" is not placed.
- **Step 6, "Saltar"**: the documents disagree. The flow has none, the PRD has one on every optional step, and the screens design has one that leaves the step not passed yet leads to "Listo".
- **End, a partner undoes a required step during steps 3–6**: the flow does not say where the tap that passes step 6 lands.
- **End, import**: the flow shows "Listo". Per the PM, "Listo" is never shown and the after-import notice takes over.
- **Progress indicator**: it is given only as text. The shape still has to be drawn from tokens, never by colour alone.
- **Discard confirmation**: it must also cover "Saltar" and "Saltar el resto".
- **Not drawn yet**: the step layout, "Listo" and the Inicio notice. Design draws them before build.
- **Listo**: use the InicioPrimeraVez checklist row for its lines.
- **Inicio notice**: draw it as one tappable card, not a checklist.
- **Disabled "Mandar catálogo"**: the state already exists in Variedades; reuse it.

## Interview: Tech Lead efímero→Tech Lead

### Round 1: questions

### Round 1 - the solution's shape

Concrete failure of the obvious plan ("put an `onboarding` flag on the business and compute everything in `GET /panel`"): the flag cannot tell a pre-existing business from a new one unless a migration backfills it, and `BusinessOpening` (ADR-0006) is the only seam that sees the new-business branch of `accept_invitation`. A join invitation must land on Inicio, a new-business invitation in the Onboarding. If the seed lives anywhere but that port, one of the two paths silently gets the wrong landing. A second failure: step 1 "done" derived from "has a price today" can flip back to not done after a price is removed, which contradicts "never ends before the required steps".

❓ **Q1** - **Where does the Onboarding live?**: A new `app/onboarding/{domain,application,adapters}` hexagon (registered in `HEXAGON_ROOTS` and `TABLE_MODULES`) versus state inside accounts or results. Results already composes the panel from owners' ports (ADR-0013). Accounts owns the business and its invitations. If the state goes in either, that capability starts to know about varieties, stock and catalog, against ADR-0006.
➡️ A dedicated `onboarding` hexagon. It declares its own ports (one per owner: varieties, stock, catalog, pinned catalog, invitations, expenses) and is wired only in `app/main.py`. Results asks it one port question ("is the Onboarding showing, and where do we resume?") and does not recompute it.

❓ **Q2** - **How is a pre-existing business told apart, and who seeds a new one?**: A migration backfill (mark every existing business as "over") versus an absence-of-row rule. Absence is dangerous: a business created through a path that does not seed (CLI, import, a second `BusinessOpening` listener) would look pre-existing and never get the checklist. Conversely, a pre-existing business with no row would be shown the checklist.
➡️ An explicit per-business state row with `business_id`. The migration backfills "pre-existing" rows with a test that fails when any business lacks one, and the seed is a `BusinessOpening` listener, so create-business and accept-new-business-invitation both go through it. Test both landings: new-business invitation to the Onboarding, join invitation to Inicio.

❓ **Q3** - **What is stored, and what is derived?**: Steps 1–2 are "done" from data (price today, any stock entry). Optional steps are "passed". If "done" is only derived, it can flip back (price removed, stock emptied). If everything is stored, it drifts from the owners' data, and a record made outside the Onboarding never ticks a step.
➡️ Store only what data cannot say: passes, "closed", "over". Latch required steps as done on first success, in the same transaction as the owner's use case (single commit through `Transaction`). "Over" is a stored fact decided on the server. The step count and resume step come from the server. How do you guarantee the latch when the owner's use case lives in another hexagon?

❓ **Q4** - **Import versus Onboarding**: ADR-0018 eligibility is per-owner `has_records()`. Passes must not count as records, and a successful import must end the Onboarding. Today the import adapter reaches owners only through ports. What stops a half-done Onboarding (a step-1 variety already created) from making the import ineligible, or an import landing while a step form is open and the guard still showing it?
➡️ The state table is excluded from every `has_records()`, and the import's success path ends the Onboarding through a port the import declares. Test: import during a half-done Onboarding, in both orders, and the next `GET /panel` reports "over".

❓ **Q5** - **Business removal and the new table (ADR-0015)**: The state table carries `business_id`, so it is exported and deleted automatically, but the suite fails a table without a `name` or declared label, and PM said it is counted in the dry run. What do you declare as its label, and do you want it exported as data or counted but withheld, as with `reset_link` in ADR-0019?
➡️ Export and delete with a declared label, as PM decided. Add the removal test for it, plus entries in `test_business_isolation.py`, `test_gated_routes.py` and the migrations ownership test.

❓ **Q6** - **Subtask split and test seams**: The slice has the hexagon and migration with backfill, the `BusinessOpening` seeding and landing change, owner-port adapters for six steps, the panel integration, the import integration, and the front (progress indicator, six step screens, leave-guard, routes in `screens.json`, `contract.json` codes). That is well over four production modules plus tests plus an ADR in one piece.
➡️ Split in this order, each with its own seam:
1. backend state, migration and seeding (fake ports);
2. step adapters, one per owner, with owner use cases reused;
3. panel and import integration;
4. front (after Design draws the progress indicator; `compare.sh` before and after).

Docs (accounts.md, catalog.md, navigation.md, results.md, CONTEXT.md) go with the subtask that changes the behaviour. Which of these can run in parallel, and where is the fake-port seam for 2?

### Invariants proposed
- Every Onboarding step calls its owner's existing use case and returns its refusal codes, with no rule restated in the onboarding hexagon.
- The Onboarding state table carries `business_id`, follows the naming convention, has a declared label for ADR-0015 and is never counted by any owner's `has_records()`.
- A use case that changes Onboarding state commits once through the `Transaction` port, together with the owner's write when it is a step.
- The business is always read from `CurrentBusinessId`, never from a request body or path parameter, in every Onboarding route.

### Round 1: answers

### Before the answers: what the code says

- **Seams.** `BusinessOpening` is already a composite (`app/main.py`, `CompositeOpening(ExpenseCategoriesOpening, CatalogHeaderFooterOpening)`). `accept_invitation` calls it only on the new-business branch, before its single commit. This is the one place a business is created.
- **Existing screens the steps can reuse.** Inicio composes notices from routes other than `/panel`: the pinned-catalog notice reads `GET /pinned-catalog/status`. Every owner screen a step needs already exists in `web/src/screens/` (`varieties/VarietyNew`, `PriceForm`, `EntradaStock`, `settings/CatalogMessageGroup`, `IntegrationsGroup`, `UsersGroup`, `ExpenseCategoriesGroup`).
- **Three things the PRD assumes exist but the code does not have:**
  - the first-run checklist (`web/src/screens/Variedades.tsx`: "the first-run checklist … has no source in the app yet");
  - the after-import notice;
  - the "+ Nueva venta says what is missing" rule.

  I have sent this to PM (below). None of the decisions here depends on the answer, except the scope of the front subtask.

**Q1**: **Accepted:** a dedicated `app/onboarding/` hexagon, added to `HEXAGON_ROOTS` and `TABLE_MODULES` in the diff that creates it. I overrule the second half of the recommendation: results does not ask it anything.
- `/panel` stays as it is. The Onboarding answers its own `GET /onboarding`, and Inicio reads it the way it already reads `GET /pinned-catalog/status`.
- This keeps results free of an Onboarding port and keeps ADR-0013's statement-count test unchanged.
- The Onboarding screen needs the same answer anyway, so one route serves both.

The ports are read-only questions the onboarding hexagon declares in `application/ports.py`, each implemented in its owner's `adapters/` and wired in `app/main.py` (ADR-0006):
- varieties: is a variety priced today, and how many varieties are there;
- stock: is there any stock entry, and how many;
- pinned catalog: are bot settings saved;
- accounts: how many users the business has.

Onboarding never writes an owner's data (see Q3 and Q6). It has no write port to any owner.

**Q2**: **Overruled in part.** The seed is a `BusinessOpening`, as recommended: `OnboardingOpening`, added to `CompositeOpening` in `app/main.py`. It inserts the business's `onboarding` row in the same transaction that opens it. There is no backfill, for two reasons:
- **The row exists exactly when the business was offered the Onboarding.** A pre-existing business has no row and never sees it; a joined user's business is a pre-existing business.
- **`accept_invitation`'s new-business branch is the only creator of a business.** The import does not create one; it takes a business id. So "a path that does not seed" does not exist today. If a future path forgets to seed, it fails safe: no Onboarding, never a wrong one.

A backfilled "pre-existing" row would be a third state that encodes nothing the absence does not.

**Table** `onboarding` (migration 0013, §11 names):
- `id`;
- `business_id`, not null, with `uq_onboarding_business_id`;
- `is_header_footer_passed`, `is_bot_passed`, `is_partner_passed`, `is_categories_passed`;
- `ended_at`, a nullable `UtcDateTime`;
- `created_at`.

**Landing.** The accept answer does not change.
- The front already knows the invitation's kind from the lookup. After a `new_business` accept it navigates to the Onboarding route; after `join_business` it goes to Inicio.
- The Onboarding route reads `GET /onboarding`. When that says not offered or over, it replaces itself with Inicio (navigation.md's rule for an Onboarding path).

**Tests:**
- accepting a new-business invitation creates exactly one row;
- accepting a join invitation creates none;
- the front lands on the Onboarding after a new-business accept and on Inicio after a join;
- a business with no row answers `offered: false`.

**Q3**: **Overruled.** No latch.
- The PRD settles it (items 21 and 27): required steps are *worked out from the data*. If the only price today is deleted, step 1 is not done again and resume goes back to it. That is the defined behaviour, not drift.
- The PRD's guarantee is about ending, not about done-ness: it "never ends before both required steps are met". So that is where the check goes.

**Stored:** the four passes and `ended_at`, nothing else. **Derived:** everything else, by a pure `progress(facts, passes) -> Progress` in `app/onboarding/domain/`:
- the count of steps done or passed;
- the resume step (the first neither done nor passed, in order 1 to 6);
- `can_skip_rest` (both required steps done);
- the "Hecho" of steps 4 and 5 from existing data (bot saved; a second user);
- the counts for "Listo" (varieties, stock entries).

**Why no cross-hexagon transaction is needed.** Onboarding never wraps an owner's write. Steps 1 and 2 save through the owners' own routes, and the screen re-reads `GET /onboarding` afterwards. With no latch, there is nothing to keep in step.

**Writes (each commits once through `Transaction`):**
- `POST /onboarding/passes` with `{step}`, for steps 3 to 6 only. Any other step is refused as `onboarding.step_invalid`.
- `POST /onboarding/skip-rest`.

**Ending.** Either write ends the Onboarding when, after it, every optional step is passed. In the same use case and commit, it re-reads both required facts from the owners' read ports:
- If both are met, it sets `ended_at` and answers `over: true`. The front shows "Listo" from that answer.
- If not, it refuses with `onboarding.required_steps_unmet` (422) and stores nothing.
- `skip-rest` checks the same way.
- Both codes go in `contract.json`.

A write on an Onboarding that is already over is a no-op answering `over: true`, and the front goes to Inicio. A write from a business with no row answers 404 `onboarding.not_found` (kind `gone`).

**Q4**: **Accepted, and simpler than feared.**
- **Eligibility needs no change.** It is the owners' own `has_records()`, and onboarding is not an owner in `plan_import`/`write_import`. Passes cannot count.
- **The ending.** `spreadsheet_import/application/ports.py` declares an `OnboardingEnding` port with one method, `imported(at, transaction)`, implemented in `app/onboarding/adapters/`. `write_import` calls it under the same `NoCommit`, so the end lands in the import's single commit or not at all.
- **What it writes.** `ended_at` on the row if there is one and it is not set; nothing if there is no row (a pre-existing business). "Listo" is never shown, because it is only ever shown from a pass or skip-rest answer.
- **A form open during the import.** The form's next save goes to its owner's route, under the owner's rules. A pass or skip-rest answers `over: true`, and the screen goes to Inicio.

**Tests:**
- passes recorded and no owner records: the import is accepted, and the next `GET /onboarding` reads `over: true`;
- a step-1 variety saved: the import is refused with `import.business_has_records` and the row is unchanged;
- a dry run never touches the row;
- a refused real run leaves `ended_at` null.

**Q5**: **Accepted.** `onboarding` goes into `LABEL_AS_BUSINESS` (one row per business, nothing of its own worth naming, like `catalog_header_footer` and `pinned_catalog`).
- It is exported as data, not withheld. PM decided this, and none of its columns is flagged by ADR-0015's segment rule.
- It is deleted by `business_id` through reflection, so the dry run counts it with no change to the command.

**Tests that join:**
- `tests/test_business_isolation.py`: a seeder row and `BODIES` for both writes; from another business `GET` answers `offered: false` and the writes 404;
- `tests/test_gated_routes.py`;
- `tests/migrations/test_business_ownership.py` and `test_naming.py` (no allowlist change);
- a removal test: 0 rows left, and exported count = deleted count.

**Q6**: **Overruled in shape.** Steps write through the owners' existing routes, so subtask 2 as proposed ("step adapters, one per owner") disappears. What is left on the backend is four small read adapters, one per fact, each in its owner's `adapters/`.

**The fake-port seam:**
- `progress()` is tested pure.
- The use cases are tested over fake facts ports.
- The behaviour is tested through HTTP with the real adapters, the fixtures `client` and `other_client`, and the movable clock (a price effective tomorrow does not make step 1 done today).

**Order:**
- **0. Design** draws in `pantallas/` the step layout, the progress indicator, "Listo" and the Inicio notice, with entries in `scripts/design/screens.json`. It runs **in parallel with 1**.
- **1. Backend:** the hexagon, migration 0013, `OnboardingOpening`, the four facts adapters, the three routes, the refusal codes in `contract.json`, the isolation/gated/removal coverage, and an ADR ("The Onboarding stores only passes and its end; done-ness is derived; it writes no owner data").
- **2. Import ending** (the port, the adapter, the tests). It depends on 1 and runs **in parallel with 3**.
- **3. Front,** after 0 and 1:
  - the Onboarding route and its six steps, reusing the owners' form bodies, extracted from their `SettingsGroup`/page chrome where needed (old shapes coexist: only the touched forms are extracted);
  - the leave-guard (`useLeaveGuard`), the landing after accept, the Inicio notice, "Listo" and Nueva venta returning to Inicio;
  - `compare.sh` before and after.

**Docs** go with the subtask that changes the behaviour:
- with 1: CONTEXT.md;
- with 3: accounts.md (the landing line), catalog.md (the "Mandar catálogo" line), navigation.md and results.md.

### On the proposed invariants
- **"Every step calls its owner's existing use case":** kept, and made stronger. Every step calls its owner's existing *route*, so the hexagon cannot restate a rule.
- **"State table … never counted by `has_records()`":** kept.
- **"Commits once":** kept, but onboarding's commits never include an owner's write. The one shared commit is the import's.
- **"The business from `CurrentBusinessId`":** kept. `step` in the pass body is the only input.

### Left open pending PM
- Whether this task builds the first-run checklist, the after-import notice and the "+ Nueva venta" missing-price wording. None of them exists in the code today.

### Round 1: PM answers

### Answers to the Tech Lead (round 1)

**1. This task builds neither the checklist nor the after-import notice.** Businesses that existed before the Onboarding, and imported businesses, keep exactly what Inicio shows them today:
- "Arrancá por las variedades" when no variety has a price today;
- the out-of-date pin notice.

Both elements stay defined in navigation.md and get built in their own task. Until then, their rules apply to nobody.

What this task still builds, because it is the Onboarding's own state:
- the Onboarding is never offered to a business that existed before it shipped;
- the Onboarding ends for good once the spreadsheet import loads a business. Its notice never shows again, and "Listo" is never shown.

**2. Neither side rule is added, for Onboarding businesses or anyone else.** "+ Nueva venta" and "Mandar catálogo" stay as they are today. The two rules belong to the checklist and are built with it. The interview kept them only on the belief that they already existed, so there is nothing to carry over. While the Onboarding runs, the job is still served:
- Inicio's "Seguí con la configuración" notice says in words what is missing ("Falta: …");
- Nueva venta already shows "<variety> no tiene precio para esa fecha" before saving;
- the catalog screen opens to its own empty state when nothing qualifies.

**Change to my earlier handover.** Item 5 of "For the next role" no longer applies to catalog.md. Its line ("Mandar catálogo" is disabled only while the checklist shows) stays correct as written. The accounts.md landing line still needs updating, because a new-business invitation now lands in the Onboarding.

### What changed in the definitions

- **onboarding.md**:
  - the side rules are removed;
  - it now says this capability builds neither the checklist nor the after-import notice;
  - the "Who gets it" metrics no longer expect the after-import notice.
- **navigation.md**:
  - the Onboarding section adds no side rule;
  - "Mandar catálogo" is disabled only by the checklist again;
  - First run notes that the checklist and the after-import notice are built in their own task, and the first-run matrix is split the same way.

### Round 2: questions

### Round 2 - ending, route collision, Inicio composition, front size

Concrete failures found in the answers:
- **Stuck Onboarding.** The ending fires only on `POST /onboarding/passes` or `skip-rest`. A business can reach "every step done or passed" with no further pass write, and then it never ends.
- **Route clash.** A front route `/onboarding` clashes with `GET /onboarding` under ADR-0024/0030 when the page is reloaded.

❓ **Q1** - **Who ends an Onboarding that finishes by data?**: Steps 4 and 5 are "Hecho" from data (bot saved, second user). Step 5 can complete with no action by the owner, because the partner accepts the invitation from their own session. Step 4 completes when the bot is saved through the pinned-catalog route. Your rule ends the Onboarding only when a pass or skip-rest write finds every optional step passed. A business that does 3 to 6 through the owners' routes, with no pass at all, has no ending event. The Onboarding stays offered forever, and "Seguí con la configuración" keeps showing with nothing missing.
➡️ Define the ending over "done or passed", not "passed". Run the check in one use case, `settle(business)`, which `GET /onboarding` does not call (a GET must not write). The front calls `settle` through `POST /onboarding/passes` and `skip-rest`, and also through an explicit `POST /onboarding/settle` that the screen sends after each owner save. Or, if you want no new route, say how `GET /onboarding` answers `over: true` with no write, and what stores `ended_at` then. Test the case: all optional steps done by data and no pass, and the resulting state.

❓ **Q2** - **Front path versus API path**: `GET /onboarding` and `POST /onboarding/passes` are API routes, and the front route is also an Onboarding path (navigation.md's "an Onboarding path"). ADR-0024 and ADR-0030 govern front paths that share a first segment with the API. Check the actual rule in `app/static_spa.py` before settling. A reload or deep link on `/onboarding/...` risks reaching the API handler and answering JSON or 401 instead of the SPA, and `/onboarding/passes` collides directly. Your answer says "the Onboarding route" without a path.
➡️ State the exact front path and the API prefix, and show how ADR-0030's allowlist or test treats them. If they share `onboarding`, the front takes a different first segment (for example `/setup`, English, registered in `screens.json`), or the API goes under a non-colliding prefix. Add the reload test: a hard load of the front path serves the SPA, and the API path never does.

❓ **Q3** - **Inicio composes notices from several routes**: Inicio already reads `/panel` and `/pinned-catalog/status`, and you add `GET /onboarding`. For a business in the Onboarding with no priced variety, `/panel` still produces "Arrancá por las variedades" (PM confirms it stays for everyone). The out-of-date pin notice is composed too, and "Seguí con la configuración" is a third. Three notices at once, with step 1 missing stated twice, is the failure. Notice order and precedence is set in results.md. Does it say what wins while the Onboarding is offered? If not, that is a gap to consult PM about, not for you to invent. A second failure: each added Inicio request adds a render step (a loading flash of the wrong notice) before `/onboarding` answers.
➡️ Read the notice-order paragraph in results.md and quote it in the plan. If it is silent on Onboarding versus "Arrancá por las variedades", send that to PM as a question. Settle where the precedence lives: one pure function in the front (the `Inicio` notice selector), covered by a table test over (offered, over, priced, pin out of date). Say whether Inicio waits for `/onboarding` before painting notices.

❓ **Q4** - **Front subtask size**: Subtask 3 as you wrote it holds the route, six step screens, the extraction of the owners' form bodies from seven screens (`VarietyNew`, `PriceForm`, `EntradaStock`, `CatalogMessageGroup`, `IntegrationsGroup`, `UsersGroup`, `ExpenseCategoriesGroup`), the leave-guard, the landing after accept, the Inicio notice, "Listo" and `compare.sh`. That is far more than four production modules plus tests. Extracting from owners' screens also risks changing screens that are not part of this task, where `compare.sh` is the only net.
➡️ Split it:
- 3a: shell (route, progress indicator, `GET /onboarding` client, landing after accept, Inicio notice, "Listo", leave-guard). Steps are placeholder slots.
- 3b: form extraction, one per owner screen, each pure refactor with `compare.sh` before and after and no behaviour change.
- 3c: wiring the six steps to the extracted forms.

3b items run in parallel with each other and with 1. Which extractions are really needed? For example, can steps 3 and 6 embed the existing group component unchanged?

### Invariants proposed
- A use case that sets `ended_at` re-reads both required facts through the owners' ports in the same transaction, and stores nothing when either is unmet.
- `ended_at` once set is never cleared or moved by any route, use case or migration.
- No `GET` handler in the onboarding hexagon writes to the database.
- A front step screen reuses its owner's form component and its owner's route, and holds no validation of its own.

### Round 2: answers

### PM's round-1 answers, applied

**Not in this task:**
- the first-run checklist;
- the after-import notice;
- the two side rules on "+ Nueva venta" and "Mandar catálogo";
- any change to the "Mandar catálogo" line in catalog.md.

**Still in this task, as Onboarding state:**
- a business that existed before it shipped is never offered the Onboarding (Q2 of round 1: no row);
- a successful import ends it (Q4 of round 1: the `OnboardingEnding` port).

**Docs:** the accounts.md landing line still changes in front subtask 3a.

**Q1**: **Accepted in substance.** The end is decided over "done or passed", never over "passed" alone.

Some of the stuck case cannot happen:
- Steps 3 and 6 have no done-by-data. Step 3 is passed only by "Guardar" or "Dejar así", step 6 only by "Seguir" (PRD table).
- So the last thing to resolve is always a tap on 3, 6 or "Saltar el resto", and each of those is a write.
- A business that saves the bot and gets a partner through the owners' routes, and never taps, does not end, and should not. Its resume step is 3 and the notice says "Falta: encabezado y pie". Nothing is missing that it doesn't name.

There is one real stuck case:
1. Every optional step is passed.
2. The step-1 price was deleted in the meantime, so the last optional pass is refused with `onboarding.required_steps_unmet`.
3. The seller re-does step 1 through the owner's route.
4. No onboarding write follows, so nothing ends it.

**The fix:**
- One use case, `advance(business)`, in `app/onboarding/application/`. It runs `progress()` over the owners' facts, read in the same session. It sets `ended_at` only when every optional step is done or passed and both required facts are met; otherwise it stores nothing.
- `POST /onboarding-progress/passes` and `POST /onboarding-progress/skip-rest` record their pass and then call `advance` in the same commit.
- A third write, `POST /onboarding-progress/advance`, is sent by step 2's "Seguir". That tap is the "Seguir" after a variety and its opening stock. In the stuck case above, it is the tap that ends the Onboarding and shows "Listo". In every other case it answers the progress and the front moves to the resume step.
- `GET /onboarding-progress` never writes. Its `over` is exactly `ended_at IS NOT NULL`.

On the refusal I gave in round 1:
- A pass whose `advance` finds a required step unmet is **recorded, and not refused**: the answer says `over: false`, with the resume step 1.
- `onboarding.required_steps_unmet` stays only for `skip-rest`, which the front offers only once both required steps are done. That refusal is the server's backstop.

**Tests** (HTTP, real adapters, movable clock):
- bot saved and a second user, with no pass: not over, count 4 of 6, resume step 3;
- then 3 and 6 passed: over, and "Listo" answered;
- every optional step passed and then the only price deleted: the last pass is recorded with `over: false` and resume step 1; after a new price, `advance` answers `over: true`;
- `GET` twice issues no write (a statement-count assertion with no `INSERT`/`UPDATE`).

**Q2**: **Accepted, by the API taking a different segment.** This follows the precedent ADR-0019 set for reset: front `/reset`, API `/reset-links`.

**The front owns `onboarding`:**
- `/onboarding/variety`
- `/onboarding/stock`
- `/onboarding/catalog-message`
- `/onboarding/bot`
- `/onboarding/partner`
- `/onboarding/categories`
- `/onboarding/done` ("Listo")

Each one goes into `scripts/design/screens.json`, and `onboarding` goes into `contract.json`'s `frontSegments`.

**The API is `/onboarding-progress`:** `GET`, `POST …/passes`, `POST …/skip-rest` and `POST …/advance`.

What `app/static_spa.py` does with this:
- `onboarding` is not an API first segment, so `SpaStatic` falls back to the shell for every `/onboarding/*` path.
- No entry is needed in `SHARED_FRONT_PATHS` or `SHARED_FRONT_PATTERNS`, and ADR-0024/0030 are untouched.
- The contract test (API segments ∩ `frontSegments` ⊆ shared segments) passes because the two sets are disjoint, and it fails if anyone later names an API route `/onboarding/...`.

**Tests:**
- a browser navigation (`GET`, `Accept: text/html`) to each `/onboarding/*` path answers the shell, also signed out;
- `GET /onboarding-progress` with `Accept: application/json` reaches the API (401 signed out);
- `/onboarding-progress` with `text/html` does not get the shell.

**Q3**: **No PM question: results.md settles it.** Its order paragraph says:

> 1. the first-run element of the business, at most one of: "Seguí con la configuración", while the Onboarding is not over …; the first-run checklist while it shows … Once neither applies, "Arrancá por las variedades" takes this place whenever no variety has a current price today. It never shows together with the Onboarding notice or the checklist; 2. "Tu catálogo en Telegram está desactualizado" …; 3. [after-import notice] …; 4. the tab's figures.

That gives three things:
- Slot 1 holds one element: "Seguí con la configuración" while the Onboarding is offered and not over, otherwise "Arrancá por las variedades" when nothing is priced today.
- The pin notice is slot 2 and may appear with either. It concerns a different fact and is allowed by the order.
- Slot 3 does not exist in this task.

**Where the precedence lives:** one pure front function, `inicioNotices({ onboarding, pricedToday, pinOutOfDate })`, in `web/src/screens/inicioNotices.ts`. It answers the ordered list of notices to draw. A table test covers:
- `offered` true or false, × `over` true or false (and absent), × `pricedToday` true or false, × `pinOutOfDate` true or false;
- the onboarding answer still loading.

Inicio renders only what it answers.

**No flash of the wrong notice:**
- Slot 1 draws nothing until both `/panel`, which carries today's price, and `/onboarding-progress` have answered. While either is loading, slot 1 is empty and shows no placeholder.
- The figures and the pin notice paint as they do today, each from its own query.
- If `/onboarding-progress` fails, Inicio's existing "Sin conexión" or generic retry applies; slot 1 never falls back to "Arrancá" on a guess.
- A front test drives the order of the answers (onboarding late, panel late) and checks that "Arrancá" never appears for an Onboarding business.

**Q4**: **Accepted: split into 3a, 3b and 3c.** I checked which extractions are really needed. Every owner screen wraps its body in its own chrome:
- each Configuración group renders inside `SettingsGroup` (its title, "Cerrar" to `/settings`, its own `useLeaveGuard`);
- `VarietyNew` and `EntradaStock` carry their own guard and their own navigation after save.

The Onboarding step has a different frame: "Paso N de 6", "Atrás", "Saltar", "Saltar el resto", and "Cerrar" to Inicio. Nested in it, a group would draw a second title, a second "Cerrar" and a second leave guard. **So steps 3 and 6 cannot embed their group unchanged.**

**3b, the extractions.** Each one is a pure refactor:
- The form body is extracted to a component taking `onSaved` and `onDirtyChange`; the existing screen becomes its frame plus that body.
- The existing tests pass unedited.
- `compare.sh` runs before and after, phone and computer.

The six, which run in parallel with each other, with backend subtask 1 and with Design:
1. `CatalogMessageForm`, from `CatalogMessageGroup` (step 3);
2. `IntegrationsForm`, from `IntegrationsGroup`, carrying Buscar mi chat and Probarlo (step 4);
3. `InvitePartner`, from `UsersGroup`: only "Invitá a un socio" and its copy and share (step 5). The user list stays where it is.
4. `ExpenseCategoriesEditor`, from `ExpenseCategoriesGroup` (step 6);
5. the name field of `VarietyNew` as `VarietyNameForm`. `PriceForm` is already its own component and is reused as it is (step 1).
6. `StockEntryForm`, from `EntradaStock`, with the variety and the origin given as fixed props (step 2: Stock inicial, today). This is the biggest (467 lines). It only moves the body, and no behaviour changes.

**Step 1 makes two owner writes.** It sends `POST /varieties` and then the price entry, through the owners' routes.
- A price refusal shows inline on its field and keeps what was typed. The variety already saved stays saved; varieties.md saves each the moment it is saved.
- A retry sends only the price, for the variety the step already holds.

**Subtask order:**
- **3a, the shell:** the routes, the progress indicator, the `/onboarding-progress` client, the landing after a `new_business` accept, `inicioNotices` and its slot, "Listo", the step frame with its one leave guard, "Cerrar" to Inicio, Nueva venta from "Listo" returning to Inicio, and the accounts.md and navigation.md docs. Steps are empty slots behind a step registry. It needs Design (0) and backend subtask 1.
- **3c, the wiring:** the six extracted bodies wired into their slots. Each step's primary action calls the owner's route, then the pass or advance write. It needs 3a and 3b.
- Backend subtask 2 (the import ending) still runs in parallel with 3a, 3b and 3c.

**Invariants**: all four accepted, with additions:
- **Setting `ended_at`:**
  - `ended_at` is set only by `advance` (with both required facts re-read through the owners' ports in that transaction) or by the import's `OnboardingEnding`;
  - both write it only while it is null;
  - no route, use case or migration clears or moves it.

  Ending by import does not re-read the required facts, because PM made the import end it unconditionally.
- **Writes:** no `GET` in the hexagon writes. Pass writes are idempotent, and on an Onboarding that is already over they answer `over: true` and store nothing.
- **Front:** a step screen reuses its owner's extracted form and its owner's route, and holds no validation of its own. The front never decides done, passed, resume, count or over: it renders `/onboarding-progress`'s answer.

### Round 3: questions

### Round 3 - the unfinished state, resume after a partial step, backend size

Concrete failures found in the answers:
- **Complete but not over.** There is a state in your design with no defined screen: every step done or passed, `ended_at` null.
- **Resume duplicates a variety.** Step 1 makes two owner writes. After a reload or on another device, resume lands on a blank name form, with the half-saved variety invisible to it.

❓ **Q1** - **What does the business see when everything is done but nothing has ended it?**: You described it yourself: all optional steps passed, the price deleted, the price re-entered through an owner's route such as Precios, and no onboarding write follows. `progress()` now has no step that is "neither done nor passed", so the resume step is undefined. The notice "Falta: …" has nothing to name, and tapping it has nowhere to go. The only exit is a front that happens to reach step 2's "Seguir". An equivalent case: the last optional pass is recorded with `over: false` because step 1 was unmet, and the seller never returns to the Onboarding.
➡️ Define `Progress` as total: `resume` is `None` only when `ended_at` is set. When all steps are done or passed and not over, the answer carries `resume: "finish"`, a state the front draws as the "Listo" screen with a single "Terminar" button that sends `POST …/advance`. Inicio's notice sends the seller there. Add the case to `progress()`'s pure table test and to the HTTP tests. If you prefer that the front call `advance` by itself when it sees "complete", say why a write from a render is acceptable. I do not recommend it.

❓ **Q2** - **Resume after a half-saved step 1, and which variety step 2 targets**: Step 1 sends `POST /varieties` and then the price. The variety stays saved (varieties.md).
