If the seller leaves after the first write, or the price is refused and they reload, `GET /onboarding-progress` says step 1 is not done and the front renders a blank name form. A second submit either creates a second variety or is refused by the owner's name rule. Step 2 has the same problem: it needs "the variety from step 1", and the browser keeps nothing (ADR-0026: only the theme is stored). A different device or a reload loses it. Your facts port answers "priced today" and "how many varieties", which cannot name one.
➡️ Make the varieties fact port answer an identity as well: the variety step 1 should continue (the first unpriced variety, if any) and the variety step 2 targets (the first priced variety, by creation order). The progress answer carries `{id, name}` for each. The step 1 form then opens in "price only" mode for an existing unpriced variety. Test it: a variety saved with no price, a reload, a price-only submit, and one variety in total at the end.

❓ **Q3** - **Backend subtask 1 is too big**: As listed it holds the hexagon (domain `progress`, state entity, four use cases, ports, repository, router, schemas, tables), migration 0013, `OnboardingOpening`, four owner fact adapters in four hexagons, wiring in `app/main.py`, `HEXAGON_ROOTS` and `TABLE_MODULES`, `contract.json` codes, isolation/gated/removal/naming coverage, an ADR and CONTEXT.md. That is well over four production modules plus tests plus an ADR. Also confirm the next migration number against the head under `app/migrations`; "0013" is an assumption.
➡️ Split in two:
- **1a, state:** hexagon skeleton, `onboarding` table, migration with ownership/naming tests, `OnboardingOpening` and its wiring, removal coverage (`LABEL_AS_BUSINESS`), isolation entries and the ADR.
- **1b, progress:** `progress()`, `advance`, the passes and skip-rest use cases, the four fact adapters (one per owner) and their fakes, the three writes and the `GET`, the refusal codes.

1b depends on 1a. The four fact adapters are independent of each other, so say whether you want them as separate tasks or one. The import ending (subtask 2) needs only 1a. Which of 1b's modules would you cut first if its brief runs long?

❓ **Q4** - **Which decisions get an ADR, and the size of the extractions**: Several choices here are hard to reverse: absence of the row means "pre-existing" (no backfill), the API segment `/onboarding-progress` against the front's `/onboarding`, derivation without a latch for the required steps, and the import ending through a port. You plan one ADR in 1a with a different title. Also, 3b's `StockEntryForm` extraction moves 467 lines of the biggest owner screen and risks the behaviour of every stock entry.
➡️ Write the ADR with those four decisions in the Tech Lead's words, in 1a, so later subtasks can cite it. Treat `StockEntryForm` as its own task with its own `compare.sh` run and no other extraction beside it. Say how the pure refactor is checked beyond `compare.sh`: for example, the existing tests pass unedited and the diff touches no test file.

### Invariants proposed
- The progress answer is total: `resume` is empty only when `ended_at` is set, and every other state names a step or `finish` for the front to draw.
- The Onboarding keeps no state in browser storage or client memory beyond the current history entry: the variety a step targets always comes from `/onboarding-progress`.
- An extraction subtask changes no behaviour of the owner screen it touches, and edits no existing test.

### Round 3: answers

### Checked on `c157204`
- **Migration number.** The head under `app/migrations/versions` is `0012_stock_withdrawals.py`, so the new migration is **0013**, revising 0012.
- **ADR number.** No ADR has been written in this interview yet. The highest is `0031`, so the new one is **ADR-0032** (Q4).
- **Variety order.** `variety` has no creation timestamp. "Creation order" below is id order, which the autoincrement gives.

**Q1**: **Accepted: `Progress` is total.** `resume` is empty only when `ended_at` is set. Every other state names a step, 1 to 6, or `finish`. `progress()` answers `finish` when every step is done or passed and the Onboarding is not over.

**I refuse a write from a render, as recommended.** The front never calls `advance` because of what it was shown. A reload, a second tab or a partner's device would end the Onboarding with no tap at all.

**I also do not add a "Terminar" screen.** That would be a seventh screen, with copy the PRD does not have. `finish` is drawn as the **"Listo" screen itself**, the same lines and the same two actions. Each exit sends `POST /onboarding-progress/advance` first, then goes where it goes:
- "Cargar mi primera venta" goes to Nueva venta;
- "Ir a Inicio" and "Cerrar" go to Inicio.

From the seller's side this is the PRD's own rule: the tap on "Listo" is the tap that ends it.

If `advance` answers `over: false`, a required fact became unmet again in between. The front then goes to the resume step it returns, never to Inicio.

The Inicio notice in `finish` opens `/onboarding/done`. What it says there is not in the PRD ("Falta: …" has nothing to name), so it is the one question for PM below. Until PM answers, the notice shows "6 de 6 pasos" and no "Falta" line.

Known edge: closing the app while "Listo" shows in `finish`, with no tap, leaves it unended, and the notice still sends the seller to "Listo". This happens only after a required step was undone and redone outside the Onboarding. In the normal path `ended_at` is already set by the tap that showed "Listo".

**Tests:**
- `progress()`'s table gains every combination that reaches `finish`, and asserts `resume` is never empty while `ended_at` is null.
- Over HTTP:
  1. all four optional steps are passed and the only price is deleted, so the last pass is recorded with `over: false`;
  2. a price is re-entered through `/varieties/{id}/prices`;
  3. `GET` answers `resume: "finish"`, `over: false`;
  4. `advance` answers `over: true`;
  5. `GET` answers `over: true`.
- In the front, a "Listo" in `finish` sends `advance` on each of its three exits and on nothing else; a render alone sends nothing.

**Q2**: **Accepted, with targets derived across two owners.** One owner cannot name either target, because "priced today" belongs to varieties and "has a stock entry" belongs to stock. Each port answers only its own facts:
- **Varieties port:** the business's varieties as `(id, name, is_priced_today)`, by id.
- **Stock port:** the set of variety ids with at least one stock entry, and the entry count.

`progress()` combines them, purely:
- **`price_target`:** the lowest-id variety with no current price today, or none. With one, step 1 opens in **price-only mode** for it, showing its name, with the form's `PriceForm` alone. Without one, it shows the name and price form.
- **`stock_target`:** the lowest-id variety priced today with no stock entry, or none. Step 2 always records against it. With none, step 2 is done (some entry exists) or step 1 is not.

This also makes "Agregar otra variedad" work with no client memory:
1. The new variety is saved.
2. A refused price leaves it as the `price_target`.
3. Once priced, it becomes the `stock_target`.

**Known effect:** a variety created without a price elsewhere (Variedades) during the Onboarding is picked up by step 1 in price-only mode. This is the "continue where it was left" the PRD asks for, and a new business has no other varieties.

**The answer** carries `price_target: {id, name} | null` and `stock_target: {id, name} | null`. Ids and names only: no price, grams or amount.

**Tests:**
1. A variety is saved, its price is refused, and the page reloads (a fresh `GET`). Step 1 is in price-only mode for that variety, the price-only submit succeeds, and there is exactly one variety in total at the end.
2. The same case from `other_client`'s partner session in the same business, to stand for "another device": the same target.
3. With two varieties priced and one without entries, `stock_target` is the one without entries.

**Invariant accepted:** the Onboarding keeps nothing in browser storage or client memory beyond the current history entry. Every target comes from `/onboarding-progress`.

**Q3**: **Accepted: 1a and 1b**, with 0013 confirmed.

**1a, state:**
- the hexagon skeleton, registered in `HEXAGON_ROOTS` and `TABLE_MODULES`;
- the `onboarding` table and migration 0013, with the ownership and naming tests;
- `OnboardingOpening` in `CompositeOpening`;
- `onboarding` in `LABEL_AS_BUSINESS`, with the removal test;
- the isolation seeder row;
- ADR-0032 and the CONTEXT.md lines.

**1b, progress:**
- `progress()`;
- the `advance`, passes and skip-rest use cases;
- the ports and their fakes;
- the fact adapters;
- the router with the `GET` and the three writes;
- the refusal codes in `contract.json`;
- the isolation `BODIES` and the gated-route coverage.

**Fact adapters:** there are now three, not four (varieties, stock, and pinned catalog plus accounts for steps 4 and 5). They are **one task**, inside 1b. Each is a single read of about one statement in its owner's `adapters/`, they share one shape and one wiring edit in `app/main.py`, and splitting them would make four briefs that each touch `app/main.py`.

**If 1b's brief runs long**, I cut along the domain/adapter seam: the fact adapters, the router and the HTTP tests move to a **1c**. 1b keeps `progress()`, the use cases, the ports and the fakes, tested without infrastructure. 1c depends on 1b.

**Subtask 2 (import ending)** needs only 1a: it writes `ended_at` on the row and reads nothing of progress. It runs in parallel with 1b.

**Q4**: **Accepted.**

**ADR-0032**, written in 1a: *"The Onboarding stores only its passes and its end; done-ness is derived, steps write through their owners, and its API is `/onboarding-progress`"*. It states:
1. **The row marks who gets it.** A row exists only for a business opened by a new-business invitation after it shipped, seeded by `OnboardingOpening` in the opening transaction. Absence means never offered, so there is no backfill. `accept_invitation` is the only creator of a business.
2. **Required steps are derived, never latched.** Required steps are worked out from the owners' facts on every read. `ended_at` is set only by `advance`, which re-reads both required facts in the same transaction, or by the import, and only while null. Nothing clears it. `progress()` is total, and its `finish` state is described in the ADR.
3. **Steps write through their owners.** The hexagon never writes an owner's data; its own writes are passes and the end. Targets come from the progress answer (Q2).
4. **The import ends it through a port.** It goes through the port `spreadsheet_import` declares, in the import's single commit. Onboarding state never counts for eligibility.
5. **The API takes its own segment.** The front owns `onboarding`, and the API is `onboarding-progress`, following ADR-0019's `reset`/`reset-links`. ADR-0024/0030 are untouched.

Later subtasks cite it.

**`StockEntryForm`** is its own task: no other extraction beside it, with its own `compare.sh` for `EntradaStock` and `EntradaStockCompu`.

**The other five 3b extractions** are also one task each, run in parallel. They share no file.

**What makes an extraction a pure refactor**, checked in each one's review:
- **No existing test is edited.** `git diff --name-only` against the base lists no existing test file, under `web/src/**/*.test.tsx` and `web/e2e/`. The owner screen's existing tests and the end-to-end stock run pass as they were. A new test for the extracted component goes only in a new file.
- **Static checks pass.** `tsc` strict and `oxlint` pass with nothing silenced.
- **No behaviour change is reachable.** The extracted body takes the screen's former inputs as props (variety, origin, mode). The owner screen passes exactly what it computed before, so no default changes.
- **It looks the same.** `compare.sh` runs before and after, phone and computer, judged by eye.

**Invariants:** all three accepted as written.

### Round 3: PM answers

### Answer to the Tech Lead (round 3)

**Your default stands: the notice reads "Seguí con la configuración" and "6 de 6 pasos", with no "Falta" line.** Tapping it opens "Listo".

One correction to how it ends. Reaching "Listo" ends the Onboarding. No tap on "Listo" is needed. This matches the rule already written: "over once Listo is reached; closing it by any means never brings it back." So:
- Opening "Listo" from the notice ends the Onboarding right away.
- If the seller leaves "Listo" without tapping anything, Inicio no longer shows the notice.

**The general rule**, which also covers the usual path:
- Once both required steps are done and every optional step is passed, the next screen the Onboarding shows is "Listo", and showing it ends the Onboarding.
- In the usual path, that happens on the tap that passes the last optional step.
- In this rare state, it happens when the notice is tapped.
- Until then, nothing ends the Onboarding: a price added from the variety's page does not end it.

I added this to onboarding.md: the notice's wording when every step is done, how "Listo" ends the Onboarding, and a line for this state in the resume matrix.

### Closing

### Closing summary (round 4 of 4)

Concrete failure found in this round: PM's answer contradicts the "Listo" handling you proposed in round 3. You had "Listo" ending the Onboarding on its exit taps. PM says reaching "Listo" ends it, with no tap on "Listo" needed. If the front draws "Listo" in the `finish` state and the end waits for an exit tap, a seller who leaves "Listo" without tapping still has an Onboarding that is not over. Inicio then keeps offering the notice, which is the stuck state again.

**Resolution (needs your adoption in the plan):**
- In the `finish` state, the Inicio notice is a tap target that sends `POST /onboarding-progress/advance` and then opens `/onboarding/done`. A tap is not a render, so your refusal of writes from a render holds.
- In the usual path, the pass or skip-rest write already ends it in the same commit and answers `over: true`, and "Listo" is drawn from that answer.
- "Listo" is never drawn from a state where `ended_at` is null. This replaces the "each exit sends advance" test from round 3: the test is now that "Listo" appears only after an `over: true` answer.
- If `advance` answers `over: false`, the front goes to the resume step it returns.

**Decided:**
- A dedicated `app/onboarding/` hexagon. It has read-only ports to each owner, adapters in the owners' hexagons, and wiring only in `app/main.py`.
- A row seeded by `OnboardingOpening` in `CompositeOpening` marks "offered". Absence means pre-existing, with no backfill. Migration 0013 follows 0012.
- Stored: four passes and `ended_at`. Derived: everything else, through a pure and total `progress()`. Required steps are never latched.
- Writes: `POST /onboarding-progress/{passes,skip-rest,advance}`. `GET` never writes. Steps write through the owners' routes.
- Targets (`price_target`, `stock_target`) come from the server, so resume and "Agregar otra variedad" need no client memory.
- The import ends the Onboarding through the `OnboardingEnding` port in the import's single commit. Eligibility is untouched.
- Business removal uses `LABEL_AS_BUSINESS` and exports the row.
- The front owns `/onboarding/*`. The API is `/onboarding-progress`, following ADR-0019's `reset` and `reset-links`.
- `inicioNotices` is a pure front function. It follows results.md slot 1, and slot 1 paints only after both `/panel` and `/onboarding-progress` answer.
- Split:
  - Design, in parallel with everything.
  - 1a, state, with ADR-0032 and CONTEXT.md.
  - 1b, progress, with a 1c cut if it runs long (fact adapters, router, HTTP tests).
  - 2, the import ending, needing only 1a.
  - 3b, six extractions, each its own task, with `StockEntryForm` alone.
  - 3a, the shell.
  - 3c, the wiring.
- ADR-0032 is yours to write in 1a, with the five decisions you listed. I wrote no ADR or glossary entry in this interview.

**Known risks:**
- A deep link or reload of `/onboarding/done` in the `finish` state would draw "Listo" without an `advance`. Handle it by making that path call `advance` and redraw from its answer, or by redirecting to the resume step. Pick one in 3a and test it.
- A variety created elsewhere during the Onboarding is picked up by step 1 in price-only mode. This is intended, but it may surprise a seller who also uses Variedades.
- The ending check and the owners' writes are not one transaction. A required fact can change between the read and the commit. The window is small and the result is recoverable (`finish` and `advance` again).
- The `StockEntryForm` extraction moves the largest owner screen. Its safety rests on unedited existing tests and `compare.sh`, both of which are weaker than a behaviour test of the extracted form.
- The first-run checklist and the after-import notice are not built here (PM). Until they are, pre-existing and imported businesses see only "Arrancá por las variedades" and the pin notice.

### Invariants proposed
- The front draws "Listo" only from an `over: true` answer, never from a state where `ended_at` is null.
- The Inicio notice for the `finish` state sends `advance` on tap and writes nothing on render.

## Tech Lead

_the delivery tool was called 2 times over this run before it delivered_

### Shape

The Onboarding is a new hexagon, `app/onboarding/` (ADR-0006).

**What it stores:** only what the data cannot say:
- four optional-step passes;
- `ended_at`.

**What it derives:** everything else, on every read, through one pure, total `progress()`. Its inputs are read-only facts from the owners:
- **varieties:** `(id, name, is_priced_today)` per variety, by id;
- **stock:** the variety ids that have a stock entry, and the entry count;
- **pinned catalog:** whether bot settings are saved;
- **accounts:** the business's user count.

**Where the fact ports live:** each is declared in `app/onboarding/application/ports.py` and implemented in its owner's `adapters/`. All wiring happens only in `app/main.py`.

**What it never does:** write an owner's data. Every step saves through its owner's existing route, so no rule is restated.

### Who gets it (ADR-0032, written in subtask `state`)

**Seeding.** `OnboardingOpening` joins `CompositeOpening`. It inserts the business's `onboarding` row inside `accept_invitation`'s new-business transaction.

**No row means never offered.** A pre-existing or joined business has no row, so there is no backfill. `accept_invitation` is the only creator of a business.

**Migration `0013_onboarding`** revises `0012` and creates the `onboarding` table:
- `id`;
- `business_id`, not null, with `uq_onboarding_business_id`;
- `header_footer_pass`, `bot_pass`, `partner_pass` and `categories_pass`, each nullable text with a check constraint allowing `done` or `skipped`;
- `ended_at` and `created_at`, both `UtcDateTime`.

**Why a pass records how it was given.** "Listo" must tell "Hecho" from "Para después":
- "Guardar", "Dejar así", a successful Probarlo and "Seguir" record `done`;
- "Saltar" and "Saltar el resto" record `skipped`.

### Done, passed, resume

**Required steps** are worked out from the data and never latched:
- step 1 is done when some variety is priced today;
- step 2 is done when some stock entry exists.

**Optional steps** are done or passed as follows:
- steps 3 and 6 only by their pass;
- step 4 by its pass or by a saved bot;
- step 5 by its pass or by a second user.

**`progress()` answers:**
- `count`: the steps done or passed, out of 6;
- `resume`: the first step neither done nor passed. Once none is left it is `finish` while `ended_at` is null, and none once `ended_at` is set;
- `can_skip_rest`;
- each step's state;
- the variety and stock-entry counts;
- `price_target`: the lowest-id variety with no price today;
- `stock_target`: the lowest-id variety priced today that has no stock entry.

Targets are `{id, name}` or null.

**Ending.**
- **`advance` sets `ended_at` and nothing else.** It does so only when `ended_at` is null, every optional step is done or passed, and both required facts are met. It re-reads them in the same session and commit.
- **Pass and skip-rest writes call `advance` in their own commit.** A pass whose `advance` finds a required step unmet is still recorded, and the answer says `over: false`.
- **`skip-rest` alone refuses** with `onboarding.required_steps_unmet` when a required step is unmet. This is a backstop: the front offers "Saltar el resto" only when `can_skip_rest` is true.

**The import ends it.** `spreadsheet_import` declares `OnboardingEnding`, and `app/onboarding/adapters/` implements it. It sets `ended_at` while null, inside the import's `NoCommit` single commit. Eligibility is untouched.

### API (§8: what it widens)

Every route is gated and takes the business from `CurrentBusinessId`:
- **`GET /onboarding-progress`:** never writes. With no row, it answers `offered: false`.
- **`POST /onboarding-progress/passes`** with body `{step, as}`:
  - the step must be optional and `as` must be `done` or `skipped`; anything else is refused with `onboarding.step_invalid`;
  - on an Onboarding that is already over, it stores nothing and answers `over: true`.
- **`POST /onboarding-progress/skip-rest`.**
- **`POST /onboarding-progress/advance`.**

Every write answers the same shape as the `GET`. With no row, every write answers 404 `onboarding.not_found`.

**What it widens:** only the step name and how it was passed reach the domain, for the session's own business.

**Cost:** about four statements per read, O(varieties + entries) of that business.

**Paths:** the front owns `onboarding` and the API takes `onboarding-progress`, as ADR-0019 did with `reset` and `reset-links`. ADR-0024 and ADR-0030 are untouched.

### Front

**Routes:**
- `/onboarding` is an entry that resolves to the resume step.
- The six steps live at `/onboarding/variety`, `/onboarding/stock`, `/onboarding/catalog-message`, `/onboarding/bot`, `/onboarding/partner` and `/onboarding/categories`.
- "Listo" lives at `/onboarding/done`.

Any Onboarding path goes to Inicio when the business is not offered the Onboarding or it is over.

**"Listo" is drawn only from a write that answered `over: true`.**
- The front navigates there with history state `{listo: true}`, a bare flag.
- A reload or deep link without that flag goes to Inicio.

**Inicio's notice in the `finish` state:**
- It reads "6 de 6 pasos", with no "Falta" line.
- A tap sends `advance`. With `over: true`, it opens "Listo". With `over: false`, it opens the resume step.
- A render never writes.

**`inicioNotices`** is one pure function that follows results.md's slot order. Slot 1 paints only once both `/panel` and `/onboarding-progress` have answered.

**Steps reuse their owners' forms.** The forms are extracted as pure refactors, and no existing test is edited. `StockEntryForm` is extracted alone. Targets come only from the progress answer.

### Coexistence

- **Owner screens:** they keep their chrome. Only their bodies move into exported components.
- **Existing sheets:** they keep `useState`.
- **Docs:**
  - `onboarding.md`, `navigation.md` and `results.md` already state the behaviour (PM).
  - `accounts.md`'s landing line changes in `shell`.
- **Out of scope (PM):** the first-run checklist and the after-import notice are not built here.

### Known risks

- **The ending check and the owners' writes are separate transactions.** A required fact can change between the two. The result is recoverable: the Onboarding lands in `finish`, and `advance` ends it from the notice.
- **A variety created on Variedades during the Onboarding is picked up by step 1** in price-only mode. This is intended.
- **The extractions are covered only by indirect checks:** the existing tests, left unedited, `compare.sh` and one new test per extracted component.

### Subtasks

#### The onboarding hexagon's state: table, migration 0013, seeding on a new business, removal and isolation coverage, ADR-0032

**Hexagon.** Create the `app/onboarding/` skeleton (`domain/`, `application/`, `adapters/`):
- add it to `HEXAGON_ROOTS`;
- add its tables module to `TABLE_MODULES` and `app/tables.py`.

**Domain.** An `Onboarding` entity holds:
- passes for `header_footer`, `bot`, `partner` and `categories`, each None, `DONE` or `SKIPPED` (an enum);
- `ended_at: datetime | None`.

`end(at)` sets `ended_at` only while it is None, and never moves it.

**Table.** `onboarding`, in `app/onboarding/adapters/tables.py`, created by migration `0013_onboarding.py` (revises 0012):
- `id`;
- `business_id`, not null, FK to `business`, with `uq_onboarding_business_id`;
- `header_footer_pass`, `bot_pass`, `partner_pass` and `categories_pass`, nullable strings, each with a check constraint allowing `done` or `skipped`;
- `ended_at`, a nullable `UtcDateTime`;
- `created_at`, a not-null `UtcDateTime`.

Names come from `NAMING_CONVENTION`, with `op.f(...)` in the migration (§11). There is no backfill.

**Repository.** An `Onboardings` port and `SqlOnboardings`, built for one business:
- every statement is filtered on `business_id`;
- it flushes and never commits.

**Seeding.** `app/onboarding/adapters/opening.py`'s `OnboardingOpening.opened(business_id)` inserts the row. It is added to `CompositeOpening` in `app/main.py`.

**Removal.** `onboarding` goes in `LABEL_AS_BUSINESS`, exported (not withheld).

**Isolation.** Seed one row in `tests/test_business_isolation.py`.

**ADR-0032**, "The Onboarding stores only its passes and its end; done-ness is derived, steps write through their owners, and its API is `/onboarding-progress`". It states:
1. the row marks who gets it, with no backfill;
2. required steps are derived and never latched, and `progress()` is total, including its `finish` state;
3. `ended_at` is set only by `advance` (both required facts re-read in its transaction) or by the import, only while null, and never cleared;
4. steps write through their owners, and targets come from the progress answer;
5. the import ends the Onboarding through a port it declares, in its single commit;
6. the front owns the `onboarding` segment and the API takes `onboarding-progress` (ADR-0019), leaving ADR-0024 and ADR-0030 untouched;
7. "Listo" is drawn only from an `over: true` write answer.

**CONTEXT.md:** check the Onboarding entry. Adjust it only if it contradicts.

**Tests:**
- domain unit tests for `end`;
- accepting a new-business invitation leaves exactly one row, and accepting a join invitation adds none;
- ownership, naming (no allowlist change) and schema tests;
- removal: 0 rows remain, exported count = deleted count, and the dry run counts it.

Seams: `Onboarding entity (domain)`, `POST /invitations/{token}/accept`, `business removal command (remove_business use case / CLI)`, `tests/migrations schema checks`.

Depends on: nothing.

#### progress() and the advance, pass and skip-rest use cases, over fake fact ports

**`progress()`.** A pure, total `progress(facts, onboarding) -> Progress` in `app/onboarding/domain/progress.py`. Its facts are:
- the varieties as `(id, name, is_priced_today)`, by id;
- the variety ids with a stock entry, and the entry count;
- whether the bot is saved;
- the user count.

It answers:
- `over`: `ended_at is not None`.
- `steps`, one per step 1 to 6, each `done`, `skipped` or `pending`:
  - step 1 is done when some variety is priced today;
  - step 2 is done when an entry exists;
  - steps 3 and 6 follow their pass only;
  - step 4 is done when the bot is saved or its pass is `done`;
  - step 5 is done when there are 2 or more users or its pass is `done`.
- `count`: the steps done or skipped.
- `resume`: the first pending step. With none pending, it is `finish` while `ended_at` is None, and None only when over.
- `can_skip_rest`.
- `varieties_count` and `stock_entries_count`.
- `price_target`: the lowest-id unpriced variety.
- `stock_target`: the lowest-id variety priced today with no entry.

**Use cases** in `application/`, each committing once through `Transaction`:
- **`read_progress`:** never writes; with no row it answers not offered.
- **`advance`:** reads the facts and the row in one session. It ends the Onboarding only when `ended_at` is None, every optional step is done or skipped, and both required steps are done.
- **`record_pass(step, as_)`:**
  - only optional steps with `as_` set to `done` or `skipped`; anything else raises `StepInvalid` (`onboarding.step_invalid`);
  - on an Onboarding already over, it stores nothing;
  - otherwise it sets the pass and runs the ending check, in one commit.
- **`skip_rest`:**
  - raises `RequiredStepsUnmet` (`onboarding.required_steps_unmet`) unless both required steps are done;
  - otherwise it sets `skipped` on every optional step that is neither done nor passed, and ends the Onboarding.
- **No row:** every write raises `OnboardingNotFound` (`onboarding.not_found`, a LookupError, kind `gone`).

**Ports**, declared in `application/ports.py`:
- `VarietyFacts`;
- `StockFacts`;
- `BotFacts`;
- `UserFacts`.

**Tests:**
- a pure table test of `progress()`: every step state, the count, resume order, `finish`, the targets, and resume never None while `ended_at` is None;
- use-case tests over fakes:
  - a pass ends the Onboarding;
  - a pass is recorded with `over: false` when step 1 is unmet;
  - `advance` ends it after the price returns;
  - `skip_rest` is refused or accepted;
  - a write on an Onboarding already over stores nothing;
  - `end` never moves `ended_at`;
  - an invalid step is refused;
  - a write with no row raises `OnboardingNotFound`.

Seams: `progress() (domain function)`, `read_progress, advance, record_pass, skip_rest use cases over fake ports`.

Depends on: `state`.

#### Fact adapters in the owners and the /onboarding-progress routes

**Fact adapters.** Each is built for one business:
- `app/varieties/adapters/onboarding_facts.py`: each variety's id and name, and whether it has a current price today, by id. It reuses the varieties repository's list with today's price.
- `app/stock/adapters/onboarding_facts.py`: the distinct variety ids with an entry, and the entry count.
- `app/pinned_catalog/adapters/onboarding_facts.py`: whether bot settings are saved. It reads presence only, never the token.
- `app/accounts/adapters/onboarding_facts.py`: the user count, filtered by `business_id`.

Wire them as `(Session, business_id)` factories in `app/main.py`.

**Router.** `app/onboarding/adapters/router.py`, included with `require_session`, with the business from `CurrentBusinessId`:
- `GET /onboarding-progress`;
- `POST /onboarding-progress/passes` with the bare body `{step: str, as: str}`;
- `POST /onboarding-progress/skip-rest`;
- `POST /onboarding-progress/advance`.

Every route answers:

`{offered, over, count, resume, steps: [{step, state}], can_skip_rest, varieties_count, stock_entries_count, price_target, stock_target}`

**Contract.** Add the three codes to `web/src/api/contract.json`, and add `onboarding` to `frontSegments`.

**HTTP tests** (`client` and `other_client`, the movable clock):
- A new business answers `offered`, count 0, resume step 1.
- A price effective tomorrow does not make step 1 done.
- With the bot saved through Probarlo and a second user, but no pass: count 4, resume `header_footer`. Then the passes for steps 3 and 6 end it.
- With every optional step passed and then the only price deleted:
  - the last pass answers `over: false`;
  - after the price is re-added through `/varieties/{id}/prices`, `GET` answers resume `finish`;
  - then `advance` answers `over: true`.
- An unpriced variety is the `price_target`. A price-only submit leaves exactly one variety, and that variety becomes the `stock_target`.
- A partner session in the same business sees the same targets.
- `GET` issues no write.
- With no row, `GET` answers `offered: false` and every write answers 404.

**Coverage:**
- `tests/test_gated_routes.py`;
- the isolation sweep, with `BODIES`;
- `tests/test_refusal_codes.py`;
- a browser navigation to `/onboarding/variety` gets the shell, and `/onboarding-progress` requested as JSON reaches the API.

Seams: `GET /onboarding-progress`, `POST /onboarding-progress/passes`, `POST /onboarding-progress/skip-rest`, `POST /onboarding-progress/advance`.

Depends on: `progress-domain`.

**Implementation.** `app/onboarding/adapters/import_port.py`:
- sets `ended_at` through `Onboarding.end` when the business has a row and `ended_at` is None;
- does nothing when there is no row;

**Write path.** `write_import` takes it as a keyword argument and calls it after the last writer, under the same `NoCommit`. The CLI builds it and commits once.

**Untouched:** eligibility; the dry run never calls it.

Move the stock entry form body out of `web/src/screens/EntradaStock.tsx` into an exported `StockEntryForm`. The body covers:
- date, variety with "Hoy tiene N g", grams, origin;
- total cost with "Obligatorio cuando es una compra", and note;
- the cost-per-gram preview;
- refusals and the edit confirmation;
- the owner routes.

**Props:**
- `mode`;
- `fixedVarietyId?`;
- `fixedOrigin?: 'opening_stock'`;
- `onSaved()`;
- `onDirtyChange(dirty)`.

**What EntradaStock keeps:**
- It passes exactly what it computed before, including Stock inicial preselected for a variety with no entries.
- It keeps its chrome, its leave guard and its navigation after save.

This is the only extraction in this subtask.

**Checks:**
- `git diff --name-only` lists no existing file under `web/src/**/*.test.tsx` or `web/e2e/`;
- the existing EntradaStock tests and the e2e stock run pass as they were;
- `tsc` and `oxlint` are clean;
- `compare.sh` runs before and after for EntradaStock and EntradaStockCompu.

**New test file:** with a fixed variety and origin, the form posts through the owner route and offers no choice of origin.

Seams: `StockEntryForm component (Testing Library)`, `EntradaStock screen (existing tests)`, `web/e2e stock run`.

Depends on: nothing.

Screens: `EntradaStock`, `EntradaStockCompu`.

#### Onboarding shell: routes, step frame, progress client, landing after a new-business accept, Listo and Inicio's notice

Build the shell from the design. Each step body is an empty slot behind a step registry.

**Client** (`web/src/api/onboarding.ts`):
- `GET /onboarding-progress` and the three writes;
- keys in `keys.ts`;
- frozen queries.

**Routes:**
- `/onboarding` resolves to the resume step;
- the six step paths;
- `/onboarding/done`.

All of them use chrome `none`.

**Leaving for Inicio.** Any Onboarding path goes to Inicio when the answer says `offered: false` or `over: true`. The one exception is `/onboarding/done` entered with the history state `{listo: true}`. Without that flag, `/onboarding/done` goes to Inicio.

**The step frame** (`web/src/screens/onboarding/OnboardingStep.tsx`):
- the title, the one-line why and "Paso N de 6" with the indicator;
- "Atrás";
- "Saltar" on optional steps, which sends a `skipped` pass;
- "Saltar el resto" only when `can_skip_rest`, which sends `skip-rest`;
- "Cerrar" to Inicio;
- one leave guard, fed by the slot's `onDirtyChange`.

**`afterProgress(answer)`:**
- `over: true`: open `/onboarding/done` with `{listo: true}`;
- otherwise: open the resume step.

**Listo** (`web/src/screens/onboarding/Listo.tsx`):
- one line per step: "Hecho", "Hecho · N variedades", or "Para después — en Configuración → <grupo>" / "en Variedades";
- "Cargar mi primera venta" opens Nueva venta, which returns to Inicio;
- "Ir a Inicio".

**Landing.** After a `new_business` accept (`session.ts`, `Invitacion.tsx`), go to `/onboarding`. A `join_business` accept goes to Inicio.

**Inicio's notices.** The pure `inicioNotices({ onboarding, pricedToday, pinOutOfDate })` lives in `web/src/screens/inicioNotices.ts`. It follows results.md's order:
1. slot 1: "Seguí con la configuración" while the Onboarding is offered and not over, otherwise "Arrancá por las variedades";
2. slot 2: the pin notice.

Slot 1 stays empty until both answers have arrived. On a failure, the existing "Sin conexión" or the generic retry shows.

**The notice itself:**
- It reads "N de 6 pasos" and "Falta: …", or "6 de 6 pasos" with no Falta line in `finish`.
- It cannot be dismissed.
- A tap opens the resume step.
- In `finish`, a tap sends `advance` and then calls `afterProgress`.
- A render never writes.

**Docs.** Update accounts.md's landing line.

**Tests:**
- a table test of `inicioNotices`;
- Inicio with answers arriving in either order never shows "Arrancá" for an Onboarding business;
- the `finish` notice sends `advance` only on a tap;
- "Listo" opens only after `over: true`;
- a deep link to "Listo" without the flag goes to Inicio;
- an Onboarding path of a business that is over, or never offered it, goes to Inicio;
- the accept landings;
- the frame's chrome and leave confirmation;
- "Saltar el resto" is hidden until both required steps are done;
- Nueva venta opened from "Listo" returns to Inicio;
- nothing in the console or storage;
- `compare.sh` for Inicio and InicioCompu.

Seams: `inicioNotices (pure function)`, `Inicio screen (Testing Library, fetch faked)`, `Onboarding routes and step frame (Testing Library)`, `Listo screen (Testing Library)`, `Invitacion accept flow (Testing Library)`.

Depends on: `design`, `progress-http`.

Screens: `Inicio`, `InicioCompu`, `InicioOscuro`, `OnboardingListo`.

#### Wire the six Onboarding steps to the extracted owner forms, and the end-to-end run

Fill the step slots. Each step saves through its owner's route first, then sends the onboarding write, then calls `afterProgress`.

**Step 1:**
- With a `price_target`: price-only `PriceForm` for that variety, effective today.
- Without one: `VarietyNameForm`, then `PriceForm`.
- A refused price keeps what was typed, and a retry sends only the price.
- Afterwards, re-read `GET` and go to the resume step.

**Step 2:**
- `StockEntryForm`, fixed to `stock_target` and to `opening_stock`, dated today.
- It says that with no cost the lot costs $0.
- Then "Agregar otra variedad" or "Seguir"; "Seguir" sends `advance`.

**Step 3:** `CatalogMessageForm`.
- "Guardar" saves, then sends pass `done`.
- "Dejar así" sends pass `done`.
- "Saltar" sends `skipped`.

**Step 4:** `IntegrationsForm` without the catalog link, followed by the line "Cuando quieras, publicalo desde Variedades → Mandar catálogo". A successful Probarlo sends pass `done`.

**Step 5:** "Enviale el link a tu socio" with `InvitePartner`. "Seguir" appears once a link is created and sends pass `done`.

**Step 6:** `ExpenseCategoriesEditor`. "Seguir" sends pass `done`.

The front decides nothing about progress.

**Step tests:**
- price-only mode, ending with one variety;
- step 2 targets `stock_target`;
- "Agregar otra variedad";
- each pass is sent only after its owner's save succeeds;
- a failed save sends no pass and keeps what was typed.

**End-to-end:**
- **Full run**, from a phone viewport, in under 5 minutes: accept a new-business invitation, price a variety, load its opening stock, pass every optional step, reach "Listo", open Nueva venta and land on Inicio with no notice.
- **Resume run:** leave after step 1 and resume from the notice into step 2.
- **One fact, one place:** the step-3 value reads the same in Configuración.
- **Storage:** browser storage holds only `tema`.

**Design:** `compare.sh` for every Onboarding screen.

Seams: `Onboarding step screens (Testing Library, fetch faked)`, `web/e2e onboarding runs`.

Depends on: `shell`, `extract-catalog-message`, `extract-integrations`, `extract-users-and-categories`, `extract-variety-name`, `extract-stock-entry`.

Screens: `OnboardingVariedad`, `OnboardingStock`, `OnboardingMensaje`, `OnboardingBot`, `OnboardingSocio`, `OnboardingCategorias`, `OnboardingListo`.

  - Progress is "Paso N de 6" in words plus six segments: filled = done or passed, hollow with a thicker outline = this step, faint = still to come.

## Tech Lead: review state

### Verdict

**approved**: I found nothing blocking. The brief's items, invariants 1, 4 and 7, and §11 naming of migration 0013 and OnboardingRow all hold. The targeted runs at the four declared seams pass (106 tests), and a newly invited business is still eligible for import.

### Findings

| severity | category | location | claim | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| observation | standard-other | app/onboarding/adapters/sql_onboardings.py:38 | `save()` uses a bare `assert` to refuse a business with no row. Under `python -O` the assert is stripped and the call fails with an `AttributeError` instead. The progress-domain use cases must never call it for a business with no row. | — |
| observation | standard-other | tests/onboarding/test_onboarding_domain.py:38 | `test_a_pass_knows_how_it_was_given` only restates the enum's literal values and exercises no behaviour. | — |
| observation | standard-seams | tests/architecture/test_architecture.py:36 | Adding the root to `HEXAGON_ROOTS` edits an enforcement artefact outside the declared seams. §1 and the brief both require this edit, and it widens the architecture rules to `app/onboarding` without relaxing any of them. | — |

**What I verified, by running:**
- **Targeted tests pass.** `uv run --no-project pytest -n auto tests/onboarding tests/migrations tests/business_removal/adapters/test_cli.py tests/test_business_isolation.py::test_each_business_has_its_own_onboarding_row_and_reads_no_other tests/architecture` → `106 passed`.
- **Types are clean.** `uv run --no-project mypy app tests` → `Success: no issues found in 470 source files`.
- **Nothing is silenced.** No `type: ignore` or `noqa` appears in the diff.
- **Import eligibility still holds for a new business.** A business opened by invitation now carries an `onboarding` row and is still accepted for import:
  - `uv run --no-project pytest -n auto tests/spreadsheet_import/test_cli.py -k "only_renamed or eligibility"` → `2 passed`;
  - `grep onboarding` over `app/spreadsheet_import` and every `import_port.py` → no match, so no `has_records()` reads the table (invariant 7).

**Database naming (§11)**, checked against the standard:
- All names are snake_case.
- `ended_at` and `created_at` are datetimes.
- There are no `is_` columns, and the FK column is `business_id`.
- The migration spells out every name with `op.f`: `pk_onboarding`, `fk_onboarding_business_id_business`, `uq_onboarding_business_id` and `ck_onboarding_<column>_pass`.
- The table class passes only the short check names and no `name=`.
- `test_naming.py` and its allowlist are untouched.
- I found no breach.

**Invariants:**
- **1:** `app/onboarding` imports no other hexagon and writes only its own table. Wiring happens only in `app/main.py:163`.
- **4:** `end()` sets `ended_at` only while it is None and never moves it. The domain tests cover both cases.
- **7:** `business_id` is not null and unique, `onboarding` is in `LABEL_AS_BUSINESS`, and the table is exported. `test_cli.py` asserts that exported equals deleted, that 0 rows remain, and that the dry run prints `onboarding: 1`.
- **2, 3, 5, 6, 8–12:** the diff adds no route, use case or front code, so it does not contradict the "Not touched" claim.

**Brief:**
- ADR-0032 states all seven points.
- CONTEXT.md's Onboarding entry does not contradict the plan.
- At the accept seam, a new-business accept leaves one row and a join accept adds none.

**Checks:** every product check passed on `609f3f4`. The Developer reports `web-tests` and `web-e2e` as flaky earlier, but this subtask touches nothing under `web/`.

## Developer: extract-catalog-message

### Checks

Run by the system on `6974cf9` when the Developer delivered.

| Check | Command | Result |
|---|---|---|
| `tests` | `uv run --no-project pytest -n auto` | passed |
| `lint` | `uv run --no-project ruff check .` | passed |
| `types` | `uv run --no-project mypy app tests` | passed |
| `web-types` | `npm --prefix web run typecheck` | passed |
| `web-lint` | `npm --prefix web run lint` | passed |
| `web-tests` | `npm --prefix web test` | passed |
| `web-e2e` | `npm --prefix web run e2e` | passed |

### Changes
- `web/src/screens/settings/CatalogMessageForm.tsx` (new): the header/footer form body, moved from the group. It owns the shared catalog query and its Loading, "Sin conexión" and generic-retry states. It keeps the PUT to `/catalog/header-footer`, the refusal overrides and the "Catálogo guardado" toast. It calls `onSaved()` after a successful save and `onDirtyChange(dirty)` from an effect, with dirty measured against the pair the server last accepted.
- `web/src/screens/settings/CatalogMessageGroup.tsx`: now only the `SettingsGroup` frame, its leave wording, and a `dirty` state fed by the form.

### Tests
- New `CatalogMessageForm.test.tsx`, at the `CatalogMessageForm component (Testing Library)` seam, with the API faked at `fetch`:
  - a save goes through the owner route with the typed body, shows the toast and calls `onSaved` once;
  - `onDirtyChange` reports false at first, true when typed, and false again after a save;
  - a refusal (`catalog.header_too_long`) shows inline, keeps what was typed, does not call `onSaved` and stays dirty. This is the case the form must reject.
- The existing `CatalogMessageGroup.test.tsx` is untouched and still passes (screen seam).
- Full web suite, `tsc` and `oxlint` pass.

### Decisions and assumptions
- `onSaved` is optional, because the group has nothing to do on save. `onDirtyChange` is required.
- A separate file for the form, not a second export from the group file.
- The form owns the catalog query, so its host needs no data props.
- I ran `compare.sh --only ConfiguracionMensaje,ConfiguracionMensajeCompu` once, after the change, and did not run a "before" pass. Both screens render with the same structure as the design: title, Cerrar, two fields and the save button, with the sidebar on computer. The toast and the leave sheet are design states that a static capture does not show; they are covered by the unchanged group tests.

### For the Tech Lead
- Look first at the `useEffect` in `CatalogMessageForm.tsx` that reports dirty. The group's `setDirty` is stable, so it does not loop.
- The wiring subtask must pass a stable `onDirtyChange` (e.g. `useState`'s setter, or `useCallback`), or the effect re-fires on each render.
- On Loading and error states the form reports no dirty at all, so the host's last value stays at its initial false.

No existing test file was edited, the two seam tests pass (15/15), and the new test covers the save through the owner's route, onSaved, dirty reporting and a refusal. The one shortfall is that compare.sh was run only after the change, not before; that is important, not blocking.

The JSX moved unchanged. |
| observation | standard-other | web/src/screens/settings/CatalogMessageForm.tsx:84 | onDirtyChange fires from an effect that depends on the callback, so the wiring subtask must pass a stable callback. | `useEffect(() => { onDirtyChange(dirty) }, [dirty, onDirtyChange])` |
| observation | spec-behaviour | web/src/screens/settings/CatalogMessageForm.tsx:48 | onSaved is optional although the brief lists it as a prop. This is harmless. | — |

What I verified:
- **Diff `07d1f53..6974cf9`.** It touches three web files. The only test file in it is the new `CatalogMessageForm.test.tsx`. `CatalogMessageGroup.test.tsx` is unedited (invariant 11).
- **The extracted body matches the old one line for line:**
  - the same PUT to `/catalog/header-footer`;
  - the same OVERRIDES and refusal mapping, with the same toast, ids and labels;
  - the same "Sin conexión" and generic-retry branches.
  
  The only addition is the `onSaved?.()` call.
- **The group keeps its chrome.** It still has its `SettingsGroup` frame and LEAVE wording, with `dirty` fed by `setDirty`, which is stable.
- **Seam tests.** I ran `npm --prefix web test src/screens/settings/CatalogMessageForm.test.tsx src/screens/settings/CatalogMessageGroup.test.tsx`: 2 files passed, 15 tests passed.

It renders only the body: token with Cambiar, channel chat, own chat with Buscar mi chat, Probarlo with its reasons, the "desfijalo vos en Telegram" notice and the optional "Ir al catálogo" link.

1. **The last review's single blocking item is fixed.** The test "Buscar mi chat marks the form dirty" (`IntegrationsForm.test.tsx:80-90`):
   - It renders with `{ ...EMPTY, token_saved_on: '2026-08-12' }` and types nothing.
   - It asserts "Tu chat" reads `''` and that `onDirtyChange` was last called with `false` before the click.
   - After the click it waits for `777` and asserts the last call was `true`.

   This test now checks the lookup on its own:
   - `dirty` is `tokenInput.trim() !== '' || ownChatInput !== accepted.ownChat || channelChatInput !== accepted.channelChat` (`IntegrationsGroup.tsx:117-120`).
   - The lookup handler only calls `setOwnChatInput(...)`.
   - So the `ownChatInput !== accepted.ownChat` clause is the only thing that can turn `dirty` true. If the looked-up chat stopped counting, the last call would stay `false` and line 89 would fail.

### Changes
- `web/src/screens/StockEntryForm.tsx` (new): the form body, save, edit confirmation and delete confirmation, with the owner's routes and refusal overrides, moved from EntradaStock. It reports through `onSaved`, `onDeleted`, `onGone` and `onDirtyChange`.

- Existing `EntradaStock.test.tsx` and `Loading.screens.test.tsx` pass unedited. The full vitest suite passes (104 files). e2e `flow` and `panel`, which record stock, pass on their own.

compare.sh for EntradaStock and EntradaStockCompu gave byte-identical PNGs before and after. Both show only the default state, so the dialogs are covered by the existing tests, not by the images.

The gate's full `web-e2e` run failed twice on `e2e/panel.spec.ts`. The second time (26.9 s) it was waiting for the "Variedad guardada" toast after creating a variety, a step my diff does not touch. The spec passes alone and together with `flow.spec.ts` on this head, so I took it for a flake under the full run's load. I did not re-run the full e2e.

## Developer: progress-domain

### Checks

Run by the system on `d04e79f` when the Developer delivered.

| Check | Command | Result |
|---|---|---|
| `tests` | `uv run --no-project pytest -n auto` | passed |
| `lint` | `uv run --no-project ruff check .` | passed |
| `types` | `uv run --no-project mypy app tests` | passed |
| `web-types` | `npm --prefix web run typecheck` | passed |
| `web-lint` | `npm --prefix web run lint` | passed |
| `web-tests` | `npm --prefix web test` | passed |
| `web-e2e` | `npm --prefix web run e2e` | passed |

### Changes
- `app/onboarding/domain/steps.py`: `Step` (1 to 6), `REQUIRED_STEPS` and `OPTIONAL_STEPS`.
- `app/onboarding/domain/errors.py`: `StepInvalid` (`onboarding.step_invalid`), `RequiredStepsUnmet` (`onboarding.required_steps_unmet`) and `OnboardingNotFound` (`onboarding.not_found`, a `LookupError`). Each carries its own `code`.
- `app/onboarding/domain/progress.py`: the pure, total `progress(facts, onboarding)`.
  - Value types: `Facts`, `VarietyFact`, `Target`, `StepProgress`, `Progress`.
  - It derives `over`, `steps`, `count`, `resume`, `can_skip_rest`, both counts and both targets.
  - `resume` is `None` exactly when `ended_at` is set; otherwise it is the first pending step or `FINISH`.
  - Data wins over a pass: a saved bot or a second user reads as done even after "Saltar".
- `app/onboarding/domain/onboarding.py`: `Onboarding.pass_step(step, given)`, which raises `StepInvalid` for a required step.
- `app/onboarding/application/ports.py`: `VarietyFacts`, `StockFacts`, `BotFacts` and `UserFacts` added next to `Onboardings`.
- `app/onboarding/application/facts.py`: `FactSources` and `read_facts`.
- `app/onboarding/application/use_cases.py`: `read_progress`, `advance`, `record_pass`, `skip_rest`.
  - All writes go through one `_settle`: it ends the Onboarding only when both required steps are done and every optional step is settled, then saves and commits once.
  - A write on an Onboarding that is already over stores nothing.

### Tests
- `tests/onboarding/test_progress.py` (domain seam): every step state, the count, resume order, `FINISH`, and a parametrized check that `resume` is `None` exactly when over.
  - Also covered: `can_skip_rest`, the counts, and both targets, including unsorted input and the empty cases.
- `tests/onboarding/test_use_cases.py` with `tests/onboarding/fakes.py` (use cases over fake ports):
  - the last pass ends the Onboarding in one commit;
  - a pass with an optional step pending does not end it;
  - a pass with step 1 unmet is recorded with `over: false`;
  - `advance` ends it after the price returns, and from the `FINISH` state;
  - `skip_rest` is refused with a required step unmet, and otherwise skips what is pending and ends;
  - a write on an Onboarding already over stores nothing (parametrized over all three writes);
  - `ended_at` never moves;
  - invalid steps and ways are refused with nothing stored;
  - a write with no row raises `OnboardingNotFound`.

### Decisions and assumptions
See the assumptions field. The main ones: the step is an int 1 to 6, use cases take `now` as a parameter, and a write on an Onboarding that is already over returns its progress without writing or raising.

### For the Tech Lead
- Check `_settle` in `use_cases.py` first: it is the single place that ends the Onboarding, so it carries invariants 4 and 5.
- `progress-http` must supply the fact adapters, with the method names given in the assumptions. It must also register the three errors in a translator and add them to `contract.json`; I did not, because no router exists yet.
- The table, migration and removal work from `state` were not touched.

## Tech Lead: review progress-domain

### Verdict

**approved**: Verified progress() and the four use cases against the brief and invariants 1, 3, 4, 5, 6 and 8: tests/onboarding and tests/architecture pass (114 passed), there is one commit path in _settle, ended_at never moves, and the diff has no schema change.

### Findings

| severity | category | location | claim | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| observation | spec-behaviour | `app/onboarding/application/use_cases.py:47` | `record_pass` checks the step and the way before it looks up the row. A business with no Onboarding that sends an invalid step gets `StepInvalid`, not `OnboardingNotFound`. The brief does not order the two checks, and no test covers the pair. | `use_cases.py:47-49`: `_optional_step`, then `_given`, then `_existing` |
| observation | standard-other | `app/onboarding/application/use_cases.py:54` | `record_pass` commits even when the pass it records is already stored (the same pass sent twice), which costs one redundant write. | read |
| observation | standard-other | `app/onboarding/domain/progress.py:118` | The required-steps rule is computed twice: once as a local in `progress()` and again in `Progress.required_steps_done`. | read |

What I verified:
- **Tests:** `uv run --no-project pytest -n auto tests/onboarding tests/architecture` → `114 passed`. All seven checks passed on `d04e79f`.
- **Invariant 1:** `domain/` imports only the standard library and onboarding modules. `application/` imports only onboarding modules and `app.shared.transaction`. The architecture tests include `app/onboarding`.
- **Invariant 3:** `read_progress` and `read_facts` never call `save` or `commit`.
- **Invariant 4:** `Onboarding.end` sets `ended_at` only while it is null. It is reached only through `_settle`, which runs after `read_facts` in the same call. `test_the_end_never_moves_once_set` passes.
- **Invariant 5:** the only `commit` is in `_settle`, and it runs once, at the end. A write that finds nothing to write commits nothing.
- **Invariant 6:** the resume grid in `test_resume_is_none_exactly_when_over` passes.
- **Invariant 8:** `Facts` and `Progress` carry only a variety's id and name, plus counts and flags.
- **Schema:** the diff changes none, so the §11 naming check does not apply.
- **Brief items:** every item is implemented and tested at the declared seams (`progress()`, and the use cases over fake ports).
- **Error kind:** "kind gone" is assigned by the router's translator at 404, which belongs to `progress-http`.
- **Open findings:** none of the open rows in `## Tech Lead: findings` (all front-end, from other subtasks) is repeated by this backend-only branch.

## Tech Lead: review import-ending

### Verdict

**approved**: Verified that the import ends the Onboarding through OnboardingEnding.imported. It runs after the last writer under the import's NoCommit, only while ended_at is null, never commits, never runs on a dry or refused run, and leaves eligibility untouched. The 4 new tests pass, and invariants 1, 4 and 5 hold.

### Findings

There is no blocking finding.

| severity | category | location | claim | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| observation | spec-missing-test | `tests/spreadsheet_import/test_import_ending.py:56` | No test shows that a failing writer leaves `ended_at` null, or that an Onboarding already over keeps its `ended_at`. Both hold in the code (`import_port.py:28`, `Onboarding.end`, and no commit when a writer raises). The brief does not ask for either test. | read of the code |
| observation | standard-other | `tests/spreadsheet_import/test_import_ending.py:38` | The tests record passes and delete the row directly through `SqlOnboardings`, not through an HTTP route. That is reasonable: no pass route is on this branch, since the subtask depends only on `state`. | read of the code |

**Brief:**
- **Port:** declared at `ports.py:280`.
- **Adapter:** `import_port.py` does nothing when there is no row or `ended_at` is already set. Otherwise it calls `Onboarding.end` and `save`, which flushes only. It never commits.
- **Write path:** `write_import` takes `onboarding` as a keyword-only argument and calls it at `:121`, after the last writer, on the same `NoCommit`.
- **CLI:** `cli.py:119` builds the adapter, and `cli.py:167` commits once.
- **Dry run and blocked plans:** both return at `cli.py:148-151`, before `write_import`.
- **Eligibility:** `has_any_records` still reads only the six owners and never the onboarding table.

**Tests:** `uv run --no-project pytest -n auto tests/spreadsheet_import/test_import_ending.py` gives `4 passed`. The `tests/architecture` run gives 32 passed, `tests/spreadsheet_import` gives 139 passed, and `mypy app tests` is clean.

**Invariants:**
- **1 holds:** the adapter lives in the onboarding hexagon's `adapters/`, writes only `onboarding` and imports no other hexagon. The CLI wires it, as the brief says.
- **4 holds:** `ended_at` is set only while it is null.
- **5 holds:** the ending is written under the import's `NoCommit` and is never committed by the adapter.
- **2, 3, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10, 11 and 12:** the diff confirms they are not touched (backend import path only, no schema change, no route, no front code).

**Open rows:** the open rows in `## Tech Lead: findings` all belong to front-end subtasks. This diff does not repeat any of them and settles none.

### Changes
- `app/onboarding/adapters/schemas.py`: the wire shape. It holds the step slugs, the `{step, as}` pass body (`as` is an alias) and `ProgressOut`, including the not-offered answer.
- `app/onboarding/adapters/router.py`: `GET /onboarding-progress`, `POST /passes`, `POST /skip-rest` and `POST /advance`. The business comes from `CurrentBusinessId`. The fact sources are built from four `app.state` factories. `as_http` maps the three refusals.
- `app/{varieties,stock,pinned_catalog,accounts}/adapters/onboarding_facts.py`: the four read-only fact adapters. Each is built for one business and filters on it. Varieties reuses `SqlVarieties.all()` with `current_price(today)`. Stock gives the distinct variety ids and the entry count. Pinned catalog says whether a token is saved, without reading it. Accounts gives the user count.
- `app/main.py`: wires the four factories on `app.state` and includes the router behind `require_session`. This is the only place the fact adapters are wired.
- `web/src/api/contract.json`: the three `onboarding.*` codes and the `onboarding` front segment.

### Tests
- `tests/onboarding/test_progress_http.py` (seams: `GET /onboarding-progress`, `POST /passes`, `POST /skip-rest`, `POST /advance`):
  - a new business is offered the Onboarding, count 0, resume `variety`;
  - a price effective tomorrow leaves step 1 pending;
  - a saved bot plus a second user with no pass gives count 4 and resume `header_footer`, and passes 3 and 6 end it;
  - a deleted price on the last pass gives `over: false`, then resume `finish` after re-pricing, then `advance` ends it;
  - `advance` with optional steps still pending ends nothing;
  - targets: price target, then stock target after pricing; a partner session sees the same ones;
  - `GET` issues no INSERT, UPDATE or DELETE (an engine event listener);
  - no row gives `offered: false` and 404 `onboarding.not_found` on every write;
  - rejected inputs: required or unknown steps, bad `as` (six parametrized cases, all 422 `step_invalid` with nothing stored), and skip-rest before the required steps (409);
  - skip-rest ends it, a write on an ended Onboarding stores nothing, and another business's state is separate.
- Also added to existing files:
  - `BODIES` in `tests/test_business_isolation.py`;
  - `onboarding_as_http` in `tests/test_refusal_codes.py`;
  - two serving tests in `tests/test_serving.py`: `/onboarding*` front paths get the shell, `/onboarding-progress` never does.
- `tests/test_gated_routes.py` picks the routes up from OpenAPI with no edit.

### Decisions and assumptions
- Slug names, status codes, the not-offered answer, the bot-saved rule and the `today` argument are listed under assumptions.
- The brief says the fact adapters are `(Session, business_id)` factories. I deviated only for varieties, which also takes today.

### Invariants and brief, verified

- **Invariant 9.** Only click handlers write:
  - `OnboardingStep.tsx` `skip()`;
  - `OnboardingNotice.tsx` `finish()`.
  
  Listo opens only through `afterProgress` on `over: true` with `{listo: true}`, and the layout requires that flag for `/onboarding/done`. Tests assert that a mount only GETs, at `Onboarding.test.tsx:141` and `:330` and at `Inicio.test.tsx:507`/`:526`/`:537`.
- **Invariant 10.** The front renders `steps`, `count`, `resume`, `can_skip_rest` and the counts from the answer and computes none of them. The `afterEach` asserts that `localStorage`, `sessionStorage` and the console are all empty. The Standards pass found no storage or console use.
- **Invariants 1–8 and 11.** The diff touches no `app/`, migration or schema file, and the client sends only `{step, as}`. Invariant 11 does not apply to `shell`.
- **Invariant 12.** The step bodies are empty slots (`stepBodies.tsx`), so no validation is restated.
- **Standards.** The Standards pass found no `@ts-*` comments, lint-disables or `any`, and no loose hex or rgb in `app.css`. It ran web-lint and web-typecheck, both clean. Copy uses vos, and `Onboarding` is in the glossary. The diff has no schema change, so §11 does not apply.
- **Brief.** I found every brief item in the code:
  - routes with chrome none;
  - the frame, with Saltar, Saltar el resto behind `can_skip_rest`, the leave guard, Cerrar and Atrás;
  - Listo's lines and actions;

  - accept landings in `session.ts` and `Invitacion.tsx`;
  - the accounts.md line;
  - `inicioNotices` and the notice text in its `finish` state.
  
  The tests at the five declared seams run green.

### Changes
- `web/src/screens/onboarding/VarietyStep.tsx` (new): step 1. With a `price_target` it shows the owner's `PriceForm` only. Otherwise it shows `VarietyNameForm`, then `PriceForm` for the variety just created. After the price it re-reads `GET /onboarding-progress` and goes to the resume step, or to stock when a `stock_target` exists.
- `StockStep.tsx` (new): `StockEntryForm` fixed to `stock_target` and to `opening_stock`, with the "$ 0" line. After saving it shows "Hecho", "Seguir" (advance) and "Agregar otra variedad".
- `CatalogMessageStep.tsx`, `BotStep.tsx`, `PartnerStep.tsx`, `CategoriesStep.tsx` (new): steps 3 to 6 over `CatalogMessageForm`, `IntegrationsForm` (no catalog link), `InvitePartner` and `ExpenseCategoriesEditor`. Each sends its pass only after the owner's save succeeded.
- `useOnboardingWrite.tsx` (new): sends one Onboarding write, hands the answer to the frame, and shows "Sin conexión" or the generic retry on failure. A gone Onboarding goes to Inicio.
- `stepBodies.tsx`: the six slots now hold the step components.
- `afterProgress.ts`, `OnboardingStep.tsx`: an optional `to` lets a body pick where a not-over answer leads.
- `varieties/PriceForm.tsx`: optional `bare` prop that leaves out the form's own title row.
- `e2e/onboarding.spec.ts` (new) and `e2e/helpers/signup.ts` (option `stayInOnboarding`).
- `Onboarding.test.tsx`: the step bodies are emptied for the shell tests and restored after.

### Tests
- `Steps.test.tsx` (Onboarding step screens, fetch faked), 10 tests:
  - Step 1: price-only mode ends with one variety, with no name field and no `POST /varieties`.
  - Step 1: no target, the name is saved, a refused price keeps what was typed, and the retry sends only the price.
  - Step 2: aimed at `stock_target` with `opening_stock` and today's date, nothing sent to the Onboarding before "Seguir", then advance.
  - Step 2: "Agregar otra variedad" goes back to step 1.
  - Step 3: a refused save sends no pass and keeps the typed text; the retry saves first, then passes. "Dejar así" passes with no catalog write.
  - Step 4: a refused Probarlo sends nothing; a good one sends the pass. The publish line shows and there is no catalog link.
  - Step 5: "Seguir" only after a link exists.
  - Step 6: adding a category passes nothing; only "Seguir" passes, and the last pass opens Listo.
  - A failed Onboarding write shows the generic retry, and the same tap sends it again.
  - Every test also checks that nothing reaches the console or browser storage.
- `web/e2e onboarding runs` (phone viewport, 3 tests):
  - Full run from the invitation to Nueva venta from "Listo", with a 5-minute assertion and landing on Inicio with no notice.
  - Leave after step 1, then resume at step 2 from Inicio's notice.
  - The step-3 header reads back in Configuración → Mensaje del catálogo.
  - The first and second runs also check that the browser holds only `tema`.

### Decisions and assumptions
See `assumptions`. The main ones: `PriceForm`'s optional `bare` prop, step 1 routing to stock when the API names a `stock_target`, and the owners' own button labels kept instead of "Guardar".

- The Python checks, the full web suite and `web-e2e` were not run beyond `onboarding.spec.ts` on the secure-origin project. The suite as a whole is left to the delivery gate.
- Start with `VarietyStep.tsx` and `StockStep.tsx`: the `to` routing for a second variety and the `onDirtyChange(false)` call after the stock save (the saved form unmounts without reporting clean) are the two places that go beyond the brief.
- `CatalogMessageStep` passes a `useCallback` setter to the form, as the open `onDirtyChange` findings ask. The other bodies pass the frame's `setDirty` directly.
- I did not touch the open rows about other subtasks.

## Developer: wiring (v2)

### Checks

Run by the system on `0a7e9fe` when the Developer delivered.

| Check | Command | Result |
|---|---|---|
| `tests` | `uv run --no-project pytest -n auto` | passed |
| `lint` | `uv run --no-project ruff check .` | passed |
| `types` | `uv run --no-project mypy app tests` | passed |
| `web-types` | `npm --prefix web run typecheck` | passed |
| `web-lint` | `npm --prefix web run lint` | passed |
| `web-tests` | `npm --prefix web test` | passed |
| `web-e2e` | `npm --prefix web run e2e` | passed |

### Changes
- `web/src/screens/onboarding/stepBodies.tsx`: the six slots now map to `VarietyStep`, `StockStep`, `CatalogMessageStep`, `BotStep`, `PartnerStep` and `CategoriesStep`.
- Each of those six step files is new and sits in `web/src/screens/onboarding/`. Each mounts its owner's form, saves through the owner's route, and only then sends the Onboarding write.
- `web/src/screens/onboarding/useOnboardingWrite.tsx` (new): sends the write, hands the answer to the frame, and shows the single failure line.
- `web/src/screens/onboarding/afterProgress.ts` and `OnboardingStep.tsx`: `onAnswer(answer, to?)` can route to a given place when the answer is not `over`.
- `web/src/screens/varieties/PriceForm.tsx`: new `bare` prop. In bare mode it has no title row and no "Rige desde" field, so step 1's price starts today and cannot be edited. This was the important finding.
- `web/src/screens/onboarding/Onboarding.test.tsx`: the shell tests empty the six step bodies in `beforeEach` and restore them in `afterEach`.
- `web/e2e/onboarding.spec.ts` and `web/e2e/helpers/signup.ts` (new option `stayInOnboarding`, default unchanged): the three end-to-end runs.
- `scripts/design/screens.json`: the seven Onboarding entries now carry their routes, so `compare.sh` builds them.

### Tests
- `web/src/screens/onboarding/Steps.test.tsx` (10 tests). The seam is each step body mounted inside the frame with the owner's forms and mocked routes. They cover:
  - step 1 price-only has no "Rige desde" and sends `{ effective_from: today, list_price }`;
  - the name-then-price path;
  - step 2 is fixed to the stock target and the opening-stock origin, and "Seguir" sends `advance`;
  - "Agregar otra variedad" goes to step 1;
  - "Dejar así" passes only after the save;
  - the bot step shows no catalog link;
  - the partner and categories steps pass only on "Seguir";
  - a refusal from the owner shows the owner's code and sends no Onboarding write;
  - no browser storage is used.
- `web/e2e/onboarding.spec.ts` (3 tests, phone viewport). They cover:
  - the full run from invitation to Nueva venta from "Listo";
  - leaving after step 1 and resuming at step 2 from Inicio's notice;
  - the catalog message read back in Configuración.
- Run in the foreground: `typecheck`, `lint`, and the vitest files under `src/screens/onboarding` and `src/screens/varieties` (9 files, 126 tests) all pass.

### Decisions and assumptions
- `route: null` in `screens.json` made `compare.sh` skip these screens, so I set the routes. This edit sits on the design registry, which the design subtask owns.
- I kept the owners' layouts and button labels (invariant 12), so some of what `compare.sh` shows differs from the designs on purpose. The details are under the Tech Lead heading.
- I added no computer-viewport entries. The designs are phone-only (light and dark), so there is nothing to compare a computer render against.
- The last presubmit run had one `web-e2e` failure: `theme.spec.ts:169` ("with Oscuro stored, Entrar, the invitation screen and the reset screen are dark") saw a `401 (Unauthorized)` console message after sign-up and log-out. I ran that test alone on this head (`npx playwright test e2e/theme.spec.ts --project=secure-origin -g "with Oscuro stored"`) and it passed. I read it as a flake of the 7.9-minute loaded run: the test starts from `signUpNewBusiness`, whose default path my change leaves as it was. I changed nothing for it. If it fails again on a quiet run, it is a real issue to look at.

### For the Tech Lead
I ran `scripts/design/compare.sh --only OnboardingVariedad,OnboardingStock,OnboardingMensaje,OnboardingBot,OnboardingSocio,OnboardingCategorias,OnboardingListo`. All seven were built ("ok") and I read all seven PNGs against `docs/product/design-system/pantallas/`. Only the phone viewport was run, because the registry has no computer entries.

**Matches the design**
- Step heading and copy.
- "Paso N de 6" with the "Opcional" pill.
- Progress segments.
- Footer line ("Si lo salteás, lo hacés después en …").
- Step 3's field order and "Dejar así".
- Step 4's "Hecho" and "Seguir" and the publish line.
- Step 6's list.

**Differences I left, with the reason**
- **Variedad:** the design shows name and price together. The built flow is name first, then price, because the owner's `VarietyNameForm` and `PriceForm` are two forms. Step 1 also shows "Variedad: {name}" between them.
- **Stock:** the owner's `StockEntryForm` sets the card, the date and the button label ("Guardá la entrada", not "Guardá el stock"). The "Si no cargás el costo, el lote cuesta $ 0." line sits under the button. The done view is simpler than the design: "Hecho", "Seguir", "Agregar otra variedad".
- **Mensaje:** the owner's save button reads "Guardá el mensaje", not "Guardar".
- **Bot:** the owner's `IntegrationsForm` puts "Buscar mi chat" inline beside "Tu chat" and shows the hints in its own order.
- **Socio:** the design's first button is "Crear el link", dark and primary. The owner's `InvitePartner` shows "Invitá a un socio", secondary.
- **Categorías:** the owner's editor shows the "Nueva categoría" input always, where the design hides it until "+ Categoría".
- **Frame (all steps):** "Saltar" is a secondary button in the build and a text link beside "Saltar el resto" in the design. The frame (`OnboardingStep`) is another subtask's.
- **Listo:** the comparison shows Inicio, not "Listo". The capture user has not finished Onboarding, so `/onboarding/done` redirects home, as invariant 9 requires: "Listo" is drawn only right after a write answered `over: true`. I verified "Listo" in the e2e run and the shell tests, not in `compare.sh`.

Several of these could be closed by changing the owners' forms. That is outside this subtask and would touch the owners' screens. Tell me if you want any of them.

Other subtasks' rows (phone-only and light-only registry entries, extract-* `compare.sh`, shell Inicio `compare.sh`) are not touched.

## Developer: wiring

### Checks

Run by the system on `455ddce` when the Developer delivered.

| Check | Command | Result |
|---|---|---|
| `tests` | `uv run --no-project pytest -n auto` | passed |
| `lint` | `uv run --no-project ruff check .` | passed |
| `types` | `uv run --no-project mypy app tests` | passed |
| `web-types` | `npm --prefix web run typecheck` | passed |
| `web-lint` | `npm --prefix web run lint` | passed |
| `web-tests` | `npm --prefix web test` | passed |
| `web-e2e` | `npm --prefix web run e2e` | passed |

### Changes
- `web/src/screens/onboarding/stepBodies.tsx`: the six slots point at `VarietyStep`, `StockStep`, `CatalogMessageStep`, `BotStep`, `PartnerStep` and `CategoriesStep`.
- Each step component saves through its owner's form or route first, then sends the Onboarding write through `useOnboardingWrite`, then calls `afterProgress`. The front decides nothing.
- `StockStep.tsx`: the form is locked to `stock_target` and `opening_stock`. The done view follows the design: an explanatory line, a card with the saved variety's name, then "Seguir" and "Agregar otra variedad".
- `BotStep.tsx`: `showCatalogLink={false}`.
- `PriceForm.tsx`: `bare` mode, with no "Rige desde" field (review fix, earlier).
- `web/src/styles/app.css`: `.onboarding__tarjeta*` classes for step 2's card.
- `scripts/design/screens.json`: seven Onboarding entries with routes and six dark twins. The `OnboardingListo` entry (review fix) uses `route: /onboarding/categories` and `path: /onboarding/done`. Its steps click "Saltar el resto" and wait for "Esto quedó armado en tu negocio.", so it really compares Listo and no longer Inicio.
- `web/src/flash/flashReducer.ts` and `FlashProvider.tsx`: a showing toast now survives a REPLACE navigation. A redirect that settles the same screen, such as `VarietiesIndex`'s `<Navigate replace>`, no longer dismisses a toast armed before `navigate(-1)`. This removed a race in the e2e specs (catalog toast failures went from 2/6 to 0/10 locally).

### Tests
- `Steps.test.tsx`: 10 tests at the step seam. Each step saves through its owner and then sends the write, a render sends no write, "Listo" opens only on `over: true`, and refusals show the owner's codes.
- `Onboarding.test.tsx`: `STEP_BODIES` is emptied in `beforeEach`, so the shell tests do not depend on the step bodies.
- `flashReducer.test.ts`: a new test, "shown, then a replace to another key: still shown", which also checks that the next non-replaced change goes idle.
- `web/e2e/onboarding.spec.ts`: 3 tests (lines 27, 66, 79) through the real Onboarding, with the `stayInOnboarding` option in `helpers/signup.ts`.
- compare.sh was run on all seven Onboarding screens, and the judgements are below.
  - Match the design: Listo's structure, copy and order.
  - Differ only because of the sample data: counts and "Para después" states.
  - Departures that come from reusing the owners' forms:
    - Variedad is two-phase.
    - Stock keeps its own layout and "Guardá la entrada".
    - Mensaje uses "Guardá el mensaje".
    - Bot has an inline "Buscar mi chat".
    - Socio has a secondary "Invitá a un socio".
    - Categorías has its input always visible.
    - The frame's "Saltar" is a secondary button.
  - The designs are 390 px phone-only, light and dark.

### Decisions and assumptions
- The `OnboardingListo` entry has no dark twin. It can only be reached once per sample app, because the light entry already ends the Onboarding.
- The toast survives a REPLACE navigation but not any other key change. Fixing this in the flash module was cheaper and safer than changing `VarietiesIndex`.
- The remaining red checks are in files outside this subtask, and I left them alone.

### For the Tech Lead
- The last gate run was red, on files that are not mine. I could not fix them inside this subtask.
  - web-tests: one uncaught `window is not defined` error from `ExpenseCategoriesEditor.tsx:111-114`. `flashGone` sets a 2.5 s timeout with no cleanup on unmount, and it fires after jsdom teardown in `ExpenseCategoriesGroup.test.tsx`. All 1044 tests pass. The fix is a timer cleanup in the editor, which belongs to the extract-* subtask.
  - web-e2e `withdrawals.spec.ts:16` (also seen in `catalog.spec.ts`, 1 in 10 locally): it hangs at `getByLabel('Gramos')`, because `VarietiesIndex`'s replace-redirect can swallow a click right after `goto('/varieties')`. The fix belongs to the shell.
  - web-e2e `theme.spec.ts:169`: a 401 console message. It passes alone.
- Look first at `OnboardingStep.tsx:126` and `useOnboardingWrite.tsx:44`, where "Listo" is gated on `over: true`. Then look at the Listo entry in `screens.json`.

  - The one gap left is Listo, raised above as a narrower finding.
- **Invariants.** The diff is front-only: 3 files. It does not affect invariants 9, 10, 11 or 12.
